Inside one account, identity checks are **tamper-evidence**, not a wall.
- **A. Accept and document.** Write the threat model into the design: the gates stop mistakes, not lies. No work; leaves forged mail looking legitimate in the record.
- **B. Identity from the channel on one host.** Peer credentials give the connecting pid; its ancestry names the tmux pane, so the session. A `caller` that disagrees is refused **and shown** (the card, the Inbox); a caller-less request is the person only from the UI's process or from a process under no agentorc pane, and *unknown* is never the person. Stops the accidental and the casual — an agent talked into `env -u AGENTORC_SESSION` by something it read — and makes a forgery visible. Does not stop `send-keys`. Small, and needs nothing from TD-057.
- **C. A per-session secret.** Weaker than B (a same-user process can read it) and redundant beside it. Not recommended.
- **D. A secret only the person holds** (a passphrase behind person-only acts). Closes forged *mail* from the person, and is walked around by `send-keys` into the pane. Not recommended on its own.
- **E. An OS boundary.** Untrusted sessions in agents-only container nodes, one per trust level, **declared to carry no person** so the home refuses every caller-less request from that link; the person and the high-trust sessions on the home, which then runs nothing untrusted. The only choice that stops a determined rogue — forged mail, `send-keys` and the UI's API alike, since none is reachable from inside the container. Depends on TD-057, plus the no-person rule above.
**The anchor's recommendation:** **B now** — cheap, and it turns a silent forgery into a visible one — and **E as the condition for TD-075's trial**: a cheap, less-trusted model is not put beside the tech lead on the same account. A is what is true until then, and the design should say so either way.

**Paul's direction on alarms, 2026-09-20 — designed the same day (§4.8a *An alarm's answers* and *Who answers first*, §4.5a's row). **(a1) built 2026-09-20 (PR #299, `tdgrind-ao-1`):** the trail says **dismissed by you** where it said *acknowledged by you*, at the home and on a node's routed act alike — the home writes the word for a node's record, keyed by its address, or the ending would read *resolved*; `identity_ack` keeps its wire name, since it is in `NODE_ACTS` and a rename there is a protocol change that buys a person nothing. The control's own label is the page's half. **(a2) built 2026-09-20 (PR #301, `tdgrind-ao-1`):** `suspend(id, why=None)` — a person's only, refused to every session as `identity_ack` is and for the reason turned around (one that could suspend could stop its rival) — marks the record `suspended: {at, by, why}`, the home's field (so a node's record is marked here and only the `kill` is routed — and a person at a *node* has the whole call forwarded here, `modes.HOME_EDITS`, or the node would write a mark the home's next copy wipes), and stops it as a kill does, with no wrap-up. Both roads back are then closed to a session, `create` under that name and `create --resume` of that conversation, which is §4.1's one exception; the name check answers a verdict of its own, `suspended`, so `ao team start` refuses the **whole** start and names the member without knowing the rule itself. **No `unsuspend`**: the things that lift it — a person's own resume, under the old name or another, and Forget — already exist, and each is now refused to a session while the mark stands. The review of PR #301 found two real holes and both are closed: **`remove` was open** (the acting gate lets a controller forget an `unattended` member's record, which freed the name and let the suspect be started again unmarked — invariant 5 shields only a person's own interactive session), and a **kill that fails after the mark is written** left a record marked-but-running in silence; it now says so in words, and keeps the mark, because marked-but-running is the safer half to be left holding. The row's control and its flat mark are the page's half. **Left for the page, one line** (`app.js`): the New session form disables Start only on a `live` verdict and shows a message only on `supersede`, so a `suspended` verdict currently says nothing to a person about to lift one — harmless, since their create succeeds either way, but they should be told what they are doing. **(b) built 2026-09-21 (PR #318, `tdgrind-ao-1`):** `identity_log(id)` — a person's only, gated as `identity_ack` is — hands the alarm to **the record's first live controller**, read from the control graph and never from a badge (`_answers_for`, which the record's new `alarm_to` field also uses, so the page's drawn-or-not and the RPC's refused-or-not cannot disagree). The words are the home's, composed from the alarm's own fields and the record's; the only two things in it a model wrote — the `doing` line and the report line — are quoted and labelled as such. It goes as mail **from the person marked `handed`**, which is the debt extension the design asked for first: `MailEntry.owes` gains the case *from the person, handed, no outcome yet*, `Session.owed()` reads the **inbox** as well as the outbox, and `--outcome --for` accepts an id from either side — so a piece of work the person hands a session now owes an outcome exactly as the session's own answered question does, where before it could be dropped in silence. Then the list is cleared and the trail says *logged by you → `<controller>`*; a record with no live controller is refused in words. Three things the review of #318 found and all three are fixed: on a **node's** record the clearing is **routed** as an `identity_ack` (clearing the home's replica would clear what the node's next report puts straight back — the race that routing exists to avoid), a handed entry that still owes is **refused to `inbox_delete`** (it is the first debt-bearing mail in a session's own inbox, so the first a delete could silently discharge), and `_views` reads **one** graph for the batch rather than one per record, since it runs on nearly every RPC. **The rest is not built.** Build order: (a) the rename — **Dismiss**, the trail's *dismissed by you* — and **Suspend** (`suspend`, the `suspended` mark, the refusals to sessions, `ao team start` refusing the whole start as it does for a live holder), which need nothing unbuilt; (b) **Log TD** (`identity_log`, to the record's first live controller), which needs one extension of TD-079's debt first — an entry *from the person* marked `handed` is a debt on its addressee; today a debt exists only on a session's own question to the person; (c) the chain of command, which waits for TD-075's `techlead` and step 4's `person: false` and never applies on kmaster. (a) touches the host agent and is tdgrind-ao-1's; the row's page side is tdgrind-ao-2's. **The page side of (a) is complete 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-2`): the rename in PR #298, and the suspended mark and the New session verdict in PR #306** — the mark drawn on the card, the Focus header and the Inbox's state row, flat and never pressable, its words carrying the when, the who and the why; no Unsuspend anywhere, because Resume and Forget are the two acts that lift it; and the form's `suspended` verdict printing the agent's own sentence with Start left **enabled**, since a person's create is the lift. The rename, in detail: the control reads **Dismiss** on the alarm row and in the card mark's hover, with its confirm in the words §4.8a gives it; the wire name `identity_ack` is untouched, because a wire name is not a control, so the host agent's half of (a) — the trail's *dismissed by you* — and every test that drives the RPC are unaffected. **Suspend** is drawn from 2026-09-20 (PR #312), on the record's row only and only while there is something to stop — not the host's own list, not an already-suspended record, not an `exited` or `closed` one — and it is the one control on the row that **leaves the row standing**, because it acts on the session and does not answer the alarm. **Log TD** is still **not drawn at all** until `identity_log` exists — **undone, `tdgrind-ao-2`'s, waiting on PR #318** (the host-agent half, open at wrap-up 2026-09-20); the page draws it only where the record view's `alarm_to` is non-null, says *no session answers for this one* where it is null, and handles the refusal when a controller exits between draw and press, as agreed by mail: a control against a method that is not there is what §4.2 forbids, and a disabled one would be a promise the page cannot keep. Both shapes were agreed with `tdgrind-ao-1` by mail before either was built. What he said, and what the design started from: *It seems like identity items should go through the chain of command — techlead if defined, then to me. It also seems like they should have options like "Suspend agent" or "Log TD". "Acknowledge" seems like a dismiss.* He is right on all three, and the anchor had it wrong: **Acknowledge is a Dismiss under another name**, and an alarm is the one row where dismissing is the least useful thing on offer. To design: (1) **the row's answers** — **Suspend** (stop the session now, its worktree and conversation kept, and keep a team start from bringing it straight back), **Log TD** (file the alarm — channel, claim, RPC, count, times, the session's `doing` line and last report — where work is picked up; it shares its write path with TD-069's *Put on the board*), **Open**, and **Dismiss** with the trail TD-079 gives every row; *Acknowledge* is renamed. (2) **The chain of command**: with a `techlead` defined for the team (TD-075), the alarm goes to it first as mail it must answer — dismiss with a reason, suspend, log, or pass up — and reaches the person when it passes it up, when it does not answer within a bound, or when the alarm is about the techlead itself or about no record. **One condition the design must keep:** a session that can clear alarms is a session that can clear evidence, so a techlead handles alarms only where §4.8a's wall stands — on a host that enforces, for sessions in agents-only nodes (TD-075's own condition) — and its every decision on one is told to the person (*answered for you*).

**Done when** the design says what identity rests on, on one host and across hosts, and what it does not defend against; a forged `caller` and a forged person are refused and visible in the cases options 1–2 cover; and the briefs' *instructions come from controllers and people* no longer rests on a field the sender fills in.

**Open PR, handed over 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, wrapped up for the team's restart):** **#318** (step b, **Log TD**, branch `td077-b-log-td`) — the anchor's three findings of 02:35Z are answered in its last commit: each alarm carries the mode it was raised under and the report says that one, never the home's under a node's name; `identity_log` is in `modes.HOME_EDITS`, so a node forwards it and refuses it in words while the home is unreachable; and a node's reachability is checked **before** anything is sent. `tests/test_link.py::test_log_td_says_the_nodes_mode_is_the_homes_act_at_a_node_and_sends_nothing_unreachable` holds all three; the suite and lint are green. `src/sessionorc`, so the anchor merges it. What is left of TD-077 after it is (c), the chain of command, which waits on TD-075. **Merged as #318; the page's half landed 2026-09-20 (PR #335, `grinder-ao-2`)**: the alarm row draws **Log TD** only where the record's view names who answers (`alarm_to`), with a confirm naming that session and the outcome it will owe; elsewhere it reads *no session answers for this one*; a controller gone between the draw and the press is the agent's refusal, as the toast.

**Related:** design §4.4a (*identity comes from the channel*), §4.8 (the gates), §4.10 (the `[controller]` / `[person]` marks; `system`, new in TD-069, which the home alone writes), §9 invariants 5 and 14, TD-075 (the go-between, which raises the stakes), TD-057 (container nodes), TD-064 (peer-message policy).

## TD-078: `test_restart_reloads_and_reconciles` failed once in a full run

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-19 (the anchor session; reported by the worker that built TD-069 step 1)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Partly done — **the first sighting is reproduced and fixed** (PR #264, 2026-09-20): the file in a loop under two concurrent full suites failed once in 25 at the migration assertion (`assert 'exited' == 'idle'`), because the test stored a **paneless** record and ran under a one-second `CREATE_GRACE` with 0.3 s ticks — a tick between `start()` and the assertion judged it `exited`, correctly. The grace is now long while the migration is asserted and zero for the half that is about the grace passing, so each line bounds on its own rule; 40 runs under the same load, no failure. **The second sighting below is open** and is the same family, in a different test — 25 runs of `tests/test_agent.py -k send` in a loop under two concurrent full suites did not reproduce it on kmaster (2026-09-20), so it stands on its CI sighting alone. **A third sighting, 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, a local full run on the TD-151 slice 1 branch, which touches no `src/sessionorc`):** `test_send_wait_three_outcomes` failed at `tests/test_agent.py:435` — the case *busy, and the whole queued turn runs between two polls (idle, working, idle back to back): settled, never a false prompt-stalled* — with `prompt-stalled: … showed no activity within 0.6 s`, which is exactly the false stall the case exists to exclude; alone it passes. So the watch ends with a live one, and a fourth followed in CI the same day (PR #670, 3.13, the same line). **Both are fixed (2026-09-27, grinder-ao-1, PR #672): the test's race, not the agent's.** The test gave each send a fixed 0.2 s before firing the hooks that stand in for the tool; under load the paste itself (`_type`, which confirms the prompt left the composer) took longer, so the hooks landed while the prompt was still being typed — before `rpc_send` took its `rev` baseline — and the wait then watched an idle that never moved. Reproduced by slowing `_type` by 0.4 s: the old test fails with the CI's exact `prompt-stalled … within 0.6 s`, and the new one passes. Each send now waits for its own paste (`_typed`: its `sends` entry written and the pane's typing lock free) instead of a sleep. In the real system the tool's hooks follow the typed prompt, so the agent's order holds. **Seen again 2026-10-01 00:46Z (grinder-ao-2):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` hit the suite's timeout on the 3.13 runner of PR #815 (run 36797544062, a change to the page and the CLI only) and passed on the re-run. **Seen again 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` hung to the suite's timeout on CI's 3.12 job for PR #831, a diff with no `sessionorc` change (run 36807134632, attempt 1); 3.13 passed beside it and the rerun passed. The next run on 3.12 hung in the same test at the same point — PR #835, a doc-only diff (run 36807992641, attempt 1) — so on CI's 3.12 it is no longer rare: two of two runs within the hour. **Next:** the sightings are fixed; the anchor archives it after a clean week.
**Location:** `tests/test_agent_restart.py` (`test_restart_reloads_and_reconciles`)

**Why:** during the fix round of PR #249 a full local run had this one test fail; it passed alone (the file's 6 tests) and on the clean full re-run (538 passed), and the branch touched nothing it exercises. CI has not shown it. A test that can go red on a full run costs a merge sooner or later, and the way to lose an hour to it is to meet it for the first time with a deadline.

**A second one, seen in CI 2026-09-20 on a ledger-only PR (#262, Python 3.13 only; 3.12 passed the same commit):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` — `TmuxError: tmux paste-buffer -p -d -b ao-paste-… -t =ao-test-send-wait-three-outcomes0-w:: no current target`: the send raced the pane (the target session was not there, or not yet, when the paste ran). The job was re-run. Same family as the first: a wait that assumes a pane is up. Both belong to whoever takes this entry.

**What the second one actually was, 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, PR #286) — the error is what a send to a record that has *exited* looks like.** It is reproduced on demand, in one line: kill a session, wait for `exited`, `send` to it, and the suite prints that exact string, buffer name and all. `send` and `keys` refused a **closed** record and not an exited one, so the paste went to tmux and tmux answered about a target it could not resolve — a tmux error for a question about a record, naming neither the session that had gone nor when. Under a loaded 3.13 runner the record reaching `exited` before the send is the only extra ingredient the CI sighting needed (the first sighting in this entry is the same tick judging a record `exited`, from the other side). **Fixed:** the host agent refuses it in words, with the exit code (`_refuse_gone`, design §4.5a *Focus composer*), and `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_to_an_exited_record_is_refused_in_words` holds it. That does not *prove* the CI run went that way — the runner's log is gone — so the test itself is unchanged and the sighting is not called fixed: what changed is that a repeat now names its cause instead of naming tmux. **If it recurs, the message will say which session exited and with what code.** And if it **hangs** rather than fails — which is what `tdgrind-ao-2` saw on 2026-09-20, 14 minutes of nothing on a branch that touches none of this code — the suite's per-test bound now stops the run with every thread's stack (TD-063, PR #314) instead of leaving a job-level cancel whose last line is the test *before* the one that stopped. Re-check on 2026-09-27; if a week of CI and local full runs is clean, close the entry.

**A third one, and the same shape — 2026-09-20, seen by `tdgrind-ao-2` in a full local run and reported to `tdgrind-ao-1`; fixed in the same PR.** `tests/test_identity.py::test_enforce_against_real_panes` died in the JSON decoder and passed on its own immediately after. The cause is in the test, and it is the family's own: **`_probe` waited for the answer file to *exist*, and the probe's `open(out, "w")` creates it empty.** Every run has that window; load widens it, and the read then parses nothing. The probe now writes `out.part` and `os.replace`s it onto `out` — a rename within one directory is atomic, so the path appears only when the answer is whole and the waiter's signal is the right one again. Nothing in `src/` is involved. It is recorded here rather than in a new entry because the entry *is* this family: a wait bounded on something other than the thing it is waiting for.

**Done when** the failure is reproduced (run the file in a loop under load — the full suite beside it) and its wait or ordering assumption is fixed, or a week of CI and local full runs passes without it and the entry is closed as not reproduced.

**A fourth sighting, a new shape — 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, CI on PR #383, Python 3.13 only; 3.12 passed the same commit):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` at line 375, `assert (await asyncio.wait_for(task, 5))["state"] == "idle"` — `AgentError: prompt-stalled: ao-test-send-wait-three-outcomes0-w showed no activity within 0.6 s`. Not the exited-record paste of #286: the send landed and the pane was judged idle-without-activity inside the test's 0.6 s prompt-stall window, so that window is too short for a loaded 3.13 runner. Not investigated; the job was re-run (run 35666996420). If it recurs, bound the wait on the pane's own signal as TD-088 did, rather than widening 0.6 s.

**A fifth sighting — 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, CI on PR #859, Python 3.12 only; 3.13 passed the same commit, `c4346b2`, a tests-only change to another file):** `tests/test_agent.py::test_send_wait_three_outcomes` ended in the suite's per-test *Timeout*, not an assertion — the hang shape, on the same test. Run 36822297238; the failed job was run again.

**Related:** `tests/README.md` (the fixtures' timing rules), TD-069 (where it was seen).

## TD-080: A manager's round log reads as unpushed work forever

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-20 (the anchor session; Paul: *we have another 308 unpushed message — that session told me it was caught up; let's check on it*)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **part (2), the measure, is built 2026-09-20 (PR #270, a worker):** `GitInfo.unpushed` and `GitInfo.pushed_against`, computed once by the host agent in `gitinfo.py` by §4.2's three rules — the branch's own `origin/<branch>` where it has one, the upstream where it does not, and *on no remote-tracking branch at all* where there is neither — and read by the card's flag, Ready to close (which now says what it measured against when it fails) and `ao team stop --close`, whose own third git test is gone: three tests became one. `ahead` is still on the record beside it and still means *ahead of the upstream*, which is *unmerged*. Part (1), the round log leaving git, goes with the manager's brief in TD-076 and is **not built** — until it is, the manager's worktree reads 0 unpushed by rule 1 (its log is pushed to `origin/<branch>` at wrap-up) rather than 308 by the old measure, so the false row is gone even while the log stays. **Next:** part (1) is in the manager's brief, the anchor's.
**Location:** `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.md` (round step 4: *append one line per round to `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.log` … commit it to your launch branch*), `src/agentorc/briefs/lead.md`, `src/sessionorc/gitinfo.py` (`ahead`, read against the branch's upstream), `src/agentorc/teamrun.py` (`_unsafe_to_close`: a third test of *pushed*, folded into the one measure by the design of 2026-09-20), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (`ready_to_close`, `state_kind` → the *unpushed* row)

**Why:** the session was right — nothing is stranded. Branch `orchestrator-ao-1` is *ahead 308, behind 173* of **`origin/main`, which is its upstream**, and its whole difference from main is two files: `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.log` (582 lines) and `orchestrator-ao-1.relayed.json`. The 308 commits are the manager's own round log, one commit per round since run 1, exactly as its brief tells it; they are pushed to `origin/orchestrator-ao-1` at wrap-up (the remote sits at run 5's wrap-up; run 6's rounds are local until this run ends). Because the branch tracks `origin/main`, where those commits can never land, every record in that worktree — the live manager and the exited `…-push` session alike — reads *308 unpushed*, and the Inbox raises *exited with unpushed work* for it, counted, for ever. A row that is always there teaches a person to ignore the row.

**Recommended:** (1) **stop committing a round log to a branch.** It predates what the record now holds — `ao doing` (the round, with its age), the progress and findings channels, the run log — and a 582-line file in git is the least readable of them; the brief writes its one line per round to a file under the home (`runs/`), or nowhere. (2) **Measure *unpushed* against the branch's own remote** (`origin/<branch>`) when it has one, and against the upstream only when it does not — *ahead of main* is *unmerged*, which is a different question from *exists only on this machine*, and the row is about the second. (3) Until then the row for this worktree is noise, and Paul may dismiss it once TD-079's Dismiss exists for state rows.

**Done when** a manager that has run for a week raises no *unpushed* row unless commits exist only on this machine, and the briefs no longer tell it to commit a log.

**Related:** TD-069 (the *unpushed* row), TD-079 (a row that cannot be answered is a row to fix), design §4.2 (Ready to close), TD-076 (the manager's brief is rewritten there anyway).

## TD-083: A worker that ends its run on purpose parks its team

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-20 (the anchor session; found by `orchestrator-ao-1` at the end of ao-grind's first run and written up in its summary and on the board, PRs #274 and #276)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** **Step 2 (the chip) built 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-2`, PR #323):** the **restart wanted** mark on the card and the Focus header, shaped like *out of work* and following its rules — fixed words, the `why` on hover because it is a sentence a card cannot hold, never pressable, not a state, and one malformed record costs that card its chip and not the grid. **It draws `early` differently**, which is the one thing the design did not have to say and the field does: a controller does not act on an early one, so a chip that looked the same would promise a restart that is not coming; it reads *restart wanted · early*, in the needs-you tokens, and its hover says why it is for a person. Partly done — **designed 2026-09-20**, **step 1 built 2026-09-21 (PR #317, `tdgrind-ao-1`)**: `ao progress restart --why` sets `restart_wanted: {at, why, early?}` on the session's own record — declared only by the session it is about, refused without a reason, refused while an outcome is owed to the person (the fresh run does not carry the conversation the debt was made in, so nobody is left who can report it), and refusing `none` as `none` refuses it; a later declared claim clears it, since the session went on after all. `RESTART_EARLY` is thirty minutes: a restart asked inside that of the record's own start still stands — the word is the session's — but carries `early: true`, and a controller puts an early one on the board rather than acting on it. `ao status -v` prints it, and it is in the wake digest, so a lead's `ao wait` returns on it: that is what makes it a thing a manager acts on rather than a field nobody reads. Step (2), the chip, is `tdgrind-ao-2`'s and was blocked on this. **Step (3) built 2026-09-21 (the anchor), after the promote that made the verb live**: `docs/briefs/tdgrind-ao-1.md` (and so `tdgrind-ao-2.md`, which follows it), `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.md` and the presets `grinder.md`, `hunter.md` and `lead.md` — a worker ends a run with one of the three words and only then the summary; a lead restarts a member that wants it from the record's fields (`early` → the board; suspended → left alone; dirty or unpushed → one send, then the board; else `ao close` and the crash rule's `ao new`, one ceiling), counts it as not finished, and the exemption for *a written end-of-run summary* is gone — the twenty-minute send names the three words. **They take effect at the next team start**; the running `ao-grind` sessions were started on the old briefs. **One gap the briefs showed, left open here:** a hunter whose area is *covered* (quiet, not gone) stops with no word — §4.9a gives it none, `none` would be false and `restart` is not it — so under *declared, never inferred* its lead will ask it; whether *covered* needs a word of its own is a design question, not decided. The design takes the first option whole and half of the second: a third declaration, `ao progress restart --why` → `restart_wanted: {at, why}`, acted on by the controller inside the one restart ceiling; and *a summary is not a declaration* in both briefs. Making a submitted `/exit` an exit was **not** chosen — it has the adapter read what a session typed. Build: (1) the RPC, the field, its refusals (no reason; an outcome owed; `none` already declared), the early-declaration guard (`RESTART_EARLY`, recorded as the sub-field `restart_wanted.early`), `ao status -v`, and `wait` waking the lead on it — tdgrind-ao-1; (2) the chip — tdgrind-ao-2; (3) the two briefs and the three preset briefs, which take effect at the next team start. Not the same gap as TD-053's, which is a worker that *declared* itself out of work. **Next:** all three steps built; the *done when* is a live run.
**Location:** `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.md` (the round's state rules), `docs/briefs/tdgrind-ao-1.md` (*Shutdown*), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`_out_of_work`, the `progress` channel), `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/` (what a submitted `/exit` is), design §4.8 and §4.9a

**Why:** 2026-09-20, 18:36 UTC: `tdgrind-ao-1` ended its run deliberately after ten merged PRs — its context was long, the ledger still held pickable entries, and it said a restart with fresh context would pick up cleanly. It could not say so in any way its manager can act on. It was not **out of work**, so `ao progress none` would have been a lie and it rightly did not send it. A Claude Code `/exit` does not leave the session (TD-053 records that), so it was never **`exited`**, and the manager's restart rule fires only on `exited`. It was `idle` with a written end-of-run summary, which the brief says is *not* idle-with-open-work. Closing it is reserved for wrap-up. So the manager put a steer to Paul, the steer lapsed at its bound to its default — park — and the team did nothing for ninety minutes with work on the ledger, until a person acted: the anchor session, on Paul's word, ran `ao team stop ao-grind --close` (about 19:57 UTC; the worker settled and was closed), then closed the manager by hand — its own submitted `/exit` had not left either, so its name was still held — and ran `ao team start ao-grind` at about 20:10 UTC. (That sequence is the anchor session's own record of 2026-09-20; the board PRs #274 and #276 record the parking and the wrap-up, not the restart.)

Two things are missing, and the design round chooses between them or takes both:
1. **A worker cannot say *restart me*.** A third declaration beside `done` and `none` — the run is over, the lane is not — that a manager may act on by closing the record and starting it again under the same name and brief, inside the restart ceiling (three in two hours). It is a structured field, never text the manager parses.
2. **A submitted `/exit` is not an exit.** Either the adapter makes it one (the session is closed when the tool has plainly been told to leave and has settled), or a summary without a declaration does not count as an ending and the manager's idle rule applies to it.

**Done when** a worker that ends its run with work left is back at work, with fresh context, without a person — and one that is truly finished still is never restarted.

**Related:** TD-053 (out of work, §4.9a — the declared case), TD-081 (a same-name start keeps the record's mail: a restart here must too), TD-026 (schedules), TD-076 (the manager's brief is rewritten by the rename).

## TD-091: Nothing says how much context a session has left, or that it has just compacted

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, from the neighbours survey, TD-059)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — recommended by the survey, not approved; design first (§4.5a: a card and Focus field) **2026-09-21, Paul: should grinders (and hunters) compact after each TD?** The anchor's answer, pending research: a session cannot run `/compact` itself, so it is a manager's act (`ao send`), not brief guidance; between TDs a grinder's state is all in the ledger, so a fresh run (`ao progress restart`, TD-083) beats a compaction there, while a hunter's state is what it has explored, so compaction suits it better; per-TD is too often for either; and **the missing piece is this entry's signal** — with the context's size on the record, the rule is a manager's: *at a task boundary, past a threshold, restart a grinder or compact a hunter*. A Sonnet research pass on when compaction pays (cache economics, auto-compact's trigger, what survives, idle time against the cache's lifetime) was started the same day; its findings, and the configuration they suggest, land in this entry. **The research, 2026-09-21 (Sonnet; its sources: Anthropic's prompt-caching, pricing and compaction pages, the Claude Code hooks page, Anthropic's context-engineering post, and four open issues on `anthropics/claude-code`; the arithmetic is the researcher's own model from the published multipliers — about 1,500 tokens added and 500 written per turn — not a measurement):** (1) **only a user or the harness can run `/compact`** — a session cannot (open feature requests ask for it) — and a typed `/compact <instructions>` takes instructions for that one run; (2) a compaction is **an ordinary model call over the whole context**, and what decides its price is whether the cache is warm: a cached read is a tenth of base input, a five-minute cache write 1.25×, a one-hour one 2×, and the cache lives five minutes unless the longer one is asked for; (3) **on a warm cache, compacting beats both carrying on and a fresh start on tokens** — the break-even is about five further turns at 40k of context, two at 100k, fewer beyond — and **a fresh start is never the cheaper one on tokens while the cache is warm** (its re-read of brief and docs is an uncached write, and it lands at a larger context than a summary does): *the anchor's first answer above had this backwards — a restart's case is quality and a clean head, not price*; (4) **idle time turns it round**: past the cache's lifetime the whole context is paid for again at full price on the next turn, so compacting a *cold* 100k context costs about twice a fresh start — **compact before a long wait (CI, a reviewer, the anchor's merge queue), not after it**; (5) quality: Anthropic names *context rot* and advises compacting at a clean boundary, warning that an aggressive summary loses what only later turns out to matter — so a **hunter**, whose state is what it has explored, is compacted at a pause and never restarted mid-investigation, and a **grinder**, whose state is in the ledger, may be either; (6) `PreCompact` and `SessionStart` with `source: compact` are the hooks around it — the second is how state is re-injected after one, and is already read here (TD-090) — and reports disagree on whether `PreCompact` fires for an automatic compaction; the automatic trigger's threshold is set by environment (`CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` and others), its default not documented. **What it suggests as configuration** (per profile, since windows and prices differ): a context threshold past which a session is compacted or restarted *at a task boundary*, and an idle length past which it is compacted *before* the wait; the ordering — boundary, then expected idle, then the raw threshold — is policy, not configuration; the act is its manager's (`ao send "/compact"`, or the restart rule). **What is not observed yet**: this entry's context size and `compacted_at`, and any notion of *about to wait* — the host agent knows a session is idle, not that it is about to be. On a subscription the unit is the usage window rather than dollars; the ratios hold. **Design review 2026-09-22:** endorsed — it is the one thing a person reading a quiet worker cannot see; ledgered for evaluation, not scheduled. **Next:** endorsed for evaluation, design first, not scheduled.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/` (the transcript under `~/.claude/projects/`, which the adapter already reads for the model), the record, the card and Focus header; design §4.2, §4.3 (an optional adapter capability), §4.5a

**Why:** a session near its context limit is about to compact, and one that has just compacted has lost most of what it knew — the two moments a lead reading a quiet worker, or a person deciding whether to trust a long-running session's next step, most wants to know about. agentorc shows a model and each profile's usage windows and nothing about context. `tallu-wonder/agentboss` reads it from the transcript: the context in use is the last non-sidechain usage record's input + cache-creation + cache-read + output tokens, and a `type: "system", subtype: "compact_boundary"` record's `compactMetadata.postTokens` resets it until the next turn ([ADR 2026-09-20](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md)). The window size is the weak part: Claude Code's transcript does not state it, so agentboss guesses by model name.

**Fix:** an optional adapter capability, `context()` → `{used, window?, compacted_at?}`, read on the tick by modification time as the model is; the record carries it; the card and Focus show a small gauge (or `compacted 4m ago`), §4.5a row first. A tool that cannot say it shows nothing.

**Done when** a Claude Code session's card shows its context in use, and a compaction is visible on it within a tick.

**Related:** TD-090 (a compaction's `SessionStart`), TD-087 (the usage windows, the other budget), design §4.3.

## TD-092: Nothing reaches a person who is not looking at the page when a session needs them

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, from the neighbours survey, TD-059)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** decision
**Status:** Open — recommended by the survey, not approved; the channel is Paul's choice **Design review 2026-09-22 (the anchor, Fable):** endorsed as the cheapest, highest-value item on the ledger — an ntfy topic for what the Inbox counts under *Needs you*; ledgered for evaluation, not scheduled. **Next:** the channel is Paul's choice.
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — the channel (Fix)
**Location:** `src/agentorc/ui/` (the Inbox, the top bar's count), design §4.5, §4.5a

**Why:** the Inbox is where a person works from, and a `needs-you` session or an `ask` sits there until somebody opens the page. Three of the neighbours read in TD-059 push instead: agentboss sends a desktop notification on a transition into *needs you* that jumps to the session; `multi-agent-shogun` and another dashboard send to ntfy / Pushover, and the latter only when the person is not looking at the dashboard. An unattended team whose only question is waiting on a closed browser tab is a team that waits ([ADR 2026-09-20](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md)).

**Fix:** decide the channel — web push from the UI (a service worker; no second service, works on a phone), or an ntfy topic in the home's config — and the rule: only for what the Inbox counts under *Needs you*, only on a transition into it, never twice for one row, and not while the person has the page focused. §4.5a gains the opt-in control first.

**Done when** a session going `needs-you` while no page is open reaches the person once, and opening it lands on that row.

**Related:** TD-069 (the Inbox), TD-003 (the phone layout), design §4.5.

## TD-093: Who must look at a PR before it merges is a sentence in a brief and a message in an inbox

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-21 (the anchor session; asked for by Paul the same day)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Open — **slice 3 built 2026-09-23 (the anchor, PR #479):** `prs_waiting(members)` in `ui/app.py` sums each record's `prs_waiting: {n, oldest}` into the team header's *n PRs waiting · oldest <age>* (`group_head.html`, display only), and an Inbox `ask` carrying `pr` draws `#<n>` as a link to the PR (`review.pr_url`, from the sender's checkout's `origin`; bare when that cannot be said). **The `org.yml` line is set, 2026-09-23 22:00 MDT** (`review: {reader: techlead, held: ["src/sessionorc/**", "docs/briefs/**"]}` on the grinder role; `ao roles` reads it back), so the anchor's hand rule has ended: the next `ao team start ao-grind` carries it onto the grinders' records. Left: the live look of the header count and the link on the next held PR (Paul's browser walk). Was: **slice 4, the briefs, written 2026-09-23 (the anchor; PR #512):** the techlead preset gains *A held PR* (the gate first, the diff against the design, the reply on the thread with `--source`, the merge itself for an unattended asker and a recommendation for an interactive one), the grinder preset's merge step goes through `ao pr held <n>` (held → `ao msg --kind ask --pr <n> {techlead}`, next item, findings on the thread, the person past the bound), and this repo's three briefs and the techlead primer say the hand is the seat's: `review: {reader: techlead, held: [src/sessionorc/**, docs/briefs/**]}` on the grinder role in `org.yml`, which the anchor sets once this PR is merged and promoted (the record reads it at the next `ao team start`). **The anchor's hand rule ends with that `org.yml` line** — until it is set, `ao pr held` says *not held* and the old briefs' rule no longer exists, so the two land together. Left: slice 3 (#479). Was: **slice 1 built 2026-09-23 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #477; `src/sessionorc`, the anchor merges):** `review: {reader, held, bound}` on a role preset (`ROLE_KEYS`, checked by `models.normalize_review` in the loader and the host agent alike), carried onto the record, home-owned, by `ao new --role` and `ao team start`; `pr` on a mail entry, `ao msg --pr <n>`, refused on any kind but `ask`; and `prs_waiting: {n, oldest}` on the view. **Left**, with slice (2)'s `ao pr held <n>` built the same day (PR #478): (3) the team header's *n PRs waiting* and the Inbox's `#<n>` link; (4) the briefs. Was: **designed 2026-09-21 (the anchor; design §4.9b *The reader*, §5's example, one §4.5a row), and the shape decided by Paul the same evening: the setting is the role's** (`review: {reader, held, bound}` on a role preset, carried onto the record at start — some teams need it and some do not), **`held:` defaults to every PR**, approval is required for an unattended session's PR and a recommendation for an interactive one's (*much of the time, if the reader recommends a PR two other agents have reviewed, I will accept it*). Also asked: *seat* was not in the glossary — added. The build is unscheduled — (a) a role preset's `review: {reader, held, bound}`, `held` by path globs defaulting to `**`; (b) the techlead is the reader, woken by an `ask` that carries a new integer field `pr` (`ao msg --pr <n>`), it merges a worker's PR itself and only *recommends* on a person's session's PR; (c) the queue is the seat's inbox — `asks_waiting`, the *Waiting on them* row with a link and age, a *PRs waiting* header count; (d) the author picks its next item, rebases its own open held PRs, the reader merges oldest first; (e) agentorc records and shows, GitHub enforces only where the reader has its own account (`CODEOWNERS` mirroring `held:`), the cadence script is untouched. Build order when it comes: the `pr` field and `--pr`, and `review` as a record field set from the preset at start (`src/sessionorc`, the anchor merges), the check of a PR's files against the record's `held:` in the author's own `ao` (a helper, `ao pr held <n>`, for a brief to call), the two rows, the briefs (grinder, hunter, techlead, manager presets; this repo's own). Was: **the rule is decided and in force by hand; the configuration is not designed.** Paul, 2026-09-21: *we might want a configuration to be able to require "techlead merges", so that Fable gets a look at PRs before they merged. We can use that cadence here in the meantime.* **In the meantime, in this repo:** every PR that touches `src/sessionorc` — and the repo's own briefs and `org.yml` — is read and merged by the anchor (the high-trust model), after the author's own independent review; a worker's PR of that kind stops at green CI plus its `cadence-review:` comment. Everything else a worker merges itself on a passing `scripts/check_cadence.py`. **A requirement added by Paul, 2026-09-21: a person's own interactive session inside a team, with the same reader.** *As a user I would like to be able to run an interactive session from within the team, where the techlead would still review merges — a safety net for when I am not intimately familiar with an architecture but want to make changes.* So the rule keys on **the repo and its paths, never on who wrote the PR or what mode their session is in**: a PR from the person's session gets the reader a grinder's does. What exists already: `ao new --team <team>` gives a person's session the team badge, and with it the mail edge to the techlead (§4.10 *sideways*), and an interactive session stays out of every policy's reach (§9 invariant 5). What the design has to add: the techlead **woken by a PR** as it is by a question (a seat filled on demand has no other way to hear of one), and — for the person's own PR — its review as **a recommendation the person may overrule**, never a gate on them. **2026-09-22:** the hand rule is now in all three of this repo's briefs (PR #406 — the manager watches members' PRs on the three paths and asks Paul after two hours; grinder-ao-1's brief had no such line until now, which is how #402 merged without the anchor's read). The build (the role's `review:` block) is still unscheduled. **Next:** slice 3 is PR #479, the anchor's.
**Location:** design §4.9b (the techlead), §4.8 (role presets, grants), `.agentorc.yml` / the team definition, the briefs; slice 2 built 2026-09-23 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #478): `ao pr held <n>` in `src/agentorc/review.py` and `cli.py` (§4.7), which reads the record's `review` and says whether the PR waits for its reader, so (3) the header and the link and (4) the briefs are what is left; `scripts/check_cadence.py` is a SYNCED file (dev-cadence) and is not edited here

**Why:** the rule earns its keep — of the PRs held for the anchor's read on 2026-09-20/21, #301 (a node served `suspend` itself) and #318 (three findings, one of them a false sentence about a host's identity mode) had each already passed the author's cheaper-model review, and #307's review, which came twelve minutes after the anchor's read, did not find what that read had (a restart still polled at once). And the way the rule is carried fails in both directions. It lives in mail and in a brief's prose, so a worker that did not read its inbox between claim and merge merged a `src/sessionorc` PR itself (#343, the doorbell — whose test is TD-094); and it has **no answer to the reviewer being away**: the five PRs of TD-075's mail half sat green for eight hours overnight, the grinder ran out of other work, and the whole team wound down correctly with its work blocked on one reader (the manager said so on the board, twice). A rule that matters this much should be something a team is configured with, shown on the page, and honest about its queue.

**What the design round has to settle:** (a) **where it is said** — a repo's `.agentorc.yml` (it is the repo's policy: *paths* → *who merges*) or the team definition (it is the team's seat that does it), and by path, by label, or for every PR; (b) **who the reader is** — §4.9b's techlead is started per batch and holds no grants, which fits a reader woken by a PR as it is woken by a question, but merging is an act on the repo, not on a session, so agentorc can *record and show* it and cannot enforce it: GitHub's branch protection and CODEOWNERS are where enforcement lives, and the design should say which half is ours; (c) **the queue is a first-class thing** — a PR waiting for its reader is a row somewhere a person sees (the Inbox's *Waiting on them*, the team header), with its age, and **a bound**: past it the PR goes to the person rather than idling a team; (d) **the author is not idle meanwhile** — what a worker may pick while its PR waits, and how stacked PRs are handled (the five above conflicted with each other in `models.py`, the tests and one ledger line, and the reader resolved them after the authors had gone); (e) the cadence check's `review` row is self-attested by the author — a second, reader's line is a change to dev-cadence, not to this repo.

**Done when** the design says where the rule lives and what agentorc does and does not enforce, a team can be configured with it, the waiting PRs and their age are on the page, and a PR past its bound reaches the person.

**Related:** TD-075 (the techlead — the natural reader), TD-083 (the ending that made the overnight stall a clean wind-down rather than a parked team), docs/cadence.md §4 (the independent review), the memory note *read-inbox-before-merging*.

## TD-095: The Org's cards say the same thing several times, are uneven, and *working* does not stand apart

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-21 (the anchor session; asked for by Paul from a screenshot of the live Org page, kept as `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-09-21-org-cards.png`)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — live look pending (the *done when*'s last clause; on the board). **(f) and (i) built 2026-09-22 (PR #414, grinder-ao-2)**: `view()` draws *unseen* only on a record that is not `unattended`, and the team header sits on the body's 13 px with its title at a card name's 16 px. **(g) and (h) built 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-2, the PR after #414)**: the techlead preset's label is *Tech Lead* (the other built-ins take their name title-cased already); Claude Code's weekly window is labelled `week`, the chip reads *grind · week 89%*, and a profile with no reading *grind: no reading yet* — the page and `app.js` together, design §4.5a's usage row with them. **Polish, from Paul's look at the live page 2026-09-21 evening (a free-pick with the page):** (g) role labels are titles — *Tech Lead* — so the presets' `label:` (`repoconfig.py`, *Tech lead* today) and the mockups' are title-cased; an auditor's *Auditor · docs* is the label plus the definition's name, and the name stays as written; (h) the top bar's usage chip *wk 89%* is short for no reason and *stale grind no reading* beside it is cryptic: it is the account's weekly window (the label is `Window(label="wk")` in the Claude Code adapter, drawn as `<profile> wk 89%`), so *claude · week 89%*, and *grind: no reading yet* for the dimmed *no reading* chip; (i) the team header's fonts are smaller than the cards' from when it carried less — `.ghead .h2` at 14 px against a card's `.sc .name` at 16 px, and the header container at 12 px in the page's sans while the cards sit on the 13 px body (the `.meta` class is one and the same, 11.5 px mono, on both) — one scale, the header's title no smaller than a card's name. **(f) decided by Paul 2026-09-21: *unseen* is drawn only on an interactive session** (§4.2; a finished worker is plain `idle` — its manager read the result, and the slot says *out of work*); the page still marks unattended sessions, a free-pick with the TD-097 card. **Designed 2026-09-21 with Paul** (design §4.5 *The card's anatomy* and the §4.5a rows it changes; the sketch he chose from is `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-09-21-card-proposal.png`): six fixed rows at one height; a name once; the branch on a row of its own, tool · account · model on every card; one clock; an ending said once per place; the mode as a plain word with its toggle in *more*; the team's header without its manager's card in it; *working* green, *idle* blue, everything ended one grey. **His decisions**: the manager's card stays a card; blue for idle; the slot is two lines for now; a team's profiles are commonly mixed, so the profile line stays on the card; *unattended* is a word, not a chip. **Build order**: (1) the mockups (`docs/mockups/gen.py`), the legend (*States & badges*) with them — the anchor or grinder-ao-2; (2) the page — grinder-ao-2 — and what three review rounds made explicit for it: **the slot's order** (what needs a person or explains a stop → an ending, *out of work* and *restart wanted* among them → the `doing` line → the tail) **and its caption** (*ready to close ✓* lives there); **the buttons move from the slot to the foot**, the first by state — Allow / Deny, Switch profile… / Wait, **Forget for `exited` even when it reads ready to close**, Close session only for an `idle` session that passes, Details, else Focus; *under `<controller>`* ends row 3 and the `stops` note joins row 2; the team's header **loses its manager's name, state and line** (Paul's TD-074 decision of 2026-09-19, reversed by him); the stylesheet's shared rule for idle / exited / unreachable **is split**, `--done` leaves the card, *finished · unseen* stays idle's blue; the *more* entry for the mode; and the pill is the state and nothing else — a session that declared out of work is still `idle`; (3) Paul looks at the live page. One thing left for a later look, noted during the Suspend test: what the suspended mark's `why` should say. As first written: a design session is owed, the anchor's, with Paul: §4.5 and §4.5a first (a card's fields and their order are the design's), then the mockups (`docs/mockups/gen.py`), then the page (grinder-ao-2's lane). Not a free-pick until the design lands. **A second pass, asked for by Paul 2026-09-21 after the design merged (#375) — (a), (b) and (c) are written into the design (§4.5 *The card's anatomy* and its order rule, §4.5a's *mine* and editor-button rows, §5 *The person's own* — `~/.agentorc/ui.yml`, `open_in:`), so the builder builds once; (d) is open:** (a) ***interactive* is the mode that stands out**, since those are the person's own sessions and finding them is a task: *unattended* stays a plain word, *interactive* takes the `person` icon and full-strength text — a mark, never pressable; the order stays urgency first, and **within one urgency an interactive session sorts ahead**; the Org filter gains a one-press *mine*. (b) **The foot's buttons are too loud, and the quiet ones read as disabled**: the next act becomes an outlined button with normal text, the others plain links at normal strength, *dimmed* means disabled and nothing else, and the one filled button on a card is **Allow** on a *needs you* card. (c) **The *VS Code* button is the person's to configure**: a label and a URL template in **a person-scoped UI configuration that §5 does not have yet** — it knows hosts, repos and the org, and nothing that is one person's preference, so the second pass adds that scope and says where it lives — as asked, presets for VS Code, Cursor, Zed and JetBrains Gateway; **as designed, two presets — `vscode` and `cursor` — and a custom template for anything else** (Zed's and Gateway's remote links are not the same shape), or `none`, which removes the button; `vscode_host` in `hosts.yml` stays what fills the template's host. (d) **decided by Paul 2026-09-21: a session that declared itself out of work keeps idle's blue pill, for now** — the design as merged: the pill is the state. The sketch (`docs/mockups/reviews/2026-09-21-card-proposal.png`) was brought up to the second pass the same day; **the build started 2026-09-21** with the team's restart: the page is grinder-ao-2's, the mockups and the legend grinder-ao-1's. **The page, as built (grinder-ao-2, one PR per slice, each leaving the page whole):** 1/6 the state tokens — working green, idle blue, one `--ended` grey, `closed` its own class, `--new` for unread — PR #381. 2/6 the six rows at one height, the slot's one text and caption, the next act first in the foot, the mode's toggle in *more*, what the group says hidden inside it — PR #384 (its `gitinfo.py` half, *detached at `<sha>`*, is PR #385, the anchor's to merge). Not built anywhere: *Switch profile… / Wait* have no route, so a `limited` card's next act is Focus. (e) *idle · unseen* for *finished · unseen* — the pill's words, design §4.2 / §4.5 / §4.5a — and the Focus Reports panel shows a reference once, as the card's report line does: PR #386. 3/6 the quiet foot — the next act outlined, the rest plain links, Allow the only filled button, and *more ▾ → Close* disabled until Ready to close passes (§4.5 says so since 2026-09-04; it was always enabled, and Close reaps the worktree), with what it waits on as its hover — PR #388. 4/6 the team's header — the team, the `host / repo` its sessions share (*mixed* where not), the counts by state, its marks and controls, and no longer its manager's name, state or line; *No team* headed by its count — PR #389. 5/6 `~/.agentorc/ui.yml` and `open_in:` — `vscode` (the default), `none`, or a template of the person's own, a refused value named on the Org page with the default drawn; **no `cursor` preset**: Cursor's own docs do not document a folder link, so §5's condition was not met (decided with the techlead; §5 records the check and the template a Cursor user writes); *edit yml* waits for the Commands page — PR #390. 6/6 *mine* beside the Org filter (remembered per browser, composes with the box), the sort key (rank, interactive first, name) on the server and in the page's re-sort, and `person` reserved as a role's icon — PR #391. **The page is built; what is left is (3), Paul's look at the live page** — which needs the anchor's promote first. On the board as *merged, live check pending* (2026-09-21). **(e) accepted by Paul 2026-09-21: the pill *finished · unseen* becomes *idle · unseen*** — the words on the pill and in the legend, nothing else: it is still `idle` in every payload, still sorts above plain idle, and Focus still clears it; design §4.2, §4.5 and §4.5a's state-icon row take the new words with the page's PR. Paul asked what it has to do with `exited`, and whether it means *waiting to be closed*. It means neither: `exited` is a process that is gone; *finished · unseen* is a session alive at its prompt whose last turn nobody has opened Focus on since (§4.2 — it is `idle`), and whether it can be closed is *ready to close ✓*, a different fact. The word collides with the **finished** member of the managers' briefs and of design §4.9a — one that declared itself out of work (the glossary's own term for the declaration is *out of work*) — so the pill says *finished* of a turn where the rest of the product says it of a run. *idle · unseen* says the state and the mark, and nothing else. **Next:** built; what is left is the live look.
**Location:** design §4.5 (the card), §4.5a (its rows), `docs/mockups/gen.py`, `src/agentorc/ui/templates/card.html`, `group_head.html`, the state tokens in the stylesheet, `src/sessionorc/gitinfo.py` (it reads the commit, so row 3 can say *detached at `<sha>`* — the page reads *detached HEAD* until that is merged and promoted)

**Why:** Paul, 2026-09-21: *manager-ao-1 is listed multiple times on the same card (there are other redundancies as well). "Working" should probably be green to be more contrasted with the finished/exit states, and we should probably make the card sizes congruent.* What the screenshot shows, as the agenda:

- **One name, four times.** A card's heading is the tool's title and then the session's name beside it — the same word twice when the title is the name (every team member today: *manager-ao-1 manager-ao-1*); the worktree line says it twice more (*wt/manager-ao-1 → manager-ao-1*); and the team's header names the manager again.
- **The header and the manager's card repeat each other**: the manager's name, its role, its state pill and its whole report line are in the team header and again, in full, on the card directly beneath it.
- **Badges that say what the group already says**: every card inside the *ao-grind* group wears an `ao-grind` badge; every member says *under manager-ao-1* beneath a header that names the manager; and *kmaster / agentorc* and *claude-code · paul · opus-5* are identical on every card of the team — candidates for the header, shown on a card only where they differ.
- **An ending said three ways**: an *exited* pill, then *exited · ready to close ✓*; and *finished · unseen*, an *out of work* chip and *ready to close ✓* on one card. One of them is the state, one is the person's next act, and the design should say which is drawn where.
- **A report that reads as noise**: *#359 → #359 · 1/2 do…* — a reference shown as becoming itself, and truncated.
- **Uneven cards**: heights differ by what each happens to carry, and the state pill sits under the badges on some cards and at the right of the heading on others (the *No team* group against the team group). A fixed anatomy — the same rows in the same places, empty ones collapsed consistently — and one height per row of the grid.
- ***Working* does not stand apart**: it is a blue pill, close in weight to the grey *exited* and *finished* ones. Paul's suggestion is green. §4.5's state colours are tokens and mean something (the grid sorts by them), so the change is to the token table, with the contrast checked in both themes and against *needs-you* and *stalled?*, which must stay the loudest.
- **A group header that misleads**: the *No team* group is headed *samscrape*, and holds sessions of three different directories; whatever that word is drawn from, it is not the group's project.

**Constraints that stand** (none of this session's to change): state and alarm marks are never pressable; colours are tokens; nothing on a card is a control built from what a session wrote; a control not in §4.5a does not exist.

**The mockups, 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, build step 1):** `docs/mockups/gen.py` draws the Org card to §4.5 *The card's anatomy* and its second pass — six fixed rows at one height, the tool's title only where it differs, the mode a word (*interactive* with the `person` mark, *unattended* quiet), the one clock on row 2, `wt/` only when the worktree is not the name and `host / repo` only outside a team's group, the report with a reference once, the slot as one text with *ready to close ✓* its caption, and the quiet foot led by the next act (Allow the one filled button). The team header lost its manager and gained its place and counts by state; **Wind down** replaces the header's *Stop*; the *Urgent first / Pinned* toggle, retired 2026-09-18, is gone from the artboard. The legend (*States & badges*) carries the state tokens under grinder-ao-2's names for the page: `--working` green, `--idle` blue, `--ended` one grey (text `#4b5563`), `--new` for unread; a closed pill is `s-closed`, and green `s-done` is left to Focus. A finished worker (`tdgrind-4`, *idle · unseen* — renamed from *finished · unseen* by Paul, #382 — out of work) is added to the sample team. Not redrawn: the phone artboard's card (TD-003 is Paul's) and the Focus header, which only take the new colours.

**The report line, 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, handed over by grinder-ao-2):** `report_line()` in `src/sessionorc/models.py` — the one formatter for the card and `ao status -v` — drops ` → #N` when the entry's reference is that PR, so it reads `#359 · 1/2 done`; a different PR is still named (`#360 → #361`). `src/sessionorc`, so the anchor merges it.

**Promoted 2026-09-21 18:29 local (the anchor), right after #383 (18:18) and #385 (18:28) merged — the page Paul looks at is this one.** The anchor's own headless look at 1400 px, three cards across: the six rows hold and the foot is quiet, but row 2 and row 4 truncate on a card that narrow — *unattend…*, *paul ·…*, *TD-431~ → #936 …* — so the mode word and the account are the first things cut. Not fixed; for Paul's look and the polish pass after it.

**Done when** §4.5 and §4.5a say what a card carries, once each, and where; the mockups show it; the page matches them; and Paul has looked at the live page and said so.

**Related:** TD-071 (the Org page review of 2026-09-18 and its look-and-feel pass), TD-074 (the card's title and `doing` line), TD-076 step 3 (role labels), TD-091 (a context gauge would be one more thing on the card).

## TD-096: Focus hands a person the keyboard of an unattended session, and typing into one lands in the middle of its work

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-21 (the anchor session; from Paul's question the same day)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — live check pending (2026-09-21, grinder-ao-2): the read-only attach (`pump(read_only=True)` drops key frames and passes resize, scroll and wheel-only frames; the first frame says so), the Focus header's **Take over** / **Hand back** / *Switch to unattended* toggle with the composer closed and Paste inert while watching, a mode change in the feed re-attaching, Hand back clearing a stop time that passed, and the manager preset's line that an `interactive` member is left alone. **Left:** Paul's use of it (the *Done when*), and this repo's `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` saying the same as the preset — the anchor's brief. **`rpc_set_mode` now pushes its change at once**, as `set_stop` does (2026-09-23, `grinder-ao-1`, PR #492; `src/sessionorc`, so the anchor merges it): another tab re-attaches at the press, not on the next tick (review of PR #403; test `test_a_mode_change_is_pushed_at_the_press`). Before: **designed 2026-09-21 (the anchor; design §4.5 screen 2 *Focus watches*, the §4.5a rows *read-only terminal / Take over* and *Hand back*, the composer's Send row, the mode toggle's Focus-header names, §4.6 *A read-only attach*); the build is a free-pick for a worker with the page (`src/agentorc/ui/`: the attach, `pump`, the Focus header and composer) — no `src/sessionorc` change is needed, since Take over is the existing `set_mode` and invariant 5 already refuses a controller's acts on an interactive target.** What the round settled: read-only for **every** unattended session, whatever its state (an idle worker's next prompt may arrive from its controller at any moment, and the interleave is the same harm); Take over changes only the mode — a stop time, a sent wrap-up, `out_of_work` and `restart_wanted` stay written and are not acted on while the person holds it, and Hand back returns them to the policies and the manager as they are; a permission is answered from a read-only Focus (the hook, not the keyboard); on a phone it is the same page; the interactive side is untouched. The Focus header's toggle takes the name of what it does — **Take over** / **Hand back** (or *Switch to unattended* where there is nobody to hand back to); the *more* entries keep their *Switch to…* names. The read-only rule is the server's (the attach drops key frames), not the widget's. **Still to check in the build:** the manager's presets (`src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md`) and this repo's `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` do not yet say in words that an `interactive` member is left alone and is not a crash or a stall — the code refuses the acts, the brief should say why the refusals are expected (the repo's own brief is the anchor's). Proposed by the anchor and accepted by Paul 2026-09-21 (*takeover / hand back: yes*). Paul asked whether, since interacting with an unattended session disrupts its job, the manager should help it recover or **Focus** should be called what it does. The anchor's proposal: neither — **looking is not the disruption, typing is**, and the design already has the structured form of *I am taking this over*: flipping a session to `interactive` (§4.5a, the mode toggle), which puts it out of its controllers' reach on their next call (§9 invariant 5). So: **Focus watches** — on an `unattended` session the terminal opens **read-only**; one control, **Take over**, gives the keyboard and flips the session to `interactive` in the same act, so its manager stops nudging it and nothing else types beside the person; **Hand back** flips it to `unattended`, and its manager's next round picks it up as any member. A quick question without taking over stays **Message…** — mail, which types nothing. A manager never infers that a session was disturbed: it reads the mode, a field. **Next:** what is left is Paul's use of it and the anchor's `manager-ao-1.md` line.
**Location:** design §4.5 screen 2 (Focus: *keyboard passes through*), §4.5a (the Focus header's rows, the mode toggle), §4.6 (the terminal bridge — read-only is the bridge not forwarding input), `src/agentorc/ui/` (Focus), the manager's briefs (a member that turned `interactive` is left alone — check they say so)

**Why:** today Focus passes the keyboard through for every session, so a person who opens a working grinder to look, and types a question, has their words land in the middle of whatever it was composing — and nothing tells its manager, which may nudge or restart it while the person is mid-sentence. The techlead trial made the question concrete (a seat that answers a batch and ends is the worst session to type into), but it holds for every unattended session.

**What the design round has to settle:** whether read-only is the default for every `unattended` session or only while it is `working`; what **Take over** does to a pending wrap-up, a stop time and a `restart_wanted`; whether a permission prompt can still be answered from a read-only Focus (it is answered through the hook, not the keyboard — so yes, but say so); how it reads on a phone; and the interactive session's side — nothing changes there.

**Done when** the design says it, Focus on an unattended session cannot type by accident, taking over and handing back are one press each and are fields a manager reads, and Paul has used it.

**Related:** TD-095 (the mode on a card, and *interactive* standing out), TD-075 (the techlead seat), §9 invariant 5.

## TD-097: An empty seat's card says *exited*, which reads as a failure

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-21 (the anchor session; from Paul's question the same evening)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — live look pending. **Built 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-2):** `teamrun.seat_ids` names the seat records by the definition; `view()` takes them as `seats` and composes *◇ on call* over `exited` / `closed` (the state unchanged in every payload), the slot *on call — comes on the next question*, the caption *last came · `<age>`*, the foot's first button **Message…** (never Forget or Close session), and the header's *n on call*; every page that draws a pill asks the same `seats_of`, so the Org, Focus, the Inbox and the events deltas agree. The mockups (`gen.py`: Main, MainDark, Phone) carry a `techlead-1` seat. **Remaining:** Paul's look on the live page after the next promote (the *Done when* below); a trigger seat's slot words (*runs after 10 PRs*) wait on TD-098, and the report is the record's report line — no *n answered* count exists to draw. Was: **design first, the anchor's; not built.** Paul: *since the techlead is meant to exit, should we change that state to "Available" or similar — I am wondering if a user would get confused by it saying exited.* The anchor's recommendation: keep the **state** (`exited` is a fact about the tmux session, and TD-095 just made the pill the state and nothing else) and change **what a seat's card draws**, keyed on the structured fact that the team definition names the session as its seat (as `teamrun.wound_down` already keys on it, by name): an ended seat's pill reads **empty** — the word §4.9b and the glossary already use (a seat is *empty* or *filled*) — in the same grey; the slot says *fills on the next question*; the foot's first button is **Message…**, since asking it is how it fills, never Forget. *Available* is not recommended: it suggests something sitting ready, and nothing is until a question lands. **Decided by Paul 2026-09-21: *on call*** — *empty* read at a glance as something to fill, *available* and *ready* as misleading (*ready to close* is six inches away); *on call* is a plain workplace word that says nobody is there and one comes when called. **Designed the same evening (the anchor): §4.5 *The card's anatomy* (the composed pill, the slot from the trigger, the caption, Message… first), the §4.5a state-icon and team-groups rows, the glossary (*seat*, *on call*).** The build is a free-pick for a worker with the page: the card, the mockups (`docs/mockups/gen.py`, the sketch is `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-09-21-card-proposal.png`), the header count; keyed on `teamrun.seat_names`. **Next:** built; what is left is Paul's look after the next promote.
**Location:** design §4.5 *The card's anatomy* (the slot's endings, the foot's first button), §4.5a (the state pill, the foot), §4.9b (the seat), `src/agentorc/ui/templates/card.html`, `docs/mockups/gen.py`

**Why:** the seat's normal resting state — started per batch, answered, ended — is drawn with the same grey `exited` pill and *exited · ready to close ✓* slot as a crashed worker, so the one card on the page that is behaving exactly as designed looks like the one that failed.

**Done when** the design says what an ended seat's card carries, the mockups show it, the page matches, and Paul has looked at it.

**Related:** TD-075 (the seat), TD-095 (the pill is the state), TD-098 (seats with a trigger share the word).

## TD-098: Seats with a trigger — a doc audit or a test audit that fills after n PRs or every so often

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-21 (the anchor session; Paul's idea the same evening)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **step (1) built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`):** `seats:` in `org.py` (`SeatDef`, the trigger checked at load), `ao team start` starting each seat under the manager with no grants, `teamrun.seat_names` / `seat_ids` over every seat, the card's *on call — runs after 10 PRs* / *runs every 6h* and *last ran* from the trigger, and `ao team list --json` carrying `seats: [{name, role, trigger, after}]`. **Step (3)'s preset built the same day:** `auditor` in `repoconfig.PRESETS` (no grants, no lane, icon `eye`, label *Auditor*) and its built-in brief `src/agentorc/briefs/auditor.md` — hunter-shaped, area *the PRs its trigger counts (the last n, or those inside its period, from `ao team list --json`) against the docs they touch* unless a repo's own brief narrows it, declares nothing. **Left: (2) the fill rule — built on the tick by TD-103 slice (3), what is left of it is the brief's sentence, cut in TD-103 slice (5) — this repo's area briefs `docs/briefs/docs-audit.md` / `test-audit.md` and a `seats:` line in `org.yml` (the anchor's paths), (4) one real run.** Was: **designed 2026-09-21 (the anchor; design §4.9b *Seats with a trigger*, the glossary's *auditor*), on Paul's word the same evening (*I like the cards in concept, and the auditor with a trigger that is configurable*); not built.** As designed: `seats:` on a team definition, each `{name, role, trigger, brief, profile, home}`; `trigger` is `asks`, `prs: <n>` or `every: <duration>`; the manager fills a seat whose trigger is met under the team's one ceiling; a seat holds no grants, declares nothing and is not counted in a wind-down; `auditor` is a hunter-shaped preset; the count toward a `prs:` trigger is not drawn. Build order: (1) `seats:` in `org.py` and `teamrun.seat_names` over every seat, `ao team start` starting them (`src/agentorc`); (2) the manager preset's rule and this repo's `manager-ao-1.md` (the anchor's); (3) the `auditor` preset and two briefs, docs and tests; (4) one audit run for real. Was: **not designed; a design round of its own, after TD-097's word is settled.** Paul: *I am envisioning a role like "doc audit" or "test audit" that would make sure docs or tests are in good shape — they would have a similar function of only checking every so often (configurable?) like after every n PRs, and would be "Waiting" or similar when they are waiting to be awoken.* The anchor's reading: **the techlead seat with a different trigger.** Today's seat has one trigger — a question landed (`asks_waiting` leaves zero, §4.9b) — and the manager fills it. The generalisation: a team definition's `seats:` (of which `techlead:` becomes the first), each `{name, role, trigger}`, with `trigger` one of `asks` (today's), `prs: <n>` (n PRs merged to the repo since the seat's last fill — the manager already reads `gh pr list` for the queue, so the count is one call, and *last fill* is the seat's record's `created`), or `every: <duration>`. The manager's seat rule (§4.9b, its ceiling) fills a seat whose trigger is met; between runs it is **empty**, and the card's slot says why it will fill — *empty · fills after 10 PRs (3 so far)*, *fills at 06:00* — so the word is TD-097's and a person learns one thing. The audit roles are presets like any other: hunter-shaped (find and file with evidence, never fix) or grinder-shaped (fix and open a PR), the role decides; the area is the brief's. **Not *waiting*:** the Inbox already uses that word for a question the person owes an outcome on, and a seat is not waiting on anyone. Open for the round: whether a seat's role may hold grants (the techlead holds none by design); whether `every:` is the home's clock or the manager's (the home times nothing today, §4.9b *When it cannot answer*); how a trigger reads on the team's header; and whether the audit's findings go to the ledger, the board or the techlead. **Next:** what is left is the anchor's area briefs and `org.yml` seat, then one real run.
**Location:** design §4.9b (the seat, the manager's seat rule), §4.8 (role presets), §4.9 (the team definition), the manager's brief

**Why:** a check that runs on every PR is too much and one that never runs is what happens now; a seat that fills on a count or a clock is the shape in between, and the team already knows how to fill one.

**Done when** the design says what a seat with a trigger is, a team can define one, the manager fills it on its trigger and the card says why it will, and one audit role has run for real.

**Related:** TD-075 (the seat and its fill rule), TD-097 (the word for an empty seat), TD-091 (a periodic act on a count is the same shape as compacting on a threshold).

## TD-099: A team with nothing working still offers Wind down, and a manager that stops for the usage window goes idle instead of declaring

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-21 (the anchor session; from Paul's screenshot the same evening, `ao-grind` after its usage stop)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — live check pending. **Step (1) built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #448):** `teamrun.start` closes a concluded team's live sessions before its creates (`_close_concluded`): the held names refuse as before unless the team is `concluded` and every holder is one of its sessions; each of them, a seat included, passes the wrap-up close's `_unsafe_to_close` first, so one with uncommitted or unpushed work refuses the whole start by name with nothing closed; the result carries `closed`, which `ao team start` prints and the page's toast names. Done in `agentorc` rather than as a *concluded* verdict from `_name_verdict`: the host agent's `live` verdict with the holder id is enough to match against `teamrun.concluded`, so `src/sessionorc` is untouched. **Left:** the live look — a concluded team's Start on the Org page closing its idle cards and starting the team. Was: **step (2), the page, built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-2`)**: `teamrun.concluded` (the test, taking either declaration, the state part of it, seats idle or gone) and a `concluded: {at, restart, names}` on `teamrun.rows` beside `wound_down`, so `ao team list` says *n live, concluded* from the same rows; `team_groups` draws such a team with `stopped` — folded, sorted with the stopped teams, *concluded <t> ago · restart wanted* / *· out of work*, **Start** alone, its confirm naming the sessions it closes first. **Until step (1) merges that Start is refused** by the host agent naming the live holders, as any Start over live sessions is today — the toast says so, and nothing is created. Left: (1) (`src/sessionorc`, the anchor reads it) and (3) (the briefs, the anchor). Before: **designed 2026-09-22 (the anchor, the design pass Paul chose while the ao-grind team ran; Sonnet design review)**: §4.5a *team groups* and *Start / Wind down / Stop now*, §4.5's team-groups paragraph, §4.9a *A person's Start on a concluded team*.
