Decided: **(a)** a team is **concluded** — the team's own word, since *finished* is a member's and *settled* is already the verb for reaching idle or exited (`teamrun.SETTLED`) and excludes `restart_wanted` — while still live when every live session carrying its badge is `idle` and has declared (`out_of_work` or `restart_wanted`), its seats idle or gone, the rest exited or closed — *live* is not *running*, since `/exit` does not leave, and the state is part of the test because a declared session that takes a turn keeps the word; **(b)** its header reads *concluded <t> ago*, *· restart wanted* when any declaration is `restart` and *· out of work* otherwise, folds and sorts like a stopped team; **(c)** its one control is **Start**, which closes the concluded sessions first under the wrap-up's own safety check and then runs the start — `ao team start` gains the same; a live session that is not concluded, or holds uncommitted or unpushed work, is still refused by name; **(d)** **Wind down** stays while any live session is undeclared, a paused team included (§6, TD-100), and **Stop now** leaves with it; **(e)** the manager's half: until TD-100's gate is built, a manager stopping for the usage window declares `restart --why "usage window, resets <time>"` after its members settle — written into `manager-ao-1.md` and the preset `manager.md` with the (30, 10) edit (the anchor), and dropped once the gate pauses sessions itself. Not taken: heading the group by the manager's `doing` line, which TD-095 moved to the manager's card the same day this entry was written. Build in three: **(1)** `teamrun.start` closing declared live sessions before the create, with `_name_verdict` reporting *concluded* beside *live* — `src/sessionorc/agent.py`, the anchor reads it; **(2)** the page — `teamrun.rows` (a *concluded* instant beside `wound_down`), `team_groups`, `group_head.html`; **(3)** the briefs (the anchor). Earlier: **not designed; two halves.** Paul: *if a team is fully idle, does hitting Wind down wake them up? Is wind down really a close at that point, or should we collapse the cards automatically?* What the screenshot showed: `manager-ao-1` *idle · unseen* with the slot *stopped for the usage window — both grinders closed clean*, three `exited` cards beside it, and the header still offering **Wind down** / **Stop now**. Wind down would wake the manager (a send starts a turn on an idle session, §4.3), which would find nothing to wind down and close itself — harmless and pointless. **(1) The manager's half, the briefs (the preset `manager.md` and this repo's `manager-ao-1.md`, the anchor's):** a stop for the usage window is not a state to sit in — TD-083 says a run ends with a declaration, and this one is `ao progress restart --why "usage window, resets <time>"`, an early restart with its reason; a manager that has declared is what makes the team read *wound down · restart wanted* and collapse, as the samscrape group does. **(2) The page's half, design §4.5a *team groups* and *Start / Wind down / Stop now*:** a team with no member `working` and a manager that is `idle` is drawn collapsed like a wound-down one, headed by the manager's `doing` line and its state, with **Start** where a declaration says restart and **Close** (the manager's Close session) where it says none; **Wind down** is drawn only while a member is working. Design first, then the brief (the anchor) and the header (a worker with the page). **2026-09-22:** the weekly figure is now written in this repo's briefs — *near its limit* is **95% or more**, temporary on Paul's word (the team had stopped at 89% with a day of the window left); (1) still stands, a stop is not yet a declaration. **(e) written into the briefs 2026-09-22 (PR #439, the anchor):** `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and the preset `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` say a self-stop for usage declares `ao progress restart --why "usage window, resets <time>"` once the members are idle; the 95% figure is gone with the gate's arrival (TD-100 slice 4). **Next:** built; what is left is the live look.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md`, `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`, design §4.5a (*team groups*, *Start / Wind down / Stop now*), `src/agentorc/ui/templates/group_head.html`

**Why:** the header's controls are decided by *live* (any session not ended), and an idle manager is live, so a team that has in fact stopped looks like one that can be wound down; and the manager's own silence — a stop with no declaration — is what left it looking that way.

**Done when** the briefs say a usage stop is a restart declaration, the design says when a team's group collapses and which control it carries, the page matches, and a team stopped for the window reads as one on the live page.

**Related:** TD-083 (a run ends with a declaration), TD-095 (the header), TD-091 (the usage gate and the compaction rule are the same kind of stop).

## TD-100: The wind-down percentage is a sentence in a brief; Paul wants a knob set by the week's interactive load, later from a settings page

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; Paul the same morning)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **(4) designed 2026-09-25** in a cloud session with Paul, after an audit of every setting (ADR `docs/decisions/2026-09-25-settings-audit.md`, four Sonnet rounds to READY): §4.5 screen 8 *Settings*, §4.5a's Settings rows, §5's one file (`settings.yml` home-owned, four keys, `ui.yml` retired), §4.4a *Settings, replicated*, §6 *Team stop time* and the team's reserve priority, §4.7 `ao team until`, `ao team reserve`, `ao settings`. Paul's decisions: the reserves first (*I will probably change those once or twice weekly*), one file, the three per-team settings, the promote's `auto` per repo, read-only values with an *i* mark, the terminal's face and size as a setting. The build: TD-146 (the file and the policies), TD-147 (the replica), TD-148 (the page). **Slice (1), the host agent, built 2026-09-22 (PR #434, `grinder-ao-1`; it touches `src/sessionorc`, so the anchor merges it):** `sessionorc.settings`, `set_settings` and the `gate` read, and the gate on the tick — the `gated` mark, the pause send retried until it lands, the resume after `RESUME_MIN`, the doorbell hold, and a controller's send refused. Two choices the design left open: a session with no `pause_prompt` is marked and nothing is typed, and `set_settings` accepts any label before the profile has a reading, saying `unchecked`. **Slice (2), the clients, built the same day (PR #437, stacked on #434: the anchor merges #434 first):** `ao gate`, and `pause_prompt` / `resume_prompt` from `agentorc.teams` on every unattended create — team members, the techlead seat, `ao new --unattended`, the New session form. `ao mode … unattended` and the mode toggle send the pair too, through `set_mode` (#434 takes them, as `set_stop` takes the wrap-up). **Slice (3)'s mark built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-2`)**: the *paused · usage* mark on the card's slot and the Focus header, read from the record's `gated` (slice 1) and drawing nothing until a record carries it. **The chip's line built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #449):** the page reads the `gate` RPC beside `usage` and attaches each profile's rows as `lines` (`with_lines`) — on the render, and on each pushed `usage` event, which the page's event relay enriches with a `gate` read of its own, so nothing in `src/sessionorc` changed; `usage_chip` / `AO.usageChip` print *grind · week 61% / 70%*, rank the worst window by its gap to the line (the tool's 100% without one), and carry `near` (within ten points of a line, 80% without) which the +n collapse now keys on; the hover gives the reserve, the days a per-day reserve counts and when the line moves. **The seam below closed the same day (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #450, `src/sessionorc` — the anchor merges it):** `gated` carries the window's `resets`, and the mark says *resets `<when>`* when `next` is that reset (a flat reserve's always is), *line moves* otherwise. **Left:** a live look, and the settings page, unscheduled. Was: **Left of (3): the chip's line** — it needs each profile's reserves and lines on the page's render *and* on the pushed `usage` event, which slice 1 serves only through the `gate` RPC; build it once slice 1 has merged, with the line in the event or a poll of `gate`. With it, one seam the review of the mark found: design §4.5a's row says *line moves `<when>`* **or** *resets `<when>`*, and `gated` carries only `next`, which cannot tell the two apart (a flat reserve's line moves only at the reset), so the mark always says *line moves*; the `gate` reading's `reserve` can, and the mark should take the word from there or `gated` gain it. Open — **designed 2026-09-22 (the anchor; four Sonnet design-review rounds, READY on the fourth): §6 *Usage gate* (a line per window from a reserve per profile — flat, or per day of the days left; a pause send and a resume send, never a kill; the `gated` mark with `sent_at`; a session on a dialog is not typed at), §5 `settings.yml` and the `set_settings` RPC (a person's own), §4.7 `ao gate`, §4.5a the chip's line and the *paused · usage* mark, §4.4a ownership and the offline row, §4.10 the doorbell's pause clause, glossary *reserve* and *line*.** Build in four slices: **(1) the host agent** — `sessionorc.settings`, `set_settings`, the gate on the tick (lines, pause and resume sends with retry, `gated`, the doorbell hold, a controller's `send` refused at the home) — `src/sessionorc`, the anchor reads it; **(2) the clients** — `ao gate`, `pause_prompt` / `resume_prompt` set at create beside `wrapup_prompt` (`agentorc.teams`); **(3) the page** — the chip's line and hover, the mark in the slot and the Focus header; **(4) the briefs** — once the gate is live, the 95% / (30, 10) sentence leaves `manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md`, the gate being the stopper. (4) of the settings page stays unscheduled. Earlier: **not designed.** Paul, 2026-09-22, after the briefs got their 95% (PR #410): *"We will drop the wind down percentage back down after this week's reset. I like to have the ability to change it based on the amount of interactive work I will be doing, to maximize token usage for the subscription without hindering my interactive work. Eventually we will want a settings page where we can change this manually in the shiftlead ui."* Today the figure is prose in `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md`, read by the workers at team start and judged by them — the only lever there is, since the §6 usage gate is not built. Four decisions before a build: **(1) where the number lives** — design §6 and §5 put `usage_gate: {pct, per_window}` in the repo's `.agentorc.yml` `unattended:` block, per profile; a figure a person moves week by week does not belong in a file under git, so the likely home is a host-level setting under `~/.agentorc` that an RPC can write (the page's settings later), keyed by profile, with §6's block as the default; **(2) what happens at the threshold** — §6 says *pause … resume when usage drops*, which fits the 5-hour window and not the weekly one, where nothing drops until the reset: for the weekly window the act is the team's wind-down with the manager declaring `restart --why "usage window, resets <time>"` (TD-099 (1)), so the team reads *wound down · restart wanted*; **(3) whether the team starts again at the reset by itself** — TD-026's schedule, or Paul's word as now; **(4) the settings page** — a new screen, so §4.5 gets it and §4.5a its controls (a control not in that table does not exist), with the gate's figure per profile the first setting on it; the usage chip on the Org page could carry the figure as a mark (*wk 91% · stops at 95%*) before any page exists. Until (1)–(2) are built the briefs' sentence is the rule, and the figure is changed by editing them and restarting the team. **Paul, 2026-09-22, on (3) and (4):** a start at the reset is *a nice idea* but **configurable and off by default** (*less surprising to a user*), one more setting on the page beside the wind-down figures for the session and the weekly window; *decent, not vital* — written down here and in TD-026, not scheduled. The page's first settings are therefore three: the 5-hour figure, the weekly figure, and *start the team at the weekly reset* (off). **Paul, later the same morning — the shape of the figures:** what he actually keeps back is *about 30% of each session and 10% of the weekly in reserve for each day's interactive work*, so the settings are **a reserve per session** (the 5-hour window's threshold is steady: 100 − 30 = 70%) and **a reserve per day** for the weekly window, and the gate computes the weekly threshold from the days left before the reset: `100 − reserve_per_day × days_left`, today counted whole — 10% a day gives 30% on the reset day itself, 60% with four days left, 90% on the last day; the reset time is the adapter's (§4.3). A consequence: the weekly threshold **rises** as the week goes, so §6's *pause … resume* is right for it after all — a team paused at 60% on a Wednesday resumes on Thursday when the line moves to 70% — and the wind-down-with-declaration is what happens when the window is out for the *last* day. The board item's *figure to write back after the reset* is now the pair (30, 10), which the briefs can say until the gate is built. **Paul agreed (30, 10) the same day**, and asked whether today's 95% would have been (30, 5): yes for the last day, but a reserve of 5 a day moves the whole week's line (65% on the reset day, not 30%), so a one-day change is an **override with an expiry** — *reserve 5 today*, gone at the reset, the standing pair untouched — which is TD-026's override shape — **and not this entry's** (Paul, 2026-09-22: *I do not think we need a mechanism built for temporary percentage settings immediately, that seems brittle and probably not the current priority; the user can handle it manually for now*): a one-day change is done by hand, and the mechanism is TD-101, if ever. Before: Open — **designed 2026-09-22 (the anchor; four Sonnet design-review rounds, READY on the fourth): §6 *Usage gate* (a line per window from a reserve per profile — flat, or per day of the days left; a pause send and a resume send, never a kill; the `gated` mark with `sent_at`; a session on a dialog is not typed at), §5 `settings.yml` and the `set_settings` RPC (a person's own), §4.7 `ao gate`, §4.5a the chip's line and the *paused · usage* mark, §4.4a ownership and the offline row, §4.10 the doorbell's pause clause, glossary *reserve* and *line*.** Build in four slices: **(1) the host agent** — `sessionorc.settings`, `set_settings`, the gate on the tick (lines, pause and resume sends with retry, `gated`, the doorbell hold, a controller's `send` refused at the home) — `src/sessionorc`, the anchor reads it; **(2) the clients** — `ao gate`, `pause_prompt` / `resume_prompt` set at create beside `wrapup_prompt` (`agentorc.teams`); **(3) the page** — the chip's line and hover, the mark in the slot and the Focus header; **(4) the briefs** — once the gate is live, the 95% / (30, 10) sentence leaves `manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md`, the gate being the stopper. (4) of the settings page stays unscheduled. Earlier: **not designed.** Paul, 2026-09-22, after the briefs got their 95% (PR #410): *"We will drop the wind down percentage back down after this week's reset. I like to have the ability to change it based on the amount of interactive work I will be doing, to maximize token usage for the subscription without hindering my interactive work. Eventually we will want a settings page where we can change this manually in the shiftlead ui."* Today the figure is prose in `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md`, read by the workers at team start and judged by them — the only lever there is, since the §6 usage gate is not built. Four decisions before a build: **(1) where the number lives** — design §6 and §5 put `usage_gate: {pct, per_window}` in the repo's `.agentorc.yml` `unattended:` block, per profile; a figure a person moves week by week does not belong in a file under git, so the likely home is a host-level setting under `~/.agentorc` that an RPC can write (the page's settings later), keyed by profile, with §6's block as the default; **(2) what happens at the threshold** — §6 says *pause … resume when usage drops*, which fits the 5-hour window and not the weekly one, where nothing drops until the reset: for the weekly window the act is the team's wind-down with the manager declaring `restart --why "usage window, resets <time>"` (TD-099 (1)), so the team reads *wound down · restart wanted*; **(3) whether the team starts again at the reset by itself** — TD-026's schedule, or Paul's word as now; **(4) the settings page** — a new screen, so §4.5 gets it and §4.5a its controls (a control not in that table does not exist), with the gate's figure per profile the first setting on it; the usage chip on the Org page could carry the figure as a mark (*wk 91% · stops at 95%*) before any page exists. The page's first settings are therefore three: the 5-hour figure, the weekly figure, and *start the team at the weekly reset* (off). **Paul, later the same morning — the shape of the figures:** what he actually keeps back is *about 30% of each session and 10% of the weekly in reserve for each day's interactive work*, so the settings are **a reserve per session** (the 5-hour window's threshold is steady: 100 − 30 = 70%) and **a reserve per day** for the weekly window, and the gate computes the weekly threshold from the days left before the reset: `100 − reserve_per_day × days_left`, today counted whole — 10% a day gives 30% on the reset day itself, 60% with four days left, 90% on the last day; the reset time is the adapter's (§4.3). A consequence: the weekly threshold **rises** as the week goes, so §6's *pause … resume* is right for it after all — a team paused at 60% on a Wednesday resumes on Thursday when the line moves to 70% — and the wind-down-with-declaration is what happens when the window is out for the *last* day. The board item's *figure to write back after the reset* is now the pair (30, 10), which the briefs can say until the gate is built. **Paul agreed (30, 10) the same day**, and asked whether today's 95% would have been (30, 5): yes for the last day, but a reserve of 5 a day moves the whole week's line (65% on the reset day, not 30%), so a one-day change is an **override with an expiry** — *reserve 5 today*, gone at the reset, the standing pair untouched — which is TD-026's override shape — **and not this entry's** (Paul, 2026-09-22: *I do not think we need a mechanism built for temporary percentage settings immediately, that seems brittle and probably not the current priority; the user can handle it manually for now*): a one-day change is done by hand, and the mechanism is TD-101, if ever. **Slice (4), the briefs, 2026-09-22 (PR #439, the anchor):** the four briefs (`docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`, `grinder-ao-1.md`, the presets `manager.md` and `grinder.md`) say a pause prompt is a wait, never a stop, and name the (30, 10) reserves; the 95% sentence is gone. **Left:** the chip's line (slice 3's remainder), the promote, and `ao gate grind 5h=30 week=10/day` once promoted — the anchor's. **One seam slice (1) leaves, read at the merge:** a controller's `send` to a gated member is refused, the wrap-up's included, so a manager's wind-down cannot reach a paused member until the resume; a person's `ao team stop` can, and the stop-time kill is untouched. Fine while a pause is short; the design should say which it means. **Next:** (4) the settings page is designed 2026-09-25 and built by TD-146, TD-147 and TD-148; what is left here is the live look.
**Location:** design §6 (*Usage gate*), §5 (`usage_gate:`), §4.5 (screens) and §4.5a; `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the policies tick), `~/.agentorc`; the two briefs

**Why:** the subscription is one pool for Paul's interactive work and the team's; the team should take what he will not use that week, and only he knows how much that is. A number in a brief is read once, at start, by a model that then judges it; a number in a setting is read every tick by the host agent, moved without a restart, and shown on the page.

**Related:** TD-099 (a usage stop is a declaration; the header's collapse), TD-026 (schedules; a start at the reset), design §6, §4.2a (profiles), TD-073 (the windows and labels are the adapter's).

## TD-101: A one-day override of the usage reserves, gone at the reset

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; split from TD-100 on Paul's word the same day)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** decision
**Status:** Open — **not wanted yet.** Paul, 2026-09-22: a mechanism for temporary reserve settings *seems brittle, and probably not the current priority; the user can handle it manually for now* — a separate entry *if we think the temporary change mechanic is worth doing*. What it would be, if it is: TD-100's standing reserves (a percent per session, a percent per day of the weekly window) left untouched while a person says *reserve 5 today*, and the override expires at the window's reset on its own — TD-026's *window overrides with an expiry* shape, one more row on TD-100's settings page. Until then a one-day change is two edits by hand: the reserve down, and back up after the reset. **Next:** not wanted yet (Paul, 2026-09-22).
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — whether the override is wanted at all (not wanted yet, 2026-09-22)
**Location:** design §6 (*Usage gate*), TD-100's settings page, TD-026's overrides

**Why:** on 2026-09-22 Paul wanted one day at 95% and the team back the next week at the standing line; with reserves that is a reserve of 5 for the last day, but 5 a day for the whole week moves every day's line up (65% on the reset day, not 30%). The honest form of a one-day change is an override that expires, not a new standing number — and building it is not worth its complexity until the standing gate (TD-100) exists and the by-hand way has hurt.

**Related:** TD-100 (the standing reserves and the settings page), TD-026 (overrides with an expiry).

## TD-103: The manager is an LLM doing policy the tick should do

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the full design review with Fable, agreed by Paul the same day)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — live check pending (the *done when*: a night with the brief silent, the manager's round count halved). **The briefs' cut done 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #471):** the rules are live (the host agent runs 30ab5ad, which carries #459–#462). `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` no longer fills, closes or nudges the techlead seat, restarts a wanted restart or sends the idle nudge; it reads `restart_blocked`, `restart_ceiling` and *idle · open work* and boards them, and its round ends in `ao wait --timeout 3540` run in the background (a foreground Bash call is capped at ten minutes). Design §4.8 and §6 say so. **Left for the anchor:** `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`'s paragraph *What the host agent does on this host, so you do not* now repeats the template, and could be cut in the next brief edit. It is harmless as it stands, since it agrees with the template. A team started before the promote keeps the old words until its next start. Was: Open — **slice (5)'s Inbox row built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #462, stacked on #461; the anchor merges):** **Inbox row: restart** with Open, Resume, Snooze and a Dismiss that hides that one mark's row (the attention store's `dismissed:<at>`; the design's *and the mark* is unworkable against the tick, see design-history §6). **Left: the briefs' cut** — the four rules out of `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` and `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`, the round's `ao wait` to an hour — which waits on TD-114 (grinder-ao-2) reshaping the same files, and on #459/#461/this PR being promoted: a brief that stops saying *fill the seat* before the tick does is a gap. Was: **slice (4) built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #461, stacked on #459; `src/sessionorc`, the anchor merges):** rule 2, the wanted restart (`restart_blocked`, `restart_blocked_sent_at`), and rule 4, the idle nudge (`nudged_at`, the card's *idle · open work*), with `IDLE_NUDGE` 20 min; a node's member is not typed at by either rule yet (written into §6). **Left: slice (5)**, the Inbox row and the briefs. Was: **slice (3) built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #459; `src/sessionorc`, the anchor merges):** rule 3 on the home's tick (`_seat_pass`) — `seat_due: {at, by}` from `seat` (`asks` while a question waits, `prs` from the new home-owned `seat_count` read with `gh` per repo at the seat's path, `every` on the clock), the fill replaying the launch record with `keep_mail` (`why: fill`), `FILL_CEILING` 6 an hour over seats sharing a controller then `restart_ceiling {why: fill}` and the card's *fills exhausted*, the close of a hook-confirmed idle seat after `SEAT_IDLE_GRACE` with nothing due or unpushed, and the card's *· 4 of 10*; the three open design points settled on the techlead's answer and written into §6 rule 3. The same PR made rule 1's *not gated* read the profile (the gate clears `gated` on an exited record).  Was: **slice (2) built 2026-09-22 (`grinder-ao-1`, PR #453, stacked on slice (1)'s #447; `src/sessionorc`, the anchor merges both):** rule 1 on the home's tick (`_keep_running` → `_crash_restart`), replaying `launch/<address>.json` through `create` — locally, or routed to the node when its link is up, skipped when not; `restarts` carried onto the new record; a failed replay counts with its `error`; `RESTART_CEILING` 3 in `RESTART_WINDOW` 2 h, then `restart_ceiling` and the card's *restarts exhausted · 3 in 2 h*. **`seat: {trigger, after?}` moved into this slice from (3):** `ao team start` writes it on the techlead and each seat, since rule 1 must skip seats and nothing else on a record says which it is; (3) computes `seat_due` from it. Was: Open — **decided by Paul 2026-09-22 (option 1: the host agent's tick) and designed the same day (the anchor; eight Sonnet design-review rounds, every finding adopted, closed by Fable after round 8 — design-history.md §6): §6 *Keeping a team running* — a `supervised` field set by `ao team start` and `ao new --supervised`, the rule *a restart is not a start*, a launch record written at every supervised create, four policies (crash restart under `RESTART_CEILING`, wanted restart, seats on `seat`/`seat_due` under `FILL_CEILING`, the idle nudge on `IDLE_NUDGE`), the marks `restarts`, `restart_ceiling`, `restart_blocked`, `nudged_at`, a §4.5a Inbox row, and what stays the manager's.** Build in five slices, each its own PR: **(1) — built 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-1), awaiting the anchor's merge** — `supervised` and the launch record — `models`, `create` writing `launch/<id>.json`, `ao team start` and `ao new --supervised` setting the field, §4.4a ownership; **(2)** crash restart and the ceiling — the policy on the tick at the home, `restarts`, `restart_ceiling`, the card's ending text; **(3)** the seat policy — `seat` written at team start, `seat_due` from `asks_waiting`, the reports tick's PR count and the clock, fill and close, `FILL_CEILING` (this is TD-098 step 2 and TD-104); **(4)** the wanted restart and the idle nudge — `restart_blocked`, `nudged_at`, the fixed lines; **(5)** the Inbox row, and the briefs: the four rules leave `manager.md` and the repo's `manager-ao-1.md`, the round's `ao wait` timeout grows to an hour, in force from the next team start. Earlier: awaiting the decision — design §4.9a and TD-026 said the host agent starts nothing by itself, while §6's run window already had it starting workers. **Next:** built; the *done when* is a night with the brief silent.
**Location:** design §6 (policies), §4.8 (*Waking a manager*, the restart ceiling, *The manager is a session, not code*), §4.9a (restart_wanted), §4.9b (the seat rule); `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the policies tick), `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md`

**Why:** every mechanical rule in a brief costs a full status read on the strongest model every round, is applied with a model's judgement where none is wanted, and is stale by up to a round; the same rule on the tick is free, exact and immediate. The manager then does only judgement — cadence verdicts, escalation, the wind-down's second reading — and can run far less often, or a team with only mechanical needs runs with `manager: person` and no manager session at all. This is the largest run-cost saving the review found.

**Fix, once decided — each rule a policy in §6, keyed on `unattended` and `controllers` (§9 invariant 9), never on a role, and each a `create` or `send` the host agent makes as the record's first live controller would:**
1. **Crash restart**: a member `exited` before its stop time with no declaration → `create` again under the same name, directory, worktree, profile and brief (the record's own fields), inside the ceiling (3 in 2 h, one_for_one, then a board line). The ceiling moves from the brief to a constant.
2. **Wanted restart**: `restart_wanted` and `idle`/`exited`/`closed` with nothing dirty or unpushed → `close` then the same `create`; `early` → the board; dirty → one send naming what is left, then the board. This is §4.9a's controller act, done by the tick.
3. **Seat fill**: a seat `exited`/`closed` whose trigger is met → `create --keep-mail` (TD-104 makes the trigger a field); an idle seat with its trigger unmet → `close`. Six fills an hour per team.
   *For slice (3)'s builder (grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-22):* a build of the count and clock half, made to TD-104's older wording before #441 landed, is parked on branch `td104-seat-due` (PR #444, closed unmerged). Its field shape and ownership are wrong for §6 rule 3 (node-owned `seat_due`, `trigger` as identity). What should carry over: `reports._prs_or_none` (a `gh` outage must not read as zero merges), the pure `reports.seat_due` and `tests/test_seat_due.py`, `models.seat_trigger`, the trigger through `teams.Launch.create_params`, and the card's count. Three design points it met: (a) `seat_due: {at, by}` — `by` is defined nowhere; (b) the card draws *4 of 10 PRs* from `seat_due`, which is *set* only once met, so the count before then has no field; (c) a home-owned `seat_due` computed at the home runs `gh` against a checkout — for a seat on a container node, the same absolute path (§4.4a), which the design does not say it reads.
4. **The idle nudge**: `idle` with an open lane item for twenty minutes → the one send naming the reference and the three words a run ends with; never a second before the first is answered.
5. What stays in the brief: the cadence check and its verdicts, permission triage (or its own policy later — an allowlist per brief), the wind-down's second reading of the ledger, and escalation prose. The manager's round then ends in `ao wait` on a longer timeout, and the brief loses a page.

**Done when** a team runs a night with the four rules on the tick and the brief silent on them, the manager's round count halves, and a crashed member is back inside a tick rather than a round.

**Related:** TD-026 (the host agent starts nothing; schedules), TD-100 (the usage gate, the first policy built since the stop time), TD-104 (seat triggers), TD-098 (seats), TD-083 (restart_wanted), TD-053 (wind-down), design §6.

## TD-105: Mail carries more kinds and machinery than a night's traffic warrants

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — for evaluation, not scheduled. The one measured night (TD-052 step 5) produced one `ask`, eight replies and nothing to the person. Built since: five kinds plus a `system` sender, eight `closed_reason`s, outcome debts with `OUTCOMES_OWED_MAX` and the `handed` mark, pass-up, answered-for-you, copies, per-thread and per-pair tallies, the wake budget, damping, nonces and six person-side controls (snooze, pause, resume, go-with-it, decline, dismiss). Three cuts cost little and remove design surface without removing a guarantee: **(a) fold `conflict` into an `ask` with several addressees** — §4.10 already says *a conflict is an ask for every rule in this section* and differs only in delivery shape, and the kind appears in briefs but nowhere in the code's own logic beyond a string; **(b) freeze the outcome-debt machinery at what is built** — no new `handed` cases beyond Log TD until a night shows a debt going unpaid; **(c) describe the thread and pair tallies as one exchange bound with two numbers**, one mechanism in the design and the code, rather than two paragraphs. None of these changes a number set in TD-052 step 6. **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.
**Location:** design §4.10, `src/sessionorc/mail.py`, `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`_msg`, 435 lines), `src/agentorc/briefs/`

**Why:** every kind and closing is a case every brief, every row renderer and every fact-check has to know; the traffic does not yet justify the ones a person never meets.

**Related:** TD-052 (mail), TD-079 (outcomes), TD-069 (the Inbox), TD-039 (the conflict).

## TD-106: Identity on one host is finished as built

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — for evaluation. §4.8a runs to some 600 lines of design for what it itself calls *tamper-evidence, not a wall*: peer credentials, an ancestry walk with double reads, the session id, the controlling tty, a cgroup clause with four preconditions, three modes, coalescing alarms, suspend, Log TD, and a chain of command to a techlead that is designed and not built — on a system with one user and one account. What is built works and is in `enforce` on kmaster and the container node; the real wall is the agents-only node (`person: false`), which is built. Recommendation: declare the section finished — **drop the unbuilt techlead alarm path (*Who answers first*) and the `alarms` grant from the design** rather than carry them as debt, and decide whether the cgroup clause earns its preconditions or goes (with it off, a fully detached process on a host with a person reads as *outside*, which the design already accepts as the dev-run case). **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.
**Location:** design §4.8a, §4.9b (*Alarms*), `src/sessionorc/identity.py`

**Why:** every line of unbuilt identity design is a line a techlead primer and a fact-check must carry for a path nobody has asked to use.

**Related:** TD-077 (identity), TD-075 (the techlead), §4.4a (*A node that carries no person*).

## TD-108: The host agent is one class holding link, mail, identity and policy

**Priority:** Medium (High 2026-09-26 to 28 while the split gated a second grinder; the split and the lanes are done)
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done. **Step 2 (2026-09-28, the anchor, after TD-196 split `ui/app.py`):** the grinder lanes are redrawn by package in `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-2.md`: grinder-ao-1 holds `src/sessionorc/**`, grinder-ao-2 `src/agentorc/**`, and an entry goes to whoever's package holds its fix (the larger part when it spans both, the claim naming the other's files). This replaces the interim split sent by mail that morning. The priority drops to Medium: what is left is the fold. **Step 1a (2026-09-26, grinder-ao-1, PR #632):** `agent.py`'s module-level constants, helpers and small classes (90 definitions, 89 distinct names — one is assigned twice) moved to `sessionorc/agent_common.py`, which the mixins import without importing the agent; `agent.py` keeps `HostAgent`, `serve_until_signal` and `main` and re-exports every moved name. The eleven constants the tests patch are read as `agent_common.X` at call time, and the tests patch them there. **Step 1b (PR #633):** the container supervisor and the link → `sessionorc/agent_link.py`, `LinkMixin`, which `HostAgent` inherits. **Step 1c (PR #634):** a node's calls the home answers and the acts across the link → `sessionorc/agent_remote.py`, `RemoteMixin`. **Step 1d (PR #635):** who is calling → `sessionorc/agent_identity.py`, `IdentityMixin`. **Step 1e (PR #638):** mail → `sessionorc/agent_mail.py`, `MailMixin`. **Step 1f (PR #640):** the person's inbox bookkeeping → `sessionorc/agent_inbox.py`, `InboxMixin`. **Step 1g (PR #642):** waking and the doorbell → `sessionorc/agent_wake.py`, `WakeMixin`, with the run that followed them in `agent.py` (the hook's entry and `rpc_decide`, and the small reads and settings RPCs). **Step 1h (PR #645):** the attention trail → `sessionorc/agent_attention.py`, `AttentionMixin`. **Where `agent_wake`'s passengers belong** (`rpc_hook`, `rpc_decide` and the ten small reads and settings RPCs moved with 1g because they followed the section in the file — the techlead's read of #642) is a question for after step 1j, not a second move now. **Step 1i (PR #647):** reconcile and the tick's policies → `sessionorc/agent_tick.py`, `TickMixin`; one line changed beyond the move, `_read_repos` calling its sibling static method as `TickMixin._read_repo` (it named `HostAgent`, which the mixin cannot import). **Step 1j (PR #649):** one org, streaming and connection handling → `sessionorc/agent_serve.py`, `ServeMixin`. **Step 1 is done with it:** `agent.py` holds the lifecycle, the RPC methods and the helpers (about 1,680 lines), and `HostAgent` inherits ten mixins. Step 2 followed on 2026-09-28 (below). The question from #642 is still open: where do `agent_wake`'s passengers belong (`rpc_hook`, `rpc_decide` and the ten small reads and settings RPCs)? **`create_app` split by page (2026-09-22, grinder-ao-1):** it now builds the shared helpers (`call`, `defs`, `seats_of`, the person-inbox reads …) into one namespace and hands it to six register functions — `_pages_routes`, `_new_routes`, `_sessions_routes`, `_teams_routes`, `_inbox_routes`, `_stream_routes` — none over 200 lines, `create_app` itself 159; same module, so the tests' `monkeypatch` of `LocalClient`, `rpc` and `PtySession` still reaches every route; route order within each group unchanged. **Left:** the agent split by concern and the ten-RPC fold (both `src/sessionorc`, the anchor's to merge). **The fold is not mechanical (2026-09-22, `grinder-ao-1`, looked at before building):** the ten fall into four routing classes that `src/sessionorc/modes.py` and design §4.4a key on the **method name** — `suspend` and `identity_log` are `HOME_EDITS` (forwarded to the home from a node), `inbox_delete` / `inbox_snooze` / `inbox_pause` / `inbox_resume` / `inbox_go_with_it` are `MAILBOX` (refused offline, forwarded when linked), `inbox_delete` and `identity_ack` are in `PERSON_NODE_BOUND`, `identity_ack` is also routed by the link (`agent.py`, `_route_act`), and `inbox_dismiss` / `attention_snooze` are in none. One `inbox_act {entry, action}` would need each of those tables — and §4.4a's offline table — to key on *(method, action)*, and design §4.8a already says `identity_ack`'s wire name *stays* because it is in `NODE_ACTS`. So the fold is a design decision first (the anchor's): either the tables take an action, or the fold is dropped and the ten stay as named RPCs sharing one person-only gate helper. Not built. Was: Open. `src/sessionorc/agent.py` is 5,833 lines; `_msg` is 435 lines and `rpc_create` 180; `src/agentorc/ui/app.py`'s `create_app` is 1,090 lines and `view` 250; there are 46 RPC methods, 115 fields on the record and 186 module-level constants. Not a design fault yet, but §4.4a's per-field ownership rules and §4.8a's classification live in one class with the tick and the mail. Fix, before phase 3's policies add to it: split the agent by concern (link, mail, identity, policies, reconcile) as modules or mixins over one state object; split `create_app` by page; fold the ten person-only inbox RPCs (`inbox_delete`, `inbox_snooze`, `inbox_dismiss`, `attention_snooze`, `inbox_pause`, `inbox_resume`, `inbox_go_with_it`, `identity_ack`, `identity_log`, `suspend`) into one `inbox_act {entry, action}` with the same person-only gate. No behaviour change; the tests are the guard.
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — steps 1 and 2 are done; the fold (step 3) is a design decision first
**2026-09-26 — the split, planned (the anchor, Paul's go):** `agent.py` is now **7,053 lines** (it was 5,833 at this entry's filing), 227 methods on `HostAgent`, 53 RPCs, and the most-changed file in the repo (127 commits in 30 days). All ten open grinder builds (TD-132, 146, 149, 151, 152, 165, 173, 174, 185, 186) touch it or `models.py`, so grinder-ao-2, whose lane excludes it, declared out of work with 18 entries pickable. Paul stopped grinder-ao-2 so that grinder-ao-1 does the split alone and undisturbed. **Who and when:** grinder-ao-1 alone, after its TD-146 has merged and while no other PR touching `agent.py` is open. **Step 1, mechanical:** move each of the file's own sections into a mixin module that `HostAgent` inherits, under one state object and with no behaviour change. The sections are: reconcile and the tick's policies (restart, usage, wake); the attention trail; mail; the person's inbox bookkeeping; waking and the doorbell; the container supervisor; the link; a node's calls at the home; acts across the link; who is calling; streaming. Keep `sessionorc.agent` as the assembly and the import path. **Hazard:** the tests patch module constants by path (`sessionorc.agent.TICK_SECONDS`, `USAGE_EVERY`, `SUBMIT_SECONDS`, `CREATE_GRACE`, `WRAPUP_GRACE`, `SEND_STALL_SECONDS`, `REPORT_WRITE`, `CLOSED_KEEP`) and methods on the module (`rpc_close`, `_push_changes`, `_save`, …). A mixin that reads a constant from its own module would no longer see the patch, so either the constants stay in `agent.py` and the mixins read them as `agent.X` at call time, or the tests are retargeted in the same PR. The full suite must pass unchanged in count. It is best done as several PRs, one or two sections each, merged quickly, so no PR stays open against a moving file. **Step 2:** the anchor redraws the grinder briefs' lanes by the new files (`docs/briefs/grinder-ao-*.md`; #620 was a first try, closed for this) and restarts grinder-ao-2. **Step 3, the fold:** unchanged, a design decision first (below). `app.py`'s module split is TD-196. **Location:** `src/sessionorc/agent.py`, `src/agentorc/ui/app.py`, `src/sessionorc/models.py`

**Why:** the design's ownership rules are easier to check when the code is shaped like them, and a 1,000-line function is where the next TD-063 hides.

**Related:** TD-063 (flakes), TD-066 (frame limits), TD-103 (policies to add).

## TD-109: Docs housekeeping after the design pass

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Partly done — (1) done 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-1): the six finished step briefs moved to `docs/briefs/archive/`, the README says so; (2) done 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-1, PR #451): `docs/briefs/README.md` says what is true of briefs now — no run, date or stop time in a brief, the usage gate and `ao new --until` in place of per-brief rules, the primer named by the team definition's `context:`; (5) checked 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-2): the primer (1,850 words, already inside the recipe's two to three thousand) was read against the design after the pass and three stale claims corrected beyond the restart lines TD-103 slice (5) had already fixed — `auditor` among the roles; `lead` and `orchestrator` are unknown roles, not read for a release; and `manager: {role: person}`, not the bare string — and `tests/test_primer.py` passes against the new headings; no shrink beyond that, since nothing in it repeats the design at length. (3), (4) and (6) remain. Was: (1) `docs/briefs/` holds finished step briefs — `td036-migration.md`, `td052-step2/3/4/8/8b.md` — beside the live team briefs; move them under `docs/briefs/archive/` or delete them (git keeps them). (2) `docs/briefs/README.md` describes the hand-launch path as if teams did not exist (TD-067). (3) The ledger is 1,000 lines with 50 open entries; a sweep of what is *Built — live check pending* (TD-038, TD-058, TD-095, TD-096, TD-097) into the archive once Paul has looked. (4) `docs/design-history.md` has its row and its rule in CLAUDE.md (the design pass added both); the same rule belongs in cadence.md §2, which is a SYNCED file and is dev-cadence's to change. (6) The pass cut the design from 86,000 words to 69,000 by removing narrative and kept every rule; a second, tighter pass — reasons cut to one sentence, the §4.5a cells and §4.4a shortened further — is worth doing once a fact-check of this one has held. (5) The techlead primer (`docs/briefs/techlead-context.md`) should shrink now that the design is readable per fill, and `tests/test_primer.py` re-run against the new headings. **Next:** (3) waits on Paul's live looks, (4) is dev-cadence's, (6) waits on a fact-check.
**Location:** `docs/briefs/`, `docs/technical_debt.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/design-history.md`

**Why:** the design pass makes the design readable; the rest of `docs/` should not undo that.

**Related:** TD-067 (the operator's guide), TD-075 (the primer).

## TD-110: A night report, generated from the records

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review — a feature the review recommended)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — for evaluation. At wind-down (or at a stop time, or on `ao team report <team>` for a running one) the home writes one summary per team from what it already holds: PRs merged from members' branches (the derived reports), claims done, dropped and still open, `out_of_work` and `restart_wanted` reasons, mail and wake counts against the bounds, usage consumed per profile over the run, restarts against the ceiling, identity alarms, and what was left on the board. One FYI in the person inbox and one line on `docs/user_attention.md` (the §4.9a announcement grows into it). It replaces the manager's prose round log as the thing a person reads in the morning, and it is the first consumer of the records as a whole rather than one card at a time. **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.
**Location:** design §4.9a (*A wind-down is announced*), §4.5a (an Inbox row), `src/sessionorc/reports.py`, `src/agentorc/teamrun.py`

**Why:** "what happened overnight" is answered today by reading a manager's log on a launch branch and several transcripts; the records already know.

**Related:** TD-053 (the wind-down's board line), TD-079 (outcomes), TD-069 (the Inbox), TD-087 (usage readings).

## TD-111: `ao doctor`

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; the design review — a feature the review recommended)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** evaluation
**Status:** Open — for evaluation. A large share of the ledger's entries begin with *found live*: hooks that did not fire, a tmux server replaced under the agent, an identity clause that read the person as unknown, a usage endpoint refusing, a node linked but behind, a wheel older than `main`. One command runs the checks and prints a line each: the settings layer's hooks fire in a scratch session; the tmux server's pid, start time and cgroup; identity mode and the detached-process check; each profile's usage endpoint and credentials; each node's link state and build; the running build against `origin/main`; `settings.yml` and `org.yml` parse. Read-only, never repairs. `ao status -v` already prints some of these; this gathers them and adds the ones that need a probe. **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/cli.py`, `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`host`, `identity`, `usage` RPCs), design §4.7

**Why:** every live incident in the ledger cost a session an hour of diagnosis that a check would have printed.

**Related:** TD-062 (build versus main), TD-077 (identity), TD-087 (usage), TD-057 (nodes).

The spike is a branch and an ADR, not a shipped adapter. **Next:** for evaluation, not scheduled.

## TD-114: A repo's brief replaces the whole template, so three repos carry copies of agentorc's mechanics that go stale together

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-22 (the anchor session; Paul's question the same evening — *is there a reason all of the boilerplate ao information is kept in each repo vs being kept here?* — and his second: whether a full override should stay)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **step (1) merged and step (2) done 2026-09-22 (the anchor): `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md` cut to supplements, `grinder-ao-2.md` and the briefs README reworded to match; the manager's supplement says the restarts, fills and nudges are the tick's on this host, which overrides the template's paragraphs until TD-103 slice (5) cuts them. Step (3), telling samscrape and dev-cadence, the same day as the promote. Was: step (1) built 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-2), for the anchor to merge with steps (2) and (3)**: `{repo}` and *This repo's rules* in the five templates; `Role.brief_text(supplement=…)` — a role the package ships a template for takes a repo's brief in its slot, a repo's own role takes it whole; `teams._brief` passes a definition's `brief:` as the supplement in place of `roles.<name>.brief`; `ao new --brief`, refused beside `--prompt`; the start's note (and `ao new`'s) on a heading a supplement repeats. **Once merged, the next promote fills every repo's current whole-file brief as a supplement** — and the start's note fires for `manager-ao-1.md` (two shared headings) but not for `grinder-ao-1.md` or `grinder-ao-2.md`, which share none: steps (2) and (3) belong with that promote, not after it. Step (3)'s recipe half is in the same PR: `team_skill.md` gains *A repo's brief* — what the templates assume, what a supplement holds, and its skeleton (TD-113 (c)); what is left of (3) is telling samscrape and dev-cadence. Was: Open — **designed 2026-09-22 (the anchor; Sonnet design review)**: §4.8 *A repo's brief is a supplement, never a replacement*, §4.9's `brief:` keys, §4.7 `ao new --brief`, the glossary. Decided: **(a)** a template (`src/agentorc/briefs/<role>.md`) holds every agentorc mechanic and ships with the package, so a promote moves every team's rules with the host agent that enforces them; **(b)** a repo's brief — a definition's `brief:`, a repo's `roles.<name>.brief`, `ao new --brief` — is filled into the template's `{repo}` slot, a section near the top headed *This repo's rules*; **(c)** where the two disagree the repo's rules win, and the template says so before the slot — this is what a repo uses to try a mechanics change before its promote, the one case the whole-file override served; **(d)** there is no whole-file replacement (Paul asked; a whole brief given as `brief:` is a long supplement, not an error); **(e)** the techlead's `context:` is the same idea for that seat and stays; **(f)** the repeatable-brief warning covers the supplement; **(g)** (review round 1) a supplement may add to the never-list and never take from it, and the gates the host agent enforces are not words a brief can move; a definition's `brief:` takes the slot in place of `roles.<name>.brief`, never concatenated; and the transition is named — a whole-file `brief:` filled verbatim would win over the template, so the start warns on a heading the template also has, and all three repos cut their briefs the day the build is promoted. Why it is High: agentorc, samscrape and dev-cadence each carry a 2,000–4,000-word copy of the manager and grinder mechanics (`docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` 4,144 words against the preset's 2,579), and the night of 2026-09-22 found three defects — the hand `ao new` crash restart made stale by TD-103 slice (2), the seat fill that cannot carry `{context}` (TD-113 (9)), the 95% usage sentence — each fixed in every copy separately. **Next:** what is left is telling samscrape and dev-cadence, the anchor's.
**Location:** design §4.8, §4.9, §4.7; `src/agentorc/repoconfig.py` (`REPO_PLACEHOLDER`, `brief_text(repo=…)`), `src/agentorc/briefs/*.md` (the slot and the precedence sentence), `src/agentorc/teams.py` (`_launch` passes the `brief:` text as the supplement), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao new --brief`), `src/agentorc/team_skill.md` (what a supplement contains), `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md` (cut to supplements), `tests/test_repoconfig.py`, `tests/test_cli_teams.py`

**Why:** every rule about agentorc in a repo's file is a rule the package cannot update, read by a model that then judges it; the same rule in the template moves with the build and is read the same way by every team on the host.

**Build, in order:** (1) `{repo}` in the four templates with the *This repo's rules* heading and the precedence sentence; `brief_text(repo=…)`; `teams._launch` and `ao new --brief` pass the supplement; `brief:` no longer replaces — a test that a definition's `brief:` text appears inside the template's output, and that the template's mechanics survive it. (2) Cut `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-1.md` to supplements (the anchor: hand-rule path) — everything that duplicates the preset goes; what stays is this repo's first reads, gate, standing rules and lane; the preset gains anything ours had that the preset lacked (TD-113 (0) and (9) first). (3) The same day as the promote, before any team start: tell samscrape and dev-cadence that their `brief:` files are now supplements filled verbatim, with the heading warning as the tell, so they cut theirs to the repo's own rules; the recipe's step 4 and skeleton (TD-113) describe the supplement, and `ao new --brief` lands with (1). **Done when** a `git diff` between any two repos' manager briefs shows no agentorc mechanics on either side.

**Related:** TD-113 (the recipe and the `{manager}` placeholder), TD-103 (the manager's mechanical rules on the tick), TD-042 (a brief names no run), TD-075 (the techlead's primer as the first supplement), TD-040 (presets and the override this replaces).

## TD-118: Where a team-run-day spends tokens that buy nothing: the machine-wide board at every start, one model for every role, the out-of-work triage in prose

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-23 (the anchor session; a Sonnet review of the team run's token spend, asked for by Paul the same day)
**Owner:** dev-cadence
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **(1) and (2) built 2026-09-23 (PRs #485, #487, #489, #493); decided by Paul 2026-09-23; the profile split is live the same day.** The review read the templates, the supplements, the skill, the hooks and last night's run logs, and ranked what it found by tokens per team-run-day. (1) **The SessionStart hook prints the whole machine's attention board** — `scripts/cadence_hooks.sh --session-start` runs dev-cadence's `nudge_user_attention.py --report --due-only --fetch` over every repo in the roster: 71 items, 16,915 bytes, about 4,200 tokens, at every start, restart and seat fill, nearly all of it samscrape and contractmatch lines no agentorc session can act on. **(2) One model for every role**: `grind` pinned every unattended role to Opus, the manager's mechanical rounds and the techlead's lookups included. **(3) The out-of-work search**: a grinder re-reads every open entry (the ledger is some 1,100 lines, some Status lines 500 words) and writes a paragraph classifying each, which the manager copies onto the board, which (1) then prints into every later start. (4) `grinder.md` points at all of `docs/cadence.md` (about 15,500 tokens) where `manager.md` points at §1–§4. (5) `manager.md` still mentions the crash restart and the ceiling twice after the TD-103 cut. The techlead primer was reviewed and left: it is already lean, and its cost was the model. **Done 2026-09-23:** profiles split by role in `~/.agentorc/profiles.yml` and the org's `roles:` — grinders stay on `grind` (Opus, the stronger coder), `manager`, `hunter` and `auditor` on `grind-sonnet`, `techlead` on `grind-fable` (Paul: *they are the guards of the architecture for each repo so I want them to be strong*); one account, one login, the gate reserves set on both new profiles; Haiku for the manager is the next step after a clean night on Sonnet. **Step (1), batch 1 built 2026-09-23 (`grinder-ao-2`, PR #485):** the three lines on TD-002 to TD-057 (twenty entries), in the template and the preamble, and `test_ledger.py` checks the shape of any entry that carries them; **batch 2 the same day** (TD-058 to TD-095, PR #487), and **batch 3** (TD-096 to TD-118), with the test now requiring the header on every open entry. **The rest of step (1) and step (2) built the same day** (`grinder-ao-2`): `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md`'s out-of-work rule filters on the header where a ledger carries it and its `--why` cites counts (the manager's wind-down line copies the `why`, so it follows), its first read names cadence §1–§4, and `manager.md`'s crash-restart and ceiling sentence is cut to the rule and the Inbox row that carries it. Left: (3), dev-cadence's; (4), after a clean night. (The review of that PR noticed the *wants a restart* sentence still boards `restart_blocked` and an `early` `restart_wanted`, which the same Inbox row carries — left as it is, being TD-103 slice (5)'s deliberate wording; the anchor's call.) **Also decided 2026-09-23: briefs stay prose** (a brief is read by a model, and the data a program enforces is already YAML in `org.yml`, `.agentorc.yml` and `settings.yml`); the parsable layer goes on the ledger, below. **Next:** (1) and (2) built; (3) is dev-cadence's hook, (4) waits on a clean night.
**Location:** `docs/technical_debt.md` (every entry, and `tests/test_ledger.py`), `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md` and `manager.md`, `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md` (the out-of-work `--why`), `scripts/cadence_hooks.sh` (SYNCED — dev-cadence's), `~/.agentorc/profiles.yml` and `org.yml` (done)

**Why:** an unattended session pays for every token it is handed whether or not it acts on it, and three of the five items hand it text it never acts on; the fourth hands the strongest model work a cheaper one does as well.

**Fix, in order:** (1) **The ledger header** — three fixed lines after `**Added:**` on every open entry, enforced by `test_ledger.py`: `**Owner:** anchor | grinder | paul | dev-cadence`, `**Kind:** build | design-first | live-check | evaluation | decision`, `**Pickable:** yes | no — <one clause>`; the grinder template's out-of-work rule becomes *filter on the header, re-read only what is pickable*, and `ao progress none --why` cites counts by kind and owner (*excluded: 12 design-first, 8 anchor, 6 dev-cadence, 3 live-check*) rather than a sentence per entry; the manager's wind-down board line follows. Migrate the entries in batches of twenty, a grinder's, the test tightened once the last batch lands. (2) `grinder.md`'s first read names cadence §1–§4, as the manager's does; the two restart mentions leave `manager.md`. (3) The hook: dev-cadence scopes the SessionStart nudge to the session's own board, without `--fetch`, when the session is unattended (the fourth item on the board's dev-cadence line); the machine-wide `--fetch` view stays `/attention`'s. (4) After a clean night, `manager` to Haiku, one org line.

**Related:** TD-103 (the rules that left the manager's brief), TD-114 (briefs as supplements), TD-105 and TD-106 (the same theme in mail and identity), TD-060 (the profile names are renamed with the rest).

## TD-120: The anchor's jobs go to the team: the techlead reads the held PRs, promotion is a person's press or a policy, and a designer seat holds the design conversation

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-23 (the anchor session; Paul's question the same day — who plays the anchor's checking-and-integrating part once the anchor is gone, and a Designer role for the design conversations)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Open — **step 2 designed 2026-09-24 (the designer, PR #525):** design §5 `promote: {run, check, auto}` (the repo's own; the one key of `.agentorc.yml` the host agent reads, from the home's registry), §6 *Promote* (three readings, three preconditions, `auto` after a ten-minute settle, the run detached with an intent file so the outcome is read from `check` and not from an exit code — this repo's promote restarts the agent that starts it —, `PROMOTE_BOUND`, a failure that blocks further promotes until cleared), §4.5a *Inbox row: promote* (counted under `auto: false` and on a failure; FYI in flight; the note alone under `auto: true`), §4.7 `ao promote [<repo>] [--sha]` and `ao promote status`; the glossary's *promote*; CLAUDE.md points at it. The build is **TD-132** (grinder; `src/sessionorc` — the techlead reads it). Two defaults steered to Paul: the row counted, and the settle. Was: **step 3 decided and built 2026-09-24 (Paul, 11:30 MDT: *make the designer changes*; the anchor, this PR):** design §4.8 *Role names* says the `designer` is an `unattended` member with a `design-first` lane and the three tiers; the glossary and the history follow; `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md` is rewritten for a session nobody drives (the lane, the tiers as `ao msg` kinds, the three ending words, the lease); `org.yml` lists it in `ao-grind` as `{role: designer, name: designer-ao-1, unattended: true, lane: design-first}` once this PR is merged (the brief is read from the checkout at team start), the interactive `designer-ao-1` closed first so the name starts clean. Draft #515 (the on-demand interactive member and the header button) closed as superseded. Left: step 2. Was: **step 3 reframed 2026-09-23 21:48 MDT (Paul: *if that is its directive should we make it unattended? … ideally, obvious designs would not be shown but just done*; the anchor's answer, decision pending).** The designer ran the night's four rounds from one `ao send` without waiting on anyone, which is an unattended worker with a design lane, not an interactive seat. Proposed: the `designer` role gains `unattended: true` and a lane of the ledger's `Kind: design-first` entries, `ao-grind` lists it as a member (it then starts with the team, drains that lane, exits on empty, and counts in §4.9a's concluded test as any member does), and its brief carries three tiers of what to do with a design, each a mail kind the Inbox already draws: **obvious** → designed, fact-checked, merged, an uncounted FYI `note` with the PR; **a defensible default Paul might care about** → a `steer` with the default and a bound never shorter than a night, the PR opened at once and merged at the bound unless answered; **taste, scope, spending** → an `ask` with two to four `--answer`s, the entry waits. Batching is the Inbox: the asks and steers wait under *Needs you* and are answered when Paul opens it; Focus on the designer still works for a live conversation. The on-demand interactive member and the header button of step 3 as first written become unnecessary — starting the team starts the designer — unless Paul wants design runs separate from build runs. Risk named: the designer decides what is obvious; the FYI note with the PR, the fact-check on every design PR, and Overrule cover a wrong call. `org.yml` and `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md` are the anchor's to change, on Paul's word. Was: **decided with Paul 2026-09-23, three steps.** The anchor session has been doing four jobs beside its own design work: reading and merging the PRs on the hand-rule paths (TD-093), promoting the live copy, running design rounds with Paul, and deciding the small questions the ledger leaves open. Where each goes: **(1) the techlead reads and merges the held PRs** — already the design's shape (§4.9b *The reader*: `review: {reader: techlead, held: [...]}` on the grinder role, carried onto the record); what is left is TD-093 slice 4, the techlead's brief saying it reads a held PR against the design and merges or blocks it, and slice 3's page half (#479); then `org.yml`'s `roles.grinder` gains `review: {reader: techlead, held: [src/sessionorc/**, docs/briefs/**, org.yml]}` and TD-093's hand rule retires the first time the techlead merges one cleanly. No integrator seat: once the techlead merges, integration is covered, and the manager already relays. **(2) Promotion is mechanical, and a repo's own** (Paul 2026-09-23: *these design items have to be generalized for any repo; the promote process needs configurable parts*): nothing in the policy names pip, a venv or systemd. A repo declares **`promote: {run, check, auto}`** in `.agentorc.yml` — `run`, the command that makes `main` live on the home checkout's host (this repo: the pip pair from CLAUDE.md; samscrape: its skaffold run; contractmatch: a wrangler deploy; dev-cadence: sync-all); `check`, a command that prints the sha that is live (this repo reads the installed wheel; samscrape the deployed image tag), which is what the Inbox row compares `main` against, and what dev-cadence's `scripts/cadence_deploy_check.sh` (cadence §5, *adapt per repo*) already is the shape of — the two agree or one calls the other; `auto: true|false`, whether the tick runs it or a press does. A repo with no block has no promote and no row. The promote itself is two commands with three preconditions — `main` clean and current, CI green on that sha, and nothing a person is mid-way through — none of which needs a model. Design first (§6, a policy; §4.5a one Inbox row): the host agent's tick sees `origin/main` move, checks the sha's CI with `gh`, runs the pair from CLAUDE.md, restarts the units (sessions live in tmux and survive it; an attached terminal reconnects under §4.6's contract), and files *promoted <sha>* to the person inbox as a `note`; a failure is a board line, and until the person clears it nothing further is promoted. A **Promote** press on the same row is the manual form and the rollback path (*promote <sha>* with an older sha). Until built, the anchor promotes by hand as CLAUDE.md says. **(3) A designer seat, and the on-demand interactive member that seats it** (Paul 2026-09-23: *do we want a Designer button on a team card?* — yes, as a generic control): a team definition may carry a member with **`unattended: false` and `start: on-demand`** — `ao team start` does not create it, §4.9a's concluded test does not count it, the tick never restarts or nudges it (it is the person's, TD-096), and the team's header draws **one button named by the role's label** (*Designer* here) that starts the session under the definition's name, worktree and badge when there is none live, and raises its Focus when there is; stopping it is the session's own Close, which exists, so there is no toggle. The row is §4.5a's, the field is the definition's and the record's (`start`), and any repo may seat any interactive role this way. That is the whole of step 3's design; what follows is the role itself. **A designer seat**: the role `designer` — interactive, Fable, one per repo team, brief `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md` — holds the design conversation: `docs/design.md` first, the rounds with Paul, the pickable entries the grinders pick, the techlead's pass-ups that Paul routes to it, and the decisions the design already answers; taste, scope, spending, `org.yml` and team starts wait for Paul. It holds no grant and merges nothing on the held paths. **Done in this PR**: the brief, §4.8 *Role names* and the glossary name the role, and `org.yml`'s `roles:` carries `designer: {profile: grind-fable}` (Paul's go-ahead 2026-09-23). **Not yet a team member**: §4.9a's *every member is finished* counts every live session carrying the badge, and an interactive member never declares, so a designer in `members:` would keep its team from ever concluding. Step 3's design, above: the on-demand interactive member — §4.9a and `teamrun`'s concluded test skip it, the manager's brief says so, the header button is §4.5a's; then `ao-grind`'s `members:` gains `{role: designer, name: designer-ao-1, unattended: false, start: on-demand, brief: docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md}` and the button seats it. Until then the designer is started by hand, `ao new --role designer -d ~/agentorc -w designer-ao-1 designer-ao-1`, outside the team. **Next:** nothing left to design; step 2's build is TD-132, and this entry archives with it.
**Blocked by:** TD-132
**Location:** `~/.agentorc/org.yml` (`roles:`, `teams.ao-grind`), `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md`, `docs/briefs/techlead-context.md` (the hand rule it states), design §4.8 *Role names*, §4.9a (the concluded test), §4.9b *The reader*, §6 (the promote policy), §4.5a (the Inbox row), CLAUDE.md *The live copy is promoted, not edited*, `src/agentorc/teamrun.py` (`concluded`), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the tick)

**Why:** the anchor is one interactive session in the main checkout, and everything it does by hand is a thing the team cannot do without it: a run ends with PRs waiting on a read nobody else may make, a merged fix is not live until someone types the promote, and the design that feeds the grinders has no seat of its own. Each job has a home that already exists in the design or costs nothing to name.

**Fix:** (1) TD-093 slice 4, then the `review:` line in `org.yml` and the hand rule's retirement in TD-093, `techlead-context.md` and CLAUDE.md. (2) Design the `promote:` block (§5, the repo's own), the policy (§6) and its Inbox row (§4.5a), then a grinder builds it (`src/sessionorc`, the reader merges); this repo's `.agentorc.yml` gets its block, and each other repo writes its own. (3) Design the on-demand interactive member (§4.9, §4.9a, §4.5a's header button), build the definition key, the concluded test's change and the button, add the designer to `ao-grind`. Every piece is a repo's declaration or a rule about mode, never about this repo or a role name.

**Related:** TD-093 (the reader), TD-062 (the live copy is promoted), TD-096 (an interactive member is left alone), TD-075 (the techlead), design §4.9a, §4.9b.

## TD-122: The usage chip polls once per profile, so four profiles on one account rate-limit the endpoint and every chip holds a stale reading; the chip names the profile, never the tool or the account

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-23 (Paul, comparing the chip with the account's usage page; the anchor session)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Open — **the live check failed 2026-09-28**: with one poll per account, the endpoint still refused every poll from 22:26Z for six hours or more (TD-231 investigates, TD-230 shows the age and bounds the gate). **built 2026-09-23 (grinder-ao-1, PR #517): the poll, its back-off and its cache keyed on the account through the adapter's `account_for`, `label` on the contract, per-model weekly windows, one chip per account; the hover's per-profile reserve, days left and next move (the techlead's read of #517) in PR #520; what is left is the live look on `docs/user_attention.md`.** **Designed 2026-09-23 (the designer): §4.2a *The usage reading is the account's*, §4.3 (`label`, `usage()` once per account, every window carried), §4.4 *Usage* (the poll, the cache, the back-off and the drop keyed on the account) and §4.5a **usage** chip (one per account, `<tool> · <account> · <label> n%`) are the spec; §6's gate reads the account's reading against the profile's lines. A grinder builds it; `src/sessionorc`, so the reader merges.** *Done when*: with two profiles on one account live, `_refresh_usage` calls the adapter once per tick of the cadence and both profiles' `usage` answers carry the same `fetched`; a `rate_limited` reply backs off both; the chip on the Org reads *Claude · paul · week n%* once, with both profiles and their sessions on hover; a profile's reserve line still draws from `gate` per profile; the adapter carries a per-model weekly window as `week · <model>` when the endpoint reports one and the worst-window rule takes it in; the shell adapter's `label` exists and its chip still does not; tests cover the once-per-account poll, the shared back-off and the per-model label. Found 2026-09-23. Since the profiles were split by role (`grind`, `grind-sonnet`, `grind-fable`, and `default`, all `account: paul`), the host agent's usage poll (`_refresh_usage`, `USAGE_EVERY` five minutes) asks the endpoint once **per profile**, four calls where one would do; the endpoint answered `rate_limited` to every call from 16:10Z on and asked for an hour (`retry_after: 3600`), so the chip holds the 16:10Z reading dimmed with *· stale* — `5h 12% · week 21%` when the account's page read 7% and 24% — and prints it four times, once per profile, as *week 21% · stale grind · week 21% / 40% · stale +1*. Two faults: the poll and the chip are keyed on the profile when the quota is the **account's** (§4.2a: one login, one usage window, however many profiles share it); and the chip never names the tool or the account, so *grind · week 21%* reads as nothing to the person who knows their account as *Claude, paul* (Paul 2026-09-23; TD-071 item 8 said the same of the older chip). A third: the endpoint reports a per-model weekly window beside *All models* (the account page shows *Fable 17%*) that the reading does not carry. **Next:** built (PR #517, the reader merges); what is left is the live look after a promote.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`_refresh_usage`, `_usage`, `_usage_wait`, `USAGE_EVERY`; the `usage` and `gate` RPCs), `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py` (`usage`, `UsageRefused`, `_retry_after`), design §4.2 (*usage*), §4.2a (accounts), §4.5a **usage** chip, §6 *Usage gate* (its reserves are per profile today), `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.js` (`#usagechip`)

**Why:** a stale number at the moment it is looked at is the failure the chip exists to prevent, and this one is self-inflicted: the fourth profile turned a working poll into a refused one. The gate (§6) reads the same readings, so a stale reading also holds every unattended session against a line that moved an hour ago.

**Fix, design first:** (1) §4.2a: **the reading is the account's** — the adapter's `usage(profile)` is called once per `(adapter, account)` among the profiles live sessions use, and every profile sharing the account carries the same reading (`fetched`, `reason`, `retry_after` included); the gate's reserves stay per profile (a reserve is a policy, a reading is a fact), each read against the account's reading. (2) §4.5a: **one chip per account**, `<tool> · <account> · <label> n%` — *Claude · paul · week 24%* — the tool's display name from the adapter (§4.3), the profiles sharing it and each one's line on hover, the rest of the row as today (worst window, the line after the number, *· stale*, red at a cap). (3) The adapter carries every window the endpoint reports, the per-model weekly ones labelled by the model (*week · Fable 17%*), and the chip's worst-window rule takes them in. (4) Tests: two profiles on one account poll once; a `rate_limited` reply backs off the account, not the profile. Then a grinder builds it.

**Related:** TD-087 (a failure says why; stale readings), TD-100 (the gate's lines), TD-073 (the adapter's windows), TD-071 item 8 (the chip reads as nothing), TD-118 (profiles split by role — the change that exposed this).

## TD-124: The pages have no keyboard: picking a team or a card, opening Focus, answering an Inbox row all take the mouse

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-23 (Paul's question; the anchor session)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — live look pending (on the board). **Built 2026-09-23 (PR #519, grinder-ao-2)**: `AO.KEYS` and one `keydown` handler in `app.js`, the card a tab stop, the **?** overlay and its top-bar button, the mockups' ring, and `tests/test_ui_keys.py` driving the table under node; no browser harness here, so the keys themselves are unexercised until the live look. Was: Open — **designed 2026-09-23 (the designer): §4.5a's four **keys** rows (every page, the Org's ring, the Inbox's ring, the **?** overlay), the paragraphs in §4.5 screens 1, 2 and 6 and the *Keys on every page* browser mechanic are the spec; a grinder builds it as one page PR.** *Done when*: on the Org and the Inbox, with the mouse untouched, `j` / `k` ring a card or a row, `g`+letter and `g`+digit jump between teams, `Enter` opens Focus, `Shift+Enter` pops out, `a` / `d` / `r` / `s` / `x` press the ringed thing's own buttons and do nothing where the button is absent, `1` / `2` / `n` / `/` / `?` work on every page, no key fires while a composer, the filter, a reply box, the *why?* box or the Focus terminal has focus, the `?` overlay lists the page's keys from the handler's own table, `docs/mockups/gen.py` draws the ring on a card and a row, and a test drives the table: every key in it names a control the page draws. The key map below was approved by Paul 2026-09-23 (*I like the plan for the hotkeys*). Asked 2026-09-23 (Paul: *many people do not like to have to use the mouse; would that be browser specific?*). The anchor's answer, recorded here: **not browser-specific.** A page's own key handling (`keydown` on the document) works the same in every browser; the only keys a page cannot take are the browser's and the operating system's own (a new tab, closing one, the address bar, alt-tab), and a page never wants those. The shape that works, and that people already know from mail clients and code hosts: **single keys when focus is not in a text field**, a **`?` overlay** listing them, and every key a name for a control that already exists in §4.5a — a key does nothing a button cannot. Nothing is stored; a person's own remapping is a later question. **Next:** built; what is left is the live look.
**Location:** design §4.5a (a **keys** row per page, or one row per key naming the control it presses), §4.5 screens 1, 2 and 6, `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.js` (one `keydown` handler, a focus ring on the selected card or row), `app.css` (the ring), the `?` overlay template

**Why:** every act on the Org page and the Inbox is a click, and the person who runs a fleet from the keyboard is the person the pages are for (TD-046 came from the same place: alt-tab, not the mouse).

**Fix, design first — the map is the decision:** (1) **Moving**: `j` / `k` (and the arrows) move a ring between cards on Org and rows on the Inbox, in the page's own order (the manager first, then urgency); `g` then a team's initial, or a number, jumps to a team; `/` focuses the filter box, `Esc` leaves it. (2) **Acting on the ringed thing**: `Enter` opens Focus (`o` the same; `Shift+Enter` pops it out, TD-046); on an Inbox row `a` / `d` are Allow and Deny, `r` opens Reply, `s` Snooze, `x` Dismiss or Done — each the row's own button, pressed. (3) **Pages**: `1` Org, `2` Inbox, `n` New session, `?` the overlay, which lists every key beside the control it presses and closes on `?` or `Esc`. (4) Rules: a key fires only when nothing editable has focus (a composer, the filter, a reply box) — so the Focus terminal, which takes every key, is untouched; a key that would act on something not ringed does nothing; the overlay's text is generated from the same table the handler reads, so the two cannot drift. (5) The §4.5a rows first; then one page PR builds the handler, the ring and the overlay; the mockups gain the ring. Not in scope: remapping, chords, and keys inside the terminal.

**Related:** TD-046 (Pop out, alt-tab), TD-069 (the Inbox's rows), TD-095 (the card's anatomy — the ring must fit it), TD-070 (suggested answers, which keys could pick by number).
