## TD-125: A team's wind-down report is a counted board line for the person, most of it a diagnostic and a pointer at a PR that waits on the reader, not on the person

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-23 (Paul, reading the 23:47Z line: *is all of this text just to point me to a PR?*; the anchor session)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Open — **the preset done 2026-09-23 (grinder-ao-1, PR #518): *Out of work* step (3) in `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` says the words below, and `tests/test_cli.py` holds them; what is left is telling the samscrape and dev-cadence supplements by mail, as TD-114 was.** **Designed 2026-09-23 (the designer): §4.9a *A wind-down is announced* is the spec — the report is an FYI `note` to the person inbox, two lines; a board line only for what waits on the person; a held PR never on the board (§4.9b *The reader*, §4.5a **PRs waiting**). What is left is the two briefs, and they split by hand rule.** *The grinder's half*: `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md`, step (3) of its wind-down order, becomes — *announce it: `ao msg person "<team> ran out of work at <t>. Merged this run: #a, #b (or: nothing). <member>: <what it looked for and did not find, from the `why` on its record>; <member>: …"` — a `note`, two lines, nothing else in it; then a line on `docs/user_attention.md`, `Due:` today, for each thing that waits on the person and nothing else: a question you passed up that nobody answered, a member you left open with uncommitted or unpushed work, a start or a close the host agent refused. A PR held for its reader is never on the board and never in the note: its wait is `prs_waiting` on the seat.* *Done when*: the preset says that in those words, `tests/test_cli.py` (the tests that read `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md`, near line 903) asserts the note's two lines and the board's three cases are in the preset and the sentence *one line on `docs/user_attention.md` … for each member what it looked for* is not, and the samscrape and dev-cadence supplements are told by mail the day it lands, as TD-114 was. *The repo brief's half is already done*: TD-093 slice 4 (PR #512, merged 2026-09-23) renamed `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`'s paragraph to *Held PRs*, dropped the over-two-hours board line and wrote *your rounds and your wind-down text name no PR* — nothing on a held path remains for this entry. Found 2026-09-23. Design §4.9a tells a manager that winds down to leave *one board line naming what each member searched*, and `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` adds the PRs held for the anchor's read (TD-093). The 23:47Z line was the result: two hundred words on the person's counted board, of which the only act for anyone was a PR waiting on the anchor — which is not the person, and whose wait is already a structured field (`prs_waiting`, TD-093 slice 1; its header count is slice 3, #479) — and the rest a diagnostic of why two grinders found nothing pickable, which is worth reading once by whoever writes the next entries and never worth a Due date. Three such lines landed on 2026-09-23 alone, each closed by the anchor after doing the thing it pointed at. **Next:** the preset is done (PR #518); what is left is telling samscrape and dev-cadence, the anchor's.
**Location:** design §4.9a (*Done when*: the board line), §4.10 (*answered for you*, the FYI shape), §4.5a **Inbox row: `note` and the rest of FYI**, `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` (the wind-down paragraph), `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` (*Escalation lines*, the hand rule), TD-093 (the reader)

**Why:** a counted item the person must clear teaches the person to clear without reading (the same lesson as TD-115's alarm). The board is for what waits on the person; everything else has a quieter place already.

**Fix, design first:** (1) §4.9a: the wind-down report is an **FYI `note` to the person inbox** (uncounted, Dismiss deletes; §4.10) — two lines: what was merged this run, and what each member looked for and did not find, in one sentence each — and **a board line only when something waits on the person** (a question passed up and unanswered, a member closed with unpushed work, a start refused). (2) A PR held for its reader is **never on the board**: it is `prs_waiting` on the seat (the header count, §4.5a) and the reader's own mail; the manager's brief says so and stops naming PRs in wind-down text. (3) The manager preset and this repo's supplement say the same in the same words; the samscrape and dev-cadence supplements are told the day it lands (as TD-114 was). (4) The 23:47Z line and its two siblings are the evidence; nothing to build in `src/`.

**Related:** TD-093 (the reader; `prs_waiting`), TD-118 (token spend — the report was one of its findings' cousins), TD-120 (the reader takes the hand), TD-069 (the Inbox), §4.9a.

## TD-126: A board line has Open board, Done and Snooze but no Reply: an instruction back to the sender means editing the file and waiting for a session to read it

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (Paul: *would it make sense to have a "reply" option on the inbox items so that the sending agent can interpret and make appropriate changes? … being able to reply to the sending agent with instructions (and not having to edit the board and wait for an agent to pick it up) might be more efficient*; the anchor session)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** **Designed 2026-09-24** in a cloud session with Paul (branch `claude/kind-cerf-dg50d4`; the board line that held it comes off in this PR). Design: §4.4 *Board write-back* (the `reply` edit, `board_reply`), §4.5a *Due strip / Inbox board row → Reply* and *standing*, §4.5 screen 6, §4.8a (`handed`'s second setter), §4.10 *A board reply owes an outcome too*; mockup `Inbox.dc.html` (a board row with its standing and Reply). Paul's shape stands with two corrections from the code: **the reply goes on the item's own line, not under it** — dev-cadence's reader and the SessionStart hook match one `- [ ]` line each, so a line under the item would be read by nothing, and the line keeps the item's identity and every row's line number (the designer's PR #531 chose an indented line under the item and asked the reader to join continuation lines — the same dev-cadence dependency, plus shifted line numbers; compared 2026-09-25). One fact found in that comparison: the hook clips a due line at 200 characters (`MAX_ITEM_CHARS`), so the reader is also asked to print the reply tails whole; until then the reply reaches the next session through the Inbox row, not the hook; and **point (c) was half right** — the file writer is agentorc's own `sessionorc.board.write_back` (the RPC of TD-069 step 3), the reader alone is dev-cadence's, so the reply edit is ours and what is asked of dev-cadence is three fields in the reader's JSON (`session`, `host`, `refs`), never a second parser here. Points (a), (b) and (d) are as decided: the board always, the lease holder as well, the standing before the press, the same words after it, a reply is not Done. Against #531 this design also keeps the standing (Paul's 12:30 decision, which postdates that PR), marks the note `handed` so the holder owes an outcome, matches the lease as `_lease_holder` does (declared, unexpired), and keeps the commit message's `(session <name>)` shape. Was: decided by Paul 2026-09-24 11:55 MDT, the shape for the designer's round: a board reply always goes on the board** — appended under the line as `— Paul, <date>: …`, the line still due, so the next session in that repo reads it from the SessionStart hook — **and is mailed as well only when a live session still holds the context**, which is mechanical: a session whose lease (`ao progress claim`, design §4.8) is on the reference the board line names (its `Context:` TD or PR) is still on that work and the reply is a `reply` into its inbox too; a sender that has moved on, or exited, is not written to (Paul: *will it have moved on and the reply is a distraction, or will it already have the context?* — the lease answers that). The row says what it did: *written under the line* / *written under the line · sent to grinder-ao-2 (holds TD-122)*. **And says it before the press (Paul, 2026-09-24 12:30 MDT):** a board row carries the sender's standing beside its name — *still on TD-122* (a live session holds the lease on the line's reference; the reply will reach it), *moved on* (live, no lease on it), *gone* (no such session) — so the person knows where a reply goes before writing it; the same three words are what the reply's result line repeats. Points (c) and (d) below stand: the file write is dev-cadence's script, and a reply is not Done. Was: **asked 2026-09-24.** A mail row (`ask`, `steer`) has **Reply**, which is a `reply` into the sender's inbox (§4.5a *Focus Inbox → Reply*, *Inbox row: ask*). A **board row** — a due line of `docs/user_attention.md`, read by dev-cadence's `nudge_user_attention.py` — has **Open board**, **Snooze ▾** and **Done** (§4.5a, §4.4's write-back) and no Reply, because a board line outlives its sender: its point is to hold an item after the session that wrote it is gone, and most senders are (the line names `session `x` on kmaster`, usually an exited grinder). So Paul's instruction on a board item today is an edit to the file, which the next session started in that repo reads through the SessionStart hook, or a message to a session he has to find. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-142 (ours) and one field change in dev-cadence's reader (on the board for Paul).
**Blocked by:** TD-142

**What the design round has to settle:** (a) **who a board reply reaches** — the sender when it is live (the line's session name resolves against the fleet; a `reply` carrying the board line's text and Paul's words), else the repo's team manager when the repo has a live team (an `ask` from the person, which the manager assigns or answers), else **the board itself**: the reply is appended under the line as `— Paul, <date>: …` and the line stays due, so the next session in that repo gets it from the hook, which is the edit Paul makes by hand today with one press instead; (b) **the row says which of the three it did** (*replied to grinder-ao-2* / *asked manager-ao-1* / *written under the line*), since the three mean different waits; (c) **the write-back is dev-cadence's** — a reply written into the file goes through the same script the row's Done and Snooze use (§4.4), never a second writer, so the file half is a dev-cadence change and the row half is ours; (d) **a reply is not Done**: the line stays counted until the instruction is carried out, and the session that carries it out closes the line as any board line is closed.

**Location:** design §4.5a (board row; *Focus Inbox → Reply*), §4.4 (the board's write-back), §4.10 (mail kinds), `src/agentorc/ui/templates/inbox_row.html` (the board row), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the board reader and the write-back), dev-cadence's `nudge_user_attention.py` (the file writer, SYNCED — asked for, never edited here)

**Why:** the board is where a session leaves what needs Paul, and the Inbox is where Paul acts on it; every other row's act goes back to a session with a press, and this one's goes back through a text editor.

**Related:** TD-125 (the board only for what waits on the person), TD-069 (the Inbox page), TD-120 (the designer's lane), the SessionStart hook (cadence §3).

## TD-128: A profile billed per token (an API key, a hosted open model) has no usage window, so the usage gate and the chip have nothing to read and nothing bounds its spend

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (numbered TD-126 until the rebase found main's TD-126; Paul, reading the Paperclip survey: *make a note to handle API (pay per token) budgets at some point, especially as open source models gain traction*; session `research_paperclip`)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Open — **reconciled 2026-09-25 in a cloud session with Paul (the reconciliation PR on branch `claude/gallant-faraday-ikikg5`)**: the designer's #547 merged while the session's park PR sat unmerged (memory *a park is a merged PR*), so the session's proposal was read against it piece by piece. **Paul took:** spend summed **per account**, not per profile (a key is an account; two profiles on one key each summing their own would each spend the whole amount) — the amount stays the profile's, as a reserve is; four token kinds with `prices: {input, output, cache_read, cache_write}` (cache reads folded into input would report a bill several times the real one); where the sum lives — a daily ledger per account, `spend.json`, the node's `spend` link method and the `usage` notification back; the amount as the window's 100, so a team's reserve priority (#544) means one thing on both billings; a metered window never `limited` and never polled; the Settings page's amount fields in place of the disabled placeholder. **Kept from #547:** `billing` on the profile, the amount as the reserve under `usage_gate:`, the string units, day / week / month in the home's clock, the chip's dollar form, the eight-tenths note (a `warn:` knob offered and not taken), no team or project scope. Sonnet rounds on the reconciled text are recorded below the Related line. Was: **designed 2026-09-25 (the designer, PR #547), the four points of the Fix as written:** §4.2a *How a profile is billed* (`billing: subscription | metered {input, output}`; spend summed per profile over `day` / `week` / `month` into a reading of the usage shape; a team or project budget rejected by §9 invariant 9), §4.3 *Spend per turn* (`spend(profile)` from the tool's own records, *spend unknown* said rather than nothing), §6 *Usage gate* (an amount per window — money or tokens — as the line, the same pause, one FYI note at eight tenths), §4.5a the chip's metered form, §5 `usage_gate:` amounts. Steered to Paul. Build: TD-151. Was: Open. The Settings page (TD-100 (4), 2026-09-25) reserves a budget row per metered profile under Usage, drawn disabled until this entry designs what it holds. Every profile today is a subscription login, whose binding budget is the adapter's usage windows (§4.2a, §6 *Usage gate*, TD-100). A profile on an API key (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), on a hosted open-weights model behind an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, or on a second adapter (TD-112) billed the same way reports no window: the chip draws nothing, the gate never trips, and a night's unattended run has no bound but the card on file. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-151, and this entry archives with it.
**Blocked by:** TD-151
**Location:** design §4.2a (profiles), §4.3 (the adapter's `usage()`), §6 *Usage gate*, §4.5a (the usage chip)

**Why:** open models and API billing are where a second tool is most likely to come from, and an unattended team on a metered profile is the case where a runaway costs real money rather than a window.

**Fix, design first** (as written 2026-09-24; the reconciliation of 2026-09-25 supersedes two details — prices per token kind, four not two, and the sum per account, not per profile — see Status): (1) a profile says how it is billed — `subscription` (today's windows) or `metered`, with a price per million tokens in and out where the adapter cannot report cost itself. (2) The adapter reports spend per turn where it can (Claude Code's transcript `usage` records; the endpoint's own response for others). The home sums spend per profile per window (day, week, month), in the same shape as a usage reading, so the chip and the gate read one kind of thing. (3) A reserve on a metered profile is an amount per window, with a warning line and a hard stop, the same pause the usage gate sends today. Paperclip's generic budget row (`scope, metric, window, amount, warnPercent, hardStop`; ADR 2026-09-24 item 3) is the shape to weigh, keyed on the profile and optionally the team (the team scope was rejected in the design round, 2026-09-25: §9 invariant 9). (4) A self-hosted model has no price. Its limit is throughput or GPU time, so `metered` with a zero price must still show tokens, and a reserve may be in tokens rather than money.

**Related:** TD-151 (the build), TD-100 (the usage gate), TD-122 (one reading per account), TD-112 (the second adapter), [ADR 2026-09-24](decisions/2026-09-24-paperclip.md) item 3, [ADR 2026-09-20](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md) (dollars at list price were not wanted *on a subscription*; this is the case where they are the bill).

## TD-130: The pages set 13 px body text, 12 px mail bodies and 11 px small print: small for comfortable reading

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (Paul: *make a note to check our font size — is it small for comfortable reading?*; the anchor session)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** **Designed 2026-09-25** in a cloud session with Paul (branch `claude/kind-cerf-dg50d4`; the board line that held it comes off in this PR). Design: §4.5 *Type scale* — six tokens on `:root` (`--t-body` 14, `--t-small` 12, `--t-cap` 11 for uppercase marks only, `--t-title` 16, `--t-mono` 13, `--t-mono-s` 12.5; `--lh` 1.5, `--lh-mono` 1.55), the reading text at the base size, no lower-case text under 12 px, heights in `em` so the card's six rows grow with the text; no density setting (the browser's zoom is one). Mockups regenerated at the scale, with a ladder artboard `Type.dc.html` / `TypeDark.dc.html`. A defensible default rather than a decision: Paul reads it on the live page after TD-144 lands, and the one number to move is `--t-body`. Was: **asked 2026-09-24.** `app.css` sets `body { font: 13px/1.4 "IBM Plex Sans", … }`, `.inbox .mail .body { font-size: 12px }`, `.small`, `.note`, `.btn.sm`, the chips and field labels at 11–12 px, and card names at 16 px; the sizes are literals, not tokens, in some fifty rules (`grep -c font-size: app.css` → 48). 13 px body with 11 px small print is a dense desk, and the text a person actually reads — a mail body, a board line, a question — is set smaller than the body. The round decides a type scale as tokens (a base of 14–15 px, small print no smaller than 12 px, line height 1.45–1.5, the mail body at the base size) checked in both themes at the widths the pages are used at, and whether a density setting (compact / comfortable, remembered per browser) is wanted rather than one size; the grinder then replaces the literals with the tokens. Paul reads the result on the live page: the *Done when* is his. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-144.
**Blocked by:** TD-144

**Location:** `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.css` (`body`, `.small`, `.note`, `.inbox .mail .body`, `.btn.sm`, the chips), design §4.5 (the card's anatomy, TD-095 — it names no sizes; the 16 px name on a 13 px body is `app.css`'s), `docs/mockups/gen.py` (the mockups' sizes follow)

**Why:** every page is read for minutes at a time; a size chosen for density is paid in every reading.

**Related:** TD-095 (the card's anatomy and its sizes), TD-082 (the Inbox's layout), TD-127 (what a row shows first).

## TD-131: Mail, the board and the records are JSON and Markdown files read whole: fine at today's size, and no answer to search over history, counts per filter, or a retention longer than twelve hours

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (Paul: *all this begs the question — do we need a db (with indexing) at some point?*; the anchor session)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** decision
**Status:** Open — **the anchor's answer 2026-09-24: not yet, and the trigger is written down.** What holds the data today: the person inbox is one JSON file (`~/.agentorc/person_inbox.json`, 39 entries and 46 KB on 2026-09-24), and a session's inbox is a field of its record, which is one JSON file per session under `sessions/` (`sessionorc/store.py`), both home-owned and read whole on every poll; closed entries are pruned after `MAIL_RETENTION` (12 h); the board is `docs/user_attention.md`, read by dev-cadence's script. At these sizes a read-and-filter in Python is under a millisecond and the page's filter runs in the browser over the rows it has; an index would buy nothing. **What would change the answer** — any one of: (1) mail kept past retention so a person can search history (*what did the techlead say about #517 last week*); (2) counts per filter across every team and host (TD-129's rail) on an inbox of thousands rather than dozens; (3) threads and outcomes joined across many sessions at once (mail stays home-owned across hosts — §4.4a: a node's replicas hold no mail — so this is a size question, not a placement one); (4) a second reader of the same data outside the host agent's process. **The step when one arrives is SQLite**, in the standard library, one file under `~/.agentorc/`, with FTS5 for the text search, written and read only by the host agent as the JSON is today — not a server, not a second process, and nothing a container node needs installed. The stores already sit behind one module each (`sessionorc/mail.py`, the record store in `sessionorc/agent.py`), so the change is local to them and the RPCs and the page do not move. What is not on the table: a database as the source of truth for the design's home-owned facts — those stay files a person can read and back up (`BACKUP_MEMBERS`). **Next:** a decision, and not yet: the trigger is below.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/mail.py` (the store, `MAIL_RETENTION`), `src/sessionorc/store.py` (one record file per session, its `inbox` a field), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`BACKUP_MEMBERS`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the filter's `data-find`), design §4.4 (the home), §4.4a (nodes), §4.10 (mail)

**Why:** the question is right and early; answering it now, with the trigger named, means the next person to feel the need knows what was decided and why.

**Related:** TD-129 (the rail's counts, the text search), TD-127 (what a row shows), TD-125 (retention and the FYI), TD-057 (nodes and where mail lives).

## TD-132: Build the promote — the `promote:` block, the home's policy, `ao promote`, the Inbox row

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (the designer; TD-120 step 2's design, PR #525)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done. **Slice 1 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #663):** `sessionorc/promote.py` (the block by key alone, the three readings, the preconditions, the intent files, the detached run, `survey`) and `sessionorc/agent_promote.py` (`PromoteMixin`: the pass detached from the home's tick, `promotes` on `host`, the *promoted …* `system` note), promote logs pruned with the run logs; `tests/test_promote.py`. Three choices written into §6: main's settle measured from its head's committer time, no check runs reads `unknown`, `check` every 15 s while in flight. Not yet: a `promotes` event on the stream (slice 3 draws the row from `host`; add the event there if the page needs it live). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #666):** `promote` and `clear_promote` (person-only, `modes.HOME_EDITS`), `ao promote [<repo>]` and `ao promote status`; the techlead's (b) from #663 — the run's process start time in the intent, `alive` before the bound, a reused pid never killed. `--sha` is refused: how a rollback's commit reaches `run` (which installs the tree precondition (1) holds at main's head) is a design question — the designer's, not built. **Open from #663:** (a) whether `auto` may act on a live it never read — Paul's call on the board (default: wait for a reading, the press goes through), the answer into §6. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #668):** the Inbox row (`promote_rows`, Promote, Snooze ▾ by time, Dismiss on a failure), and the techlead's two from #666 — the press files the notes its own reading concludes, and `--sha`'s narrowing is on the board beside (a). **Slice 4 built 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1, PR #736):** this repo's `.agentorc.yml` with `promote:` alone (`run` CLAUDE.md's pair with the checkout's path from `pwd -P`, `check` the live venv's `build.info()` commit or exit 1 *build unknown*), and (a) as Paul answered it: `auto` acts only on a live it has read (`survey`), written into §6. **Slice 5 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #743):** TD-062's last two lines — the Org top bar's **build** chip (§4.5a, drawn only when the build is not main's head, main's distance from the home's `promotes` reading so it agrees with the row) and `ao team start` ending with the build line (§4.7); TD-062 archived with it. **Left:** the live half of *Done when* — (1)–(3) and (5) on the live copy, on the board (slice 4's line, due 2026-10-01) — then archive this entry and TD-120. Design: §5 `promote:`, §6 *Promote*, §4.5a *Inbox row: promote*, §4.7 `ao promote`, §4.4 *What is running says which commit it is* (the last sentence), glossary *promote*. **Next:** slices 1–5 are built; what is left is the live half of *Done when*, on the board.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/promote.py` (new: the block reader — `promote:` alone, from each checkout in the registry — the three readings, the intent files under `~/.agentorc/promotes/<repo>/`, the constants), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the tick's pass, the `promote` RPC, `promotes` on `host`, the `system` note), `src/sessionorc/modes.py` (a home-owned write: the RPC goes in `HOME_EDITS` and its siblings — the file's own comment names the two RPCs that were once left out), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao promote`, `ao promote status`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` and the Inbox templates (the row), this repo's `.agentorc.yml` (new file: the block alone), `tests/`.

**Why:** the anchor is the only thing that makes a merge live, and it is going away (TD-120). Every night's merges wait on a person's two commands; the design makes that a press on the Inbox or a policy of the tick, and generalizes it to any repo (Paul 2026-09-23: *these design items have to be generalized for any repo*).

**Fix:** four slices, each its own PR, in this order — the first two touch `src/sessionorc/**`, so the techlead reads them (§4.9b *The reader*).
1. **The readings and the policy** (`sessionorc.promote`, the tick): the block read from each registry checkout's `.agentorc.yml` by key alone (a missing or malformed block is logged and skipped, never fatal to the tick); `live` from `check` (cwd the checkout; a failed read keeps the last good one with `why`), `main` after the home's own `git fetch origin main`, `checks` from `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{sha}/check-runs` (`green` / `pending` / `failed` / `unknown` with why), each at the reports' five-minute cadence, detached from the tick; `promotes: {<repo>: {live, main, ahead, checks, auto, inflight, failed, at}}` on the `host` RPC; the three preconditions as one function that returns the first unmet one's name and text; `auto: true` acting after `PROMOTE_SETTLE` (10 min) with main still; the run detached (`start_new_session=True`, output to `~/.agentorc/promotes/<repo>/<sha>.log`), `inflight.json` written **before** the start; the outcome from `check` on later ticks — done clears the file and files the `system` note *promoted `<repo>` `<sha>` — n commits* to the person inbox; gone or past `PROMOTE_BOUND` (20 min, killed) → `failed.json` and no further promote of that repo until it is cleared. A promote of *this* repo restarts the agent mid-pass: the test is that a fresh agent finding `inflight.json` with `check` reading the wanted commit files the note and clears the file.
2. **The RPC and the CLI**: `promote {repo, sha}` — person-only, refused to a session with the same words as `set_settings`; refused, naming it, on preconditions (1) and (3), going on through (2) with the verdict in the reply; returns `{started, log}`; `clear_promote {repo}` (Dismiss's half); `ao promote [<repo>] [--sha]`, `ao promote status` and `--json`. `ao --skill` says a session never promotes (it says it already under *Never*).
3. **The Inbox row** (§4.5a, the row's text): drawn from `promotes` alone; *Needs you* and counted under `auto: false` when ahead, and on a failure; FYI while in flight; nothing but a failure under `auto: true`; **Promote**, **Snooze ▾** (the attention store, by time), **Dismiss** on a failure row only (`clear_promote`); refusals drawn in place. Regenerate nothing: the mockup already carries the row (`docs/mockups/gen.py`, PR #525).
4. **This repo's block**: `.agentorc.yml` with `promote:` alone — `run` and `check` only, since 2026-09-25: **`auto` lives in `settings.yml` (`repos.<repo>.promote.auto`, TD-146) and slice 1 reads it there**, the Settings page's switch and `ao settings` setting it — `run` the pip pair of CLAUDE.md (`~/.local/share/agentorc-venv/bin/pip install --upgrade '<checkout>[ui]' && ~/.local/share/agentorc-venv/bin/ao service install`, the checkout's path read from the cwd, never hard-coded), `check` the venv's python printing `sessionorc.build.info()["commit"]` and exiting 1 with *build unknown* when there is none, `auto: false` — then a live check on the board: main ahead → the row; the press → the note; Paul flips `auto` when he trusts it. The other repos' blocks are their own sessions' (samscrape: skaffold and the image tag; contractmatch: wrangler; dev-cadence: its sync). Fold in TD-062's last two lines: the top bar's build line, and `ao team start` printing it.

**Done when** (1) with `auto: false`, a merge to `main` draws the row on the Inbox within five minutes, **Promote** on it makes the merge live and the FYI note names the commit, and `ao promote status` agrees with `ao status -v`'s build line; (2) with `auto: true`, three merges inside ten minutes produce one promote, ten minutes after the last; (3) a `run` that exits non-zero, or hangs past twenty minutes, is a counted row with the log's tail, and a second merge promotes nothing until Dismiss; (4) `ao promote` from inside any session is refused; (5) a promote of this repo, which restarts the host agent under its own policy, still ends in the note and a cleared intent file; (6) a repo without the block has no row and `ao promote` there says so; (7) TD-062 and TD-120 archived.

**Related:** TD-120 (the design), TD-062 (the class of failure this closes), TD-093 (the techlead reads the `src/sessionorc` slices), design §4.4a (the wheel and the nodes follow a promote as today).

## TD-133: Build the team start at the reset — `schedules:` in `settings.yml`, the tick's replay, `ao schedule`, the card's *starts* note

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (the designer; TD-026's design round, PR #526)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Open — designed, nothing built. Design: §6 *Schedule*, §5 `settings.yml` (`teams.<team>.schedule` since 2026-09-25 — the key moved under the team with the Settings page's design, TD-100 (4); the page's Teams card sets it once built), §4.7 `ao schedule`, §4.5a *team card: starts note*, glossary *schedule*. **Next:** Paul's word first: *not vital, not scheduled* (2026-09-22); ask m-32d769985798 offers to schedule it.
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — ask m-32d769985798 (not vital, not scheduled, 2026-09-22)

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (the second key and its validation), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the tick's pass beside `_keep_running`: the reset instant from the account's usage reading, the replay with `why: schedule`, `set_settings` taking the key), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao schedule`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` and the team card template (the *starts* note), `tests/`.

**Why:** the ao-grind team winds down when its lane empties and is started again by a person's hand the next day, and the designer now refills the lane overnight; a start at the weekly reset closes that loop without the host agent reading `org.yml` or inventing a team. Paul decided the shape on 2026-09-22 and called it not vital; this entry is the build, waiting for his word.

**Fix:** three slices, the first two in `src/sessionorc/**` (the techlead reads them, §4.9b).
1. **The key and the pass**: `schedules: {<team>: {start: reset, profile, window}}` parsed with the reserves (a bad entry logged and skipped, never fatal); each tick, for each rule, the instant is the named window's `resets` in that profile's account reading (§4.2a; no reading, no fire); when the instant passes — once per reset, remembered on the home as `schedules_fired: {<team>: <resets>}` so a restart of the agent does not fire twice — every record badged with the team that is `exited`/`closed` by the team's own ending (`out_of_work` set, or a manager's own close after a wind-down), not suspended, not at a ceiling, is replayed from its launch record as rule 2 replays, `why: schedule`, under `RESTART_CEILING`; a team with anything live is skipped; no launch records → nothing, logged once. `set_settings` accepts the key; a session's call is refused as today.
2. **`ao schedule`**: the listing with the next instant, `<team> reset --profile --window`, `<team> off`; the client refuses an undefined team (org.yml and the repos' `teams:`) and a label no adapter of the profile reports.
3. **The card**: the *starts* note on a team card with nothing live, from the rule and the reading, by the *stops* formatter.

**Done when** ao-grind, wound down on a Wednesday night with `ao schedule ao-grind reset --profile grind --window week` set, is running again within one tick of Thursday's reset with the manager, both grinders, the designer and the seats as they were last started, each card's `restarts` carrying `why: schedule`; the same team live at the reset is untouched; the team card read *wound down 6 h · starts at the reset · Thu 07:00* before and shows the sessions after; a session's `ao schedule … reset` is refused; TD-026 is closed or re-scoped by Paul's answer.

**Related:** TD-026 (the design and the open scope question), TD-103 (the launch record and the replay this reuses), TD-100 (`settings.yml`, the settings page that would also set this), TD-122 (the account's `resets`), TD-101 (the override shape that stays not wanted).

## TD-142: Build Reply on a board row — the `reply` edit, `board_reply`, the standing, the `handed` note to the lease holder

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-24 (TD-126's design, a cloud session with Paul)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-25 (grinder-ao-1, PR #581)**: `board.write_back` with `reply`, `rpc_board_reply` (person-only, `sent: []` and the note), the row's **Reply** through the mail composer, `/api/person/board` `action: reply`; signed with the checkout's git `user.name`. Slice 1's *trail* line is left to slice 2: the trail is keyed on a session and a board line names none until the reader carries `session`. Slice 2 (the mail half and the standing) waits on dev-cadence's reader. Design: §4.4 *Board write-back*, §4.5a *Due strip / Inbox board row → Reply* and *Inbox board row: standing*, §4.8a (`handed`), §4.10 *A board reply owes an outcome too*, mockup `Inbox.dc.html`.
**Blocked by:** decision (dev-cadence) — slice 2 waits on dev-cadence's reader carrying `session`, `host` and `refs` (asked on the board 2026-09-24)

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/board.py` (`ACTIONS` gains `reply`; `edit_line` appends ` — Paul, <date>: <text>` to the item's line, the date local as the board's are; `message` → `agentorc: reply on <head> (session <name>)`), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`rpc_board_reply {board, line, text, reply, refs}`: person-only as `board_edit`; `refs` handed in by the page from the reader's JSON, since the agent does not run the reader; the write-back first, then for each live record holding an unexpired declared `claimed` on one of the item's `refs` — the `_lease_holder` walk, over all records — a `note` from the person with `about` the ref, `handed=True`, text *board: <head>\n\nPaul: <reply>*; the reply says `{commit, sent: [{session, ref}]}` and a mail failure after the commit is in it as words), `src/sessionorc/modes.py` (a home-owned write, beside `board_edit`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (`board_rows` carries `session`, `host`, `refs` from the reader's JSON when present and computes `standing` from the records: `{word, holder, ref}`; the route for the press; the trail line), `src/agentorc/ui/templates/inbox_row.html` (the standing beside the sender, **Reply** in the foot, the dialog shared with Reply and Message with the head quoted), `app.js`, `tests/test_board.py`, `tests/test_mail.py` (the `handed` note owes an outcome).

**Why:** every other row's act goes back to a session with a press; a board line's goes back through a text editor and a wait for the next session, and Paul asked for the press (TD-126).

**Fix:** two slices; both touch `src/sessionorc/**`, so the techlead reads them (§4.9b).
1. **The file half and the row**, landable now: the `reply` edit and `board_reply` writing on the board and committing; the row's **Reply** and its dialog; the result and trail *written on the board*; no mail, no standing yet. `board_reply` refuses as `board_edit` does (the line must still hold the text; the checkout must take the commit).
2. **The mail half and the standing**, after dev-cadence's reader carries `session`, `host` and `refs` per item and prints a line's ` — Paul, <date>:` tails whole after the head it clips at 200 characters (`MAX_ITEM_CHARS`) in the SessionStart line — both asked on the board 2026-09-24; until the second, the reply reaches the next session through the Inbox row only: the `handed` note to each live lease holder; the standing word from the records — *still on <ref> — <session> holds it* when a live record holds an unexpired declared lease on a ref, *moved on* when a live record carries the item's `session` name and none holds a lease, *gone* otherwise; the result and trail *written on the board · sent to <session> (holds <ref>)*. Until then the row draws no standing and Reply's result says *written on the board — no session standing is known: the board reader carries no session fields yet*.

**Done when** (1) Reply on a due row appends ` — Paul, <date>: …` to that line in the repo's main checkout, one commit with the fixed message, the row shows the appended text on the next read, and the line is still counted; (2) with grinder-ao-2 live and holding `ao progress claim TD-122`, the row for a line whose `Context:` names TD-122 reads *still on TD-122 — grinder-ao-2 holds it*, Reply lands a `handed` note in that session's inbox, `ao inbox` there prints it, and until it reports `--outcome` the entry sits under *Waiting on them*; (3) the same line with no holder reads *moved on* or *gone* and Reply mails nobody; (4) a session calling `board_reply` is refused; (5) `pdm run test` covers the edit, the message, the holder walk and the `handed` debt; (6) TD-126 is marked built.

**Related:** TD-126 (the design), TD-069 step 3 (`board_edit`, the pattern), TD-077 b (`handed`), TD-079 (outcomes), TD-056 (the lease).

## TD-144: Build the type scale — six tokens on `:root`, every size literal in `app.css` replaced, heights in `em`

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (TD-130's design, a cloud session with Paul)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** **Built 2026-09-25** (PR #559, `grinder-ao-2`): the tokens, every literal replaced, the heights, `tests/test_css.py`; three calls against the map are in design-history §4.5 (`.toast` and `.keys` small, `.warn` body, the caption one `--lh` line). What is left is the *Done when*'s last clause — Paul's read of the live page after the promote (board line); then this and TD-130 archive. Was: open — designed, nothing built. Design: §4.5 *Type scale*; mockups `Type.dc.html`, `TypeDark.dc.html` and the regenerated artboards (`docs/mockups/gen.py` already carries the scale). **Next:** built; Paul's live look.

**Location:** `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.css` (the token block on `:root`, every `font-size:` and `font:` literal, the card's row heights, the control heights), `src/agentorc/ui/templates/*.html` (any inline `font-size`), `tests/test_screen.py` or a new `tests/test_css.py` (no pixel size outside the token block).

**Why:** every page is read for minutes at a time, and today the text a person actually reads is set smaller than the body; a size chosen for density is paid in every reading (TD-130).

**Fix:** one slice, all in `src/agentorc/ui/**` (no techlead hold).
1. Tokens on `:root`, beside the colours: `--t-body: 14px; --t-small: 12px; --t-cap: 11px; --t-title: 16px; --t-mono: 13px; --t-mono-s: 12.5px; --t-btn: 13px; --lh: 1.5; --lh-mono: 1.55;` and the heights `--row: 22px; --row1: 26px; --slot: 72px; --foot: 26px` (or the same in `em` of `--t-body`).
2. The map, literal → token, for `app.css` as it stands (48 `font-size:` rules and the `body` `font:`): `body` 13/1.4 → `--t-body`/`--lh`; `.inbox .mail .body` 12, `.inboxpage .mailrow .body` 12.5, `.note` 12, `.toast` 12, `.kv` 12, `.checks` 12, `.inline-banner` 12, `.alarms` 12, `.keys` 12.5, `.input` 13, `.composer textarea`, `.maildlg` text → `--t-body`; `.small` 11, `#hostchip` 11, `#usagechip` 11, `.flag` 11, `.mode` 11, `input.denywhy` 11, `kbd` 11, `.btn.sm` 11, `.term.tail` 11 → `--t-small`; `.btn` 12 → `--t-btn`; `.pill` 10, `.badge` 10, `.field label` 11, `.isec` summary marks, the *new* mark 9, the kind labels → `--t-cap` (the pill glyphs' `::before` tunings become `em` of the pill's size, keeping their relative look); `.h1` 16, `.ghead .h2` 16, `.sc .name` 16 → `--t-title`; `.wordmark` 15, `.focus .title` 15 → `--t-title` at weight 500 (one size fewer); `.term` 12/1.5 → `--t-mono`/`--lh-mono`; `.meta` 11.5, `.status` 11.5, `.reports .rep .ref` 11.5, `.inbox .mail .ref` 11.5, `.inboxpage .mailrow .ref` 11.5, `.ghead .name` 13, the `.st` rules 10.5, `.reports .rep .st` 10.5 → `--t-mono-s`. Anything not in the map is `--t-small` if lower case and `--t-cap` if uppercase-tracked.
3. Heights: `.sc-body > .row` 20 → `--row`, `.r1` 24 → `--row1`, `.sc-slot` 66 → `--slot`, `.sc-foot` 24 → `--foot`, `.sc-slot > .caption` 16 → `1.15em`; `.btn` 28 → 30, `.btn.sm` 24 → 26, `.input` 32 → 34, `input.denywhy` 24 → 26; the top bar stays 48. The `.sc-slot > .status` two-line clamp is already `em`.
4. A test that reads `app.css` and fails on any `font-size:` or `font:` value in `px` outside the `:root` block, so the literals do not creep back.

**Done when** the pages read at the new scale in both themes with no rule outside `:root` naming a pixel size; every Org card is still one height and six rows at 1440 and at 390 px; the Inbox column, the rail and the Focus page fit as before; `pdm run test` and `pdm run lint` pass; and **Paul has read the live page after the promote and said the size is right** — if not, `--t-body` moves and nothing else does. TD-130 archives with his word.

**Related:** TD-130 (the design), TD-095 (the card's anatomy: six rows of one height), TD-082 (the Inbox column), TD-137 (the narrow layout, which uses the same tokens), TD-127 (the fold, whose lead is `--t-body`).

## TD-146: Build the one settings file — `settings.yml` home-owned with `teams`, `repos` and `person`, `ui.yml` retired, the `settings` read, the team stop time and reserve priority in the tick, `ao team until`

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (TD-100 (4)'s design, a cloud session with Paul; ADR `docs/decisions/2026-09-25-settings-audit.md`)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** **Slice 1 built 2026-09-26 (grinder-ao-1)**: the sessionorc half, PR #626 (merged) — the four keys' readers and parsers, `set_settings` with any subset, the `settings` read, the gate's and the restart policies' team priority with `gated.team_extra` and the card's words, `settings.yml` in the backup — and the UI half, PR #629 — `open_in` from `person:` through the `settings` read, `ui.yml` retired and named as *migrate* on the Org. **Slice 2 built 2026-09-26 (grinder-ao-1, PR #630)**: the tick's `_team_stop_times` at the home (a node's member through `set_stop` over the link), the create's stamp while the instant is ahead, and Clear or a moved instant taking the members that carry it along. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-26 (grinder-ao-1, PR #631)**: `ao team until`, `ao team reserve`, `ao settings [--where]`; `ao schedule`'s key path waits for TD-133, which builds `ao schedule`. What is left is the *Done when* on the live copy after a promote. Design: §5 *The settings a person moves*, §6 *Usage gate* (the team's reserve priority), §6 *Team stop time*, §6 *Schedule* (the key path), §6 *Promote* (`auto`), §4.7 `ao team until`, `ao team reserve`, `ao settings`, glossary *setting* / *definition*. **Next:** built; what is left is the live look after a promote.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (the four keys; `reserves()` unchanged; `teams()`, `repos()`, `person()` readers; validation of each key's shape; a `ui.yml` on disk read once and reported as *migrate*), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`rpc_set_settings` grows `teams`, `repos`, `person` beside `reserves`, each validated — a team the caller names is checked by the client, the agent takes the key; a new person-only `settings` read returning the file and, per key, what it makes today; `_enforce_usage_gate` adds the team's reserve for a record carrying the badge and marks `gated.team_extra`; a new tick pass for `teams.<team>.until` giving live badge-carriers the instant as `set_stop` does and stamping a start's creates; `BACKUP_MEMBERS` gains `settings.yml`; `sessionorc.promote` (TD-132) reads `repos.<repo>.promote.auto` here), `src/agentorc/ui/uiconf.py` (reads `person.open_in` through the `settings` read, falling back to the default; the file-path form for Open file), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao team until`, `ao team reserve`, `ao settings [--where]`; `ao schedule` writes `teams.<team>.schedule`), `tests/`.

**Why:** the settings a person moves were split across two files with two write paths, and the reserves — the one Paul moves weekly — could not be set for a team or from a page; this entry makes one file the page can write.

**Fix:** three slices; the first two touch `src/sessionorc/**`, so the techlead reads them (§4.9b).
1. **The file and the RPCs**: the keys and readers; `set_settings {profile?, reserves?, teams?, repos?, person?}` — any subset, each validated, the whole file rewritten; the `settings` read; `ui.yml` retired with the migrate note; the backup member. The gate's team priority and `gated.team_extra`; the *paused · usage (team +n)* words on the card (§4.5a) ride the existing mark.
2. **The team stop time**: the tick pass and the start's stamping; `Clear`; the past-instant refusal shared with `set_stop`.
3. **The CLI**: `ao team until`, `ao team reserve`, `ao settings`, `ao schedule`'s new key path (TD-133's slice reads it there).

**Done when** (1) `ao gate grind 5h=30` and `ao team reserve ao-grind 10` leave `settings.yml` with both keys and the gate pauses an ao-grind session at 60% and a plain grind session at 70%, each card saying which line; (2) `ao team until ao-grind +2h` gives every live ao-grind member and seat that `run_until` within a tick, a member started after it carries it, and `--clear` removes it without touching a member's own earlier `ao until`; (3) `person.open_in: none` removes the editor button and a `ui.yml` left on disk is named as *migrate* and ignored; (4) `ao settings` prints the four keys with their lines and instants and `--where` names every other file and its re-read rule; (5) a session's `set_settings` and `settings` are refused; (6) the nightly backup carries `settings.yml`; (7) `pdm run test` covers each key's validation, the team line, the team stop and the migrate path.

**Related:** TD-100 (the design), TD-147 (the replica), TD-148 (the page), TD-133 (the schedule reads its new key), TD-132 (`auto` read from here), TD-026 (the team stop time answers one of its shapes), TD-095 (`open_in`).

## TD-148: Build the Settings page — screen 8, its sections and controls, the *i* marks, Open file, the terminal face and size, the tab

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (TD-100 (4)'s design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** **Built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #684):** `/settings` (`ui/settings_page.py` builds the sections from the agent's `settings`, `usage` and `host` reads and the clients' own files; `templates/settings.html`), its writes `/api/settings/usage|teams|repos|you` through `set_settings` (a reserve in `ao gate`'s forms, a stop time in `ao team until`'s, an undefined team refused naming the defined ones, an `open_in` template refused in `uiconf`'s words before it is written), the line preview (`AO.reserveLine`), the person's terminal face and size on every page's terminals (`data-term-*` on `<body>`, `AO.setTermLook`, a `BroadcastChannel` for the open ones), **Reset this browser**, the tab; `tests/test_ui_settings.py`. Left as they are: the metered card is drawn without fields (TD-151 slice 5 builds them); the page's write routes call `set_settings` as the person, as every UI route does, so *a session's request to the page's write route is refused* holds only where the session reaches the agent itself — the UI cannot tell a session's HTTP request from a person's (the same holds for the Inbox's routes); at 390 px the top bar, one tab longer, runs past the screen's edge as its buttons already did. What is left is the *Done when* on the live copy. Design: §4.5 screen 8, §4.5a *Settings page* rows and the top bar's **Settings** tab, §2 goal 12 (the terminal's face and size), mockups `Settings.dc.html` and `SettingsDark.dc.html`. **Next:** built; what is left is the live look after a promote.

**Location:** `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the `/settings` route: the `settings` and `gate` reads, the org's definitions, the registry's repos and each `.agentorc.yml`, `hosts.yml` and `profiles.yml` as the clients read them, with each file's path and re-read rule for the *i* marks; the `set_settings` calls per card; refusals in place), a new template `settings.html` (one centred column, TD-082's rules; the seven sections; the *i* mark macro the Inbox already has; the disabled rows for TD-133 and TD-128), `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.js` (the reserve field's line preview from the gate's arithmetic; the terminal size and face applied to every open terminal through `AO.termFont`'s refit; **Reset this browser**; the tab), `app.css`, `base.html` (the tab), `tests/test_ui.py`.

**Why:** every setting a person moves is a file edit or a CLI call today, and the reserves are the one Paul moves weekly.

**Fix:** one slice in `src/agentorc/ui/**` (no techlead hold). The route and the template; the Usage cards with the line preview and **Save**; the Teams cards (schedule disabled, until with the CLI's forms and Clear, reserve priority); the Repos cards with the `auto` switch and the read-only values; the You section in its two halves (its **copy on select** switch writes through `POST /api/settings/person`, the route the Focus toggle already uses — TD-174); Hosts, Profiles, Org read-only; the *i* marks with path, re-read rule and *restart the host agent to apply* where the field is start-only; **Open file** with the file-path form; the tab, last after Inbox. On a node: *set at <home>* beside each editable value, from the agent's answer to the `settings` read.

**Done when** Paul sets his weekly reserves from the page and the chip's line follows on the next tick; a team's stop time set on the page shows as the members' *stops* note; the promote switch flips `repos.<repo>.promote.auto`; the terminal's size changes on an open Focus without a reload and ligatures stay off; every read-only value shows its file on the *i* mark and Open file opens it; Reset this browser clears the `ao.*` keys after a confirm; a session's request to the page's write route is refused; the page reads at the type scale of TD-144 in both themes at 1440 and 390 px.

**Related:** TD-100, TD-146, TD-147, TD-144 (the type scale), TD-082 (the column), TD-123 (the tab rule), TD-128 (the budget row), TD-133 (the schedule row).

## TD-151: Build metered profiles — `billing` on the profile, `spend()` in the adapter, the summed reading, the amount reserve, the chip

**Priority:** Low
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (the designer; TD-128's design round, PR #547)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done. **Next:** built (PRs #670, #674, #681, #688, #689, #690); what is left is the live look and the turns' rate on the chip's hover. **Slice 1 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #670):** `billing` (`subscription` default, `metered`) and `prices` by kind on the profile, validated in `profiles.load`; the label says *metered*; the Claude Code adapter's `usage_for` answers `{reason: metered}` for a metered profile without polling (the home's own read of `billing` before the cap rule is slice 3's). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #674):** Claude Code's `spend(profile, cursors)` → `{turns, cursors, reason}`, one turn per API response within a read (`message.id`), the contract's comment in `sessionorc/adapters.py`, and `HookFedStub.spend` for the home's tests. A metered profile must name a `config_dir` of its own (the review of #674). **Slice 3 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #681):** `sessionorc/spend.py` (the ledger `spend.json`, the three windows, the reading with `pct` from the profile's amount) and `sessionorc/agent_spend.py` (the home's pass every tick, the eight-tenths note, the gate's line at the amount); `settings.amounts`/`parse_amount`; the adapter's `billing_for` and `response` on the turn; `ao status -v`'s `spend:` line; `tests/test_spend.py`. The techlead's three from #674 are answered: (1) the turn carries `response`; (2) checked on kmaster's transcripts, `claude --resume` appends to the same file, so the dedup stays per transcript; (3) both limits are closed by the ledger (the response's tokens beside the cursor, and the `at` check on a batch read from the cursor). Not in slice 3: the ledger at the home only (a node's `spend` is slice 4), `spend.json` not among `BACKUP_MEMBERS`, and `set_settings`/`ao gate` still refuse an amount (slice 5), so one is written by hand. Open from the review of #681: two metered profiles on one account that share a directory and declare different `prices` — the first by name prices the shared transcripts' turns; the design could require one account's profiles to agree (`profiles.yml`'s check). **Was, for slice 3:** a response whose entries straddle two reads is counted twice — the ledger should key a turn by its response too; and a rewrite that leaves the file at or past its cursor is not seen as one (only a cursor past the end is), so the ledger's `at`/`id` check beside the cursor is what must catch it — the design's §4.4 sentence covers the shrink only. **Slice 4 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #688):** the node's pass sends `spend {account, profile, prices, turns, cursors}` (`SpendMixin._spend_to_home`), its first on each link a read; the home's `_take_spend` ingests under that host's cursors and answers cursors, seeding and the account's sums; `_push_spend_usage` sends `usage {account, sums}` when a named profile's `pct` or a reset moves; `spend.pieces` and `spend.figure` (the offline figure); §4.4 and §4.4a say so, the command-kind limit included. Not in slice 4: the home's top bar does not draw an account only a node's sessions run under (each host's bar is its own, as a polled reading is). **Slice 5:** the line above **Related:** below. Was, for slice 4: three build facts, read 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1): (i) **a link frame is bounded** (`link.FRAME_LIMIT`), and a node's first `spend` read with no cursors returns every turn of every transcript, so one frame of them would close the link on every dial. The seeding must not travel as turns: the node first asks the home for the cursors it holds for this host and whether the profile is seeded there (a `spend` with no turns is that read), strips the turns while it is not seeded (the home's `ingest` then only takes the cursors, which are the ends), and sends a long batch in pieces cut at a turn's `offset`, which is its line's start, so the next read resumes there and the straddle rule absorbs a response cut across two pieces. (ii) **A resent batch is safe as `ingest` stands**: its turns lie before the cursor the lost reply moved, so `_fresh` takes it for a rewrite and drops through the ledgered `at`/`id`; a test should hold that. (iii) **`pct` is the profile's, not the account's**: `spend.reading` divides by the profile's amount, so the `usage {account, reading}` frame carries the account's window sums (`spend.sums`), and the node makes each profile's reading from its replica's amounts, as the home does; §4.4a's sentence should say *the account's sums*. For slice 4's PR, from the techlead's read of #681: write on this entry that a metered profile whose only live session is command-kind is not read while that is so (the spend pass and the poll share `_usage_live`); its turns are counted when an interactive session on the account is next live, on the day each turn carries, so nothing is lost and the gate is late by that much. Slice 3's notes as filed: (1) `spend` pops the turn's response id (`message`) before returning it, so the home cannot key by response — carry it on the turn and say so in the contract's comment; (2) check on a real resumed session whether `claude --resume` writes the earlier conversation's assistant entries into the new transcript — if it does, the ledger must key by response **across** transcripts, not within one (not yet verified either way); (3) the two limits above stand. Also: `profiles.yml`'s metered-directory refusal stops every `ao` call that reads the profiles (starting a session or a team, the adapter's reads, the UI) on a host whose file has such a profile (none today). Was: Open — designed (the designer, #547) and **reconciled 2026-09-25** (a cloud session with Paul; TD-128's Status says what changed), nothing built; no metered profile runs today, so the build is proven on the test adapter and on a Claude Code profile with an API key when Paul declares one. Design: §4.2a *How a profile is billed*, §4.3 *Spend per turn*, §4.4 *Usage* (the ledger, the node's road), §4.4a *Settings, replicated* (one sentence), §6 *Usage gate* (the amount reserve), §4.5 screen 8 and §4.5a *Settings page: Usage* (the metered card), §4.5a *usage chip*, §5 `usage_gate:`, glossary *metered*.
**Blocked by:** decision (designer) — what the turns' rate on the chip's hover is a rate of (the Status's *Left*; grinder-ao-2 claimed and dropped the hover on that, 2026-10-01, then built each profile's amount, which the design states whole); then the live look

**Location:** `src/agentorc/profiles.py` and `~/.agentorc/profiles.yml` (`billing`, `prices` by token kind with the cache note, validation), `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/` (`spend(profile, cursors)` from every transcript under the profile's config directory past its cursor, each turn naming its `id`, `source` and `offset`, a shrunk file read from 0 again, four token kinds, subagent turns included; the usage poll skipped for a metered profile), `src/sessionorc/adapters.py` (the contract's comment: `spend`, `Turn`; `src/agentorc/adapters/__init__.py` is the empty entry-point package), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the daily ledger `spend.json` per account, the sum per account per window into `Usage(windows=[…])` with `pct` from the profile's amount and `resets` in the home's zone with its offset, the cursors per host per transcript starting at each transcript's end on the first tick that finds the profile metered, a turn at or before its transcript's cursor dropped, the last ledgered entry's `at` and `id` kept beside the cursor so a rewritten file re-bills nothing and loses nothing on a shared timestamp, every entry at that `at` dropped when the `id` is gone, a transcript first seen later starting at 0, `cost` at that tick's prices and never rewritten, the pause at the line, the note at eight tenths, `next` at the boundary, the `usage` event; the `spend` link method from a node and the `usage` notification to it, the node's offline add), `src/sessionorc/link.py` (nothing new: a method with an `id` gets a reply), `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (an amount reserve: `$n`, `n tok`, `nM tok`; refused against a subscription profile and a percent against a metered one; the team priority as a percent of the amount), the `limited` rule (`_refresh_usage_inner` and `_cap`: skipped for a profile whose `billing` is `metered`, read before the windows), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao gate` takes the same values; `ao status -v` prints spend), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` and the top bar (the chip's metered form, *spend unknown*, amber from eight tenths), the Settings page's metered card (with TD-148), `tests/`.

**Why:** an unattended team on an API key or a hosted open model has no window to trip the gate, so a night's run is bounded by nothing but the card on file (TD-128); open models and API billing are where a second tool is most likely to come from.

**Fix:** five slices; the ones in `src/sessionorc/**` are read by the techlead (§4.9b). (1) The profile: `billing` parsed and validated, `metered` with optional `prices` by kind, the cache note in the file's comment; `ao roles` / the profile line say *metered*; the usage poll never asked for a metered profile. (2) The adapter: `spend(profile, cursors)` for Claude Code — every transcript under the config directory past its cursor, a byte offset per file returned with the turns, each turn carrying its `id` (the entry's `uuid`), `source` and `offset`, a cursor past the file's end (a rewrite) read from 0, `usage.input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `message.model` per `assistant` entry, subagent entries included; `reason` when the directory is unreadable; a test adapter that reports turns. (3) The home: the daily ledger `spend.json` per account (a row per day: tokens by kind, cost; the cursors per host per transcript with the last ledgered entry's `at` and `id`, moved after the write, a turn at or before its transcript's cursor dropped, and after a rewrite every entry before that `at` and, at it, those up to and including that `id` in file order, or all of them when none carries it; thirteen months kept), the sum per account per window into the usage shape with `resets` at the boundary and `pct` from the profile's amount, the gate's pause at `100 − team priority` and resume at the roll, the eight-tenths note once per window, `limited` never from it, `ao status -v`'s line. (4) The node: `spend {account, turns}` with a reply carrying the home's cursors for that host's transcripts, a turn at or before its transcript's cursor dropped; the home's account set per node from its `spend` calls; `usage {account, reading}` back on `hello`, after a node's first `spend` for an account, on a whole-point move and on a roll; the node's gate on the sent reading, its own turns added offline for the gate and the chip alone, never written. (5) The chip, `ao gate`, `settings.yml`'s amounts, the Settings page's metered card.

**Done when** a profile declared `metered` with prices and a `{day: "$5"}` reserve, on the test adapter emitting turns, shows *day $4.10 / $5* on the chip, files the FYI note at $4, pauses its unattended sessions at $5 with the gate's pause prompt and resumes them at midnight; two profiles on one account see one spend and pause at their own amounts; a team with a reserve priority of 10 on that profile pauses at $4.50; a restart of the host agent keeps the day's spend; a node's `spend` batch resent after a lost reply changes no row, and a late turn from a second transcript on the same account with an earlier `at` is still counted; a transcript compacted mid-day bills its new turns and none of its old ones twice, two entries sharing a timestamp across the cursor included, and a compaction that removed the last ledgered entry bills nothing at its timestamp again; a turn of a thousand cache-read tokens costs a tenth of a thousand input tokens at the example prices; a priceless metered profile shows tokens; a percent reserve on it is refused by `ao gate` naming the billing; an interactive session on the profile never shows `limited`; a subscription profile is untouched by all of it; TD-128 is archived.

**Slice 5 built 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1, PR #689):** `set_settings` takes a metered profile's amounts (`_amounts_change`: the home's three windows, a percent refused naming the billing, money refused on a priceless profile, the billing asked of every adapter before the profile's first session) and `gate` answers them with the spend; `ao gate` sets and prints them; the Settings page's metered card has its three fields with the spend beside each (`AO.amountSays` checks the form in place); the chip reads spend over amount (`_metered_chip`, `AO.usageChip`), the smallest amount among an account's profiles. Left: the turns' rate on the chip's hover, and each profile's amount on it; then the *Done when* on the live copy. **The techlead's gap from #689, closed 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1, PR #690):** `_billing_of` reads a profile only a node defines as metered from the node's `spend` calls (`_spend_node_prices`, held in memory and learned again on the node's first `spend` after a home restart); such a profile has no Settings card, since the page draws the home's `profiles.yml`, and its amount is set with `ao gate` (§4.7 says so). (Slice 4 is PR #688; this line folds into the Status once both have landed.)

**Each profile's amount on the hover built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2):** `usage_accounts` keeps each metered profile's `amounts` and the hover lists them, *api [day amount $5, week amount 2M tok]*, the same in `AO.usageChip`; of item (1) below only the turns' rate is left, and it waits on the designer's word for what the rate is. **Left, 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-1):** every slice is merged, and the techlead's gap from #689 is closed by #690. Three things remain. (1) The chip's hover lacks *each profile's amount* and *the turns' rate* (§4.5a **usage**). The rate is not defined: turns per hour, or per window? The reading carries no turn count per window yet (`spend.sums` drops the rows' `turns`), so this is the designer's word first, then a small build. (2) The *Done when* on the live copy, once Paul declares a metered profile (board line). (3) For the **Resolved:** line when this archives, the techlead's read of #690: two questions stay the person's — whether two profiles on one account must agree on prices (the review of #681), and the four changes slice 4 made to the link (on the board, due 2026-10-05).

**Related:** TD-128 (the design), TD-100 (the usage gate and the Settings page), TD-122 (one reading per account — a metered reading is the account's spend, as a subscription reading is the account's windows), TD-112 (the second adapter), TD-148 (the Settings page's build), [ADR 2026-09-24](decisions/2026-09-24-paperclip.md) item 3.

## TD-152: Build the start time — `start_at`, the `scheduled` state, the tick's create at the instant, `ao new --at` / `ao at`, the starts note and the At field

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (the designer; TD-026's second round, PR #548)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built — slices 1 and 2 (#669) and 3 (#671); the live walk after a promote is on the board. **Slices 1 and 2 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #669):** the `scheduled` state and `start_at` (home-owned), `create` with `start_at` (refused without `unattended`, in the past, with a `run_until` not after it, and with `resume`), `set_start` (in `ACTING_RPCS` and both `HOME_EDITS` tables, `now` for the next tick), `close`/`remove` as Cancel, the reconcile, the usage gate and the stop time passing a scheduled record by, and the tick's `_scheduled_start` replaying the launch record with `start_of` under the ceiling. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-27 (grinder-ao-1, PR #671):** `ao new --at`, `ao at`, `ao status -v`'s *starts* line, the ◷ pill, the card's *starts* note and `more ⋯`'s Start now / Cancel, Focus's banner (no terminal; it looks again every 20 s), the At field, and `read_when`'s `scheduled` row. Left: the *Done when*'s live walk after a promote (board), and TD-026's archive once TD-133 is built or Paul closes it. Design: §6 *Start time*, §4.2 (the `scheduled` row), §4.5a *starts* note, *At* field, *state icon*, §4.7 `ao new --at` / `ao at`, glossary *start time*. **Next:** built; what is left is the live walk after a promote.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`start_at` on the record; `scheduled` in the states), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`create` with `start_at`: the name, the worktree, the launch record and the slot, no tmux session; the tick's pass beside `_keep_running` creating it at the instant by the replay with `why: start`, under `RESTART_CEILING`; `set_start` gated as `set_stop` is; `remove` on a scheduled record frees the slot and deletes the launch record), `src/sessionorc/modes.py` (a home-owned intent: the tables), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao new --at`, `ao at <session> <when> | now`, `ao status -v`'s *starts* line by `stop_note`'s formatter), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py`, `card.html`, `focus.html`, `new_session.html` (the ◷ pill, the starts note, the At field, the banner's Start now / Cancel, `more ▾`'s two), `tests/`.

**Why:** Paul kept a per-session start time in scope on 2026-09-25: *start it tonight at eight* is a person's press with a clock on it, and the only start rule so far is the team's reset start (TD-133).

**Fix:** three slices; the first two touch `src/sessionorc/**`, so the techlead reads them (§4.9b). (1) The record and the create: `start_at` parsed as `run_until` is; `create` with it makes the record `scheduled` — name, directory, worktree, launch record, `supervised`, the slot held — and refuses `--at` without `--unattended`, a past instant, and an `--until` not after it; `set_start` (`ao at`) moves it or, with `now`, hands it to the tick's next pass; `remove` cancels. (2) The tick: a `scheduled` record whose instant has passed is created from its launch record (`why: start`), a failure counted toward the ceiling and shown as a restart's is; a node's waits for its link. (3) The pages and the CLI: the ◷ pill and the *starts* note on the card and Focus header, Focus without a terminal and its banner, `more ▾`'s Start now / Cancel, the At field, `ao status -v`'s line.

**Done when** `ao new --unattended --at +2m -d <repo> --worktree w1 w1` shows a ◷ *scheduled · starts <t>* card at once, refuses a second session in that directory meanwhile, becomes a `working` session within a tick of the instant with `restarts` carrying `why: start`; `ao at w1 now` starts one early; Cancel on a scheduled card forgets it and frees the slot; `--at` without `--unattended` is refused; the At field on the New session form does the same; TD-026 is archived once TD-133 is built or Paul closes it.

**Related:** TD-026 (the design), TD-133 (the team's reset start, the other start rule), TD-103 (the launch record and the replay this reuses), TD-081 (Resume, the other create-from-record), §9 invariants 2 and 5.

## TD-154: Read a session's transcript without resuming it: a **Transcript** control on Focus and the Resumable list, and `ao transcript`

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (raised by Paul: he resumed the designer to read what it had done)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-25 (the designer): design §4.5 screen 9 *Transcript*, §4.5a *Focus header* **Transcript**, the *Transcript* row and the Resumable **Resume** row, §4.3 `read_transcript`, §4.4a *Reads of a pane, and of a transcript*, §4.7 *Transcript*, the glossary's *transcript*, mockup `Transcript.dc.html` (the Focus artboards gain the button). Settled: (a) a folded read-only page, VS Code beside it for the raw file — the raw file alone is not a reading surface; (b) `ao transcript <id> [-n N] [--before OFFSET] [--raw]`; (c) the record's own `adapter_id` / `dir` / `adapter` / `profile` locate it, so a superseded `closed` record still reads its own run, and a row with no record passes the tool session id and directory instead; (d) served on the record's host, a node's through `read` (`NODE_READS` gains `transcript`), never copied. The build is TD-165 and TD-166; this entry archives with them. The design is PR #558; the page-against-raw-file choice went to Paul as a steer (the shape is his to prefer), and #558 merges at its bound (12 h from 2026-09-25 18:50 MDT) unless he says otherwise — the next designer run merges it if this one has ended. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-165 (the adapter's read, the RPC, the node read, the CLI) and TD-166 (the page and the button).
**Blocked by:** TD-165, TD-166

**Location:** design §4.5a (*Focus (exited / closed)*, *Resumable*, the Focus header), §4.3 (`transcript_path` on the adapter contract — Claude Code's answer is the JSONL under `~/.claude/projects/`, already read for the model and the resume id), `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py` (`transcript_path`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (a page or panel), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao transcript <id>`).

**Why:** the only way a person can read what a finished session did is to resume it, which is the wrong tool three times over: it creates a live session and a record (and today leaves the record reading `working`, TD-155), it is a lifecycle event a manager may act on, and it has to be closed again. Resuming a Claude Code session spends no tokens until a prompt is sent, so the cost is not money; it is a live thing the person did not want. The transcript is a file on the host that the adapter already locates; a read of it is a read, gated by nobody (§9 invariant 11). The raw file is one JSON object per line, so *open in VS Code* on the file alone (the §2 jump-out, `vscode://file/<path>`) is not a reading surface — it is the escape hatch beside one.

**What the design round has to settle:** (a) **the surface** — a read-only **Transcript** page in the UI (turns folded: prompt, assistant text, tool calls collapsed to their first line, the way the pane shows them) reached from the Focus header on any state and from each Resumable row, with *open in VS Code* beside it for the raw file; or the raw file only; (b) **`ao transcript <id> [--raw] [-n N]`**, the same rendering on the CLI, which is what a lead reading a quiet worker (TD-091's moment) would use; (c) **which record** — an exited record holds `adapter_id`; a `closed` and a superseded one (Resume replaces in place, TD-081) should still reach the transcript they came from; (d) **the multi-host case** — the file is on the record's host, read through the node as `tail` is (§4.4a), never copied to the home; (e) the mockup.

**Done when** Paul can read the designer's last run from its exited card without a session starting, and `ao transcript designer-ao-1` prints it.

**Related:** TD-081 (Resume, what this is not), TD-145 (a Resume's record), TD-155 (why a resume misleads today), TD-091 (context near its limit: the other reader of the transcript), §4.5a *Resumable*, §4.3 `transcript_path`.

## TD-156: UI review of the end of a session: after a person's Wrap up, Focus offers Kill in the header and Close only in the side panel, and a concluded team folds the card that is ready to close

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-25 (raised by Paul: he wrapped up the resumed designer and could not find a Close)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** design-first

**Why:** the end of a session is the one moment the person must act (only the person closes it, §2), and the screen they are on when the moment comes does not show them the act: Focus leads with Kill, Close is below the fold, and a concluded team hides the card. A session left open costs nothing in tokens but stays live, holds its worktree and its name, and is one more thing the team's next Start has to close first.

**Status:** **Designed and built 2026-09-25**, in a cloud session with Paul, from his screenshot of the designer's Focus (the header over three lines, buttons on two of them, *unattended* where *Take over* should read) and his ask: *simplify the screen, hide the rarely used info under something expandable, and fix the button layout*. The design is §4.5 *The Focus screen's anatomy* and *What Wrap up promises*, and the §4.5a rows **Close session**, **more ▾**, Wrap up, Kill, Copy / Paste, the side panel, grants, controllers and **team groups**; the mockups are `Focus`, `FocusOrc` and the new `FocusReady` artboards, and the Org artboard's fold. What each question got: **Next:** Paul's interactive UI review, likely a cloud session (board line, due 2026-09-30); the design changes it yields go to the designer or a grinder afterwards.

- (a) **Focus after a wrap-up**: yes — the header is two lines, and the acts line's first button is the next act by the card's rule: **Close session**, outlined, the moment Ready to close passes on an `idle` or `exited` session, with ***ready to close ✓*** on the identity line in the card's words; Take over is the next act on an unattended session otherwise; Kill went into **more ▾**, last and red, with Pop out, Copy, Paste, Hand back and Copy tmux command. Message, Wrap up, Open shell here and the editor button stay plain on the acts line. The grants and controllers chips left the header for the Session card.
- (b) **a concluded team's fold**: a concluded team **never folds** — only a team with nothing live does (`live`, not `stopped`, is what the fold keys on) — and every team header counts *n ready to close* beside its states.
- (c) **what Wrap up promises**: the button's hover and the press's toast read *asks it to finish, push and report — the same prompt the policy sends; you close it when Ready to close passes*. The rule that a person's Wrap up is only the prompt stands.
- (d) **Kill's place**: behind more ▾ whatever the state, as on the card; Close is the act when the checklist passes, Kill one menu away for a worktree that must be kept.
- (e) the mockups: regenerated; `FocusReady.dc.html` is the after-wrap-up case.
- (f) the fold control: beside Start, *▸ n sessions* / *▾ n sessions*.
- (g) **two cards named designer-ao-1**: **not reproduced** — no live Org in a cloud session. The client's swap is by id (`old.replaceWith(fresh)`), so two elements need two inserts of one id; the events handler now drops any second element with a card's id after a delta as a guard. **Paul, 2026-09-25: the doubled card is handled under its own TD**, so it leaves this entry's live look.
- Fixed beside it: the header's mode button read *unattended* instead of *Take over* — a card-era line rewrote every mode button's text to the bare mode word after `mode_act` had been set.
- Every side card is a fold remembered per browser; Session starts folded, its summary the profile; the stops control sits on the identity line only while a stop time is set, and in the Session card as *none — set* otherwise.
