1. **Capture:** log each hook event's name, `source` / `tool_name` / `agent_id` and time per session for a day (a debug line in `agentorc-hook` or the agent's `rpc_hook`), and catch the post-Stop event. Name it in this entry.
2. **Refuse it:** once named, map it in the adapter as TD-090 and TD-155 did, so it leaves the state as it was.
3. **Belt and braces:** before `stalled?`, a hook-fed session whose screen shows the composer at rest (no spinner, empty or dim composer) is `idle` on the screen verdict, not stalled. And a `stalled?` session with unread mail and that same resting screen may be rung.
4. **If the suspect holds:** turn prompt suggestions off for unattended launches in the `--settings` layer (Claude Code's setting for it, verified against the running version), since nobody reads a suggestion at an unattended pane.

Tests: a `PreToolUse` (or the named event) within seconds of a `Stop`, with no `UserPromptSubmit`, leaves the session `idle`; a stalled session with a resting composer reads `idle`. Done when the cause is named and an idle grinder with mail is rung.

**Watch (check by 2026-09-30, `ao status` and the Org page):** no grinder reads `stalled?` while its pane is at rest (composer empty or dim, no spinner). Log any sighting before the fix lands here, with its time, and with its hook events if step 1's capture is running.

**Related:** TD-090, TD-155 (the same family, archived), TD-103 (§6 rules; stall handling not built), TD-187 / TD-195 (waking a finished member, which also needs the idle to be true), design §4.2 (hook versus screen), §4.10 (the doorbell), §6 *Stall*.

## TD-209: The org-wide `grinder` role carries agentorc's review paths into every repo

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-27 (found when grinder-dc-1 was recreated: its launch record holds `review: {reader: techlead, held: [src/sessionorc/**, docs/briefs/**], bound: 2h}`)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** decision
**Status:** Open — **answered 2026-09-28 by TD-210's design** (design §4.9 *A repo's held paths are the repo's*): `held:` is written on the role in the repo's own `.agentorc.yml`, and the org-wide role keeps the reader and the profile. TD-229 slice 2 writes agentorc's; the org file's edit is on the board. Closes with TD-229. **Next:** `org.yml` is the anchor's, outside the repo.
**Blocked by:** TD-229
**Location:** `~/.agentorc/org.yml` (`roles: grinder: {profile: grind, review: {reader: techlead, held: ["src/sessionorc/**", "docs/briefs/**"]}}`), design §4.9b *The reader*, §4.8 (role presets and their layers)

**Why:** the `review:` line was set on 2026-09-23 for agentorc (TD-093): a grinder PR touching `src/sessionorc/**` or `docs/briefs/**` waits for the techlead. It sits on the org-wide role overlay, so every team's grinder carries it. In dev-cadence `src/sessionorc/**` doesn't exist and `docs/briefs/**` is a real path with a different meaning; samscrape's and contractmatch's grinders carry it too. It is harmless where a path is absent and wrong where it matches by accident.

**Fix:** decide where a repo's held paths live: in the repo's own `.agentorc.yml` (per repo, reviewed with the code), on the team in `org.yml`, or both with the repo's winning. Then move agentorc's two paths there and leave the org-wide role with a reader and no paths. Check §4.9b and the settings audit's definition-versus-setting rule (ADR 2026-09-25) for which it is. Done when grinder-dc-1's record holds dev-cadence's own held paths or none, and ao-grind's grinders keep theirs.

**Related:** TD-093 (the reader and `held`), TD-120 (org roles), `docs/decisions/2026-09-25-settings-audit.md`.

## TD-210: The home's definition files have no history and no copy off the machine

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul: *does it seem odd that our org.yml is not source controlled? If I set up an org then want to work it from another machine, I have to remember to grab it?*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #725; the steer to Paul is `m-4dbd743f974f`, bound 2026-09-29 10:35 MDT): design §4.9 *The org is an aggregate of what the repos define* and *What is left at the home has a history*, §4.7 `ao org`, §4.5a *Members… on a repo-defined team*, §5; the glossary's *org*. It answers TD-209 too. Closes with TD-229. What came before: **Answered 2026-09-28 (Paul, to the anchor; the ask was closed `asker_gone` at the designer's restart seven minutes after it was put, before he saw it, TD-213):** none of the four as asked. Instead, *should org.yml actually be an aggregate of each of the repos that support a team? samscrape, contractmatch and dev-cadence would each have an org.yml that defines the team(s) associated with them, then agentorc/shiftlead aggregates them to create the full org.* The round designs that: each repo defines its own teams (its `.agentorc.yml` `teams:` exists today and `ao team list` already merges it with `org.yml`'s), versioned and reviewed with that repo, and the home aggregates them. What still needs a central home, and is much smaller: which repos make up the org and where each is checked out on which host, the accounts and profiles, the person's settings, and any team spanning repos. The round decides where that remainder lives (the four answers of the ask, now over a small file) and how an org is stood up on a fresh machine from the repos plus that remainder. TD-209 (per-repo review paths) falls to the same answer. **Asked 2026-09-28** (the designer, ask `m-75762d6d9bee` in the person inbox): where the definition files live — a private git repo the home reads from, its page edits committed and pushed (recommended); the same with the anchor pushing; a local git repo in `~/.agentorc` copied off the machine by the backup; or as they are. It is Paul's because it makes a new private repo and has the host agent push to it, which nothing in the host agent does today. The round runs on his answer. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-229.
**Blocked by:** TD-229
**Location:** `~/.agentorc/` (`org.yml`, `profiles.yml`, `settings.yml`, `hosts.yml`), design §4.4a (*`org.yml` lives on the home*), §5 (the settings files), §4.9 (Members… edits `org.yml` in place), `docs/decisions/2026-09-25-settings-audit.md` (the file table)

**Why:** the home's definition files live only in `~/.agentorc/` on the home. A node reads the home's org and keeps none of its own (§4.4a), so two machines never disagree. But nothing gives the home's copy history or a second place to live. The nightly backup tars the files into `~/.agentorc/backups/`, on the same disk. Moving the home, rebuilding kmaster, or running the org from a fresh machine means remembering to copy the files by hand. There is no history: `~/.agentorc/` holds ten hand-made `org.yml.bak-*` files (2026-09-17 to 2026-09-24), which is version control by hand. The pressure grows now that Members… (TD-172) edits `org.yml` from the page. A repo's `.agentorc.yml` can hold `teams:` and is versioned with its code, but the org's projects, its `roles:` overlay and cross-repo teams have no such home.

**Fix:** design a versioned home for the definition files. The anchor's proposal: a private git repo (e.g. `agentorc-org`) whose checkout the home reads from, via `AGENTORC_ORG_DIR` or `~/.agentorc` pointing into it. Then settle: (1) **which files**: `org.yml` and `profiles.yml` surely, `settings.yml` probably (the person's; no secrets in any, since secrets stay in Doppler), while `hosts.yml` (this machine's identity) and the runtime store (sessions, runs, inbox) stay local; (2) **page edits commit**: Members… and the Settings page's writes commit with a message naming the act, as the board write-back does (§4.4); pushed, or committed and left for the anchor to push; (3) **a hand edit and a page edit meeting**: the page refuses on a dirty checkout, or commits the hand edit first; (4) **moving the home**: clone the repo on the new machine and name it as the home; `ao host up` / `ao service install` could take the repo's URL; (5) where each file stands under the settings audit's definition-versus-setting rule, and whether per-repo keys (TD-209's held paths) move to the repos instead. Done when the org can be stood up on a fresh machine from a clone and one command, and `org.yml`'s history is `git log`.

**Related:** TD-209 (the org-wide role's review paths), TD-172 (Members… edits `org.yml`), TD-146 (`settings.yml`), TD-057 (home and node), the settings audit ADR, CLAUDE.md's cross-repo convention (secrets in Doppler).

## TD-212: `ao promote --sha` is refused — design the rollback

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul, answering grinder-ao-1's board line of 2026-09-27: *add a design TD for ao promote --sha*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #719; the steer to Paul is `m-4ceaaf71206c`, bound 2026-09-29 10:08 MDT): design §6 *A rollback* (which commit, the detached worktree, the preconditions, what the repo may refuse, the hold, what it does not reach), §4.7 (`--sha`, `--back`, `ao promote clear`), §5 `promote:` (`run` makes live the tree it is started in), §4.5a *Inbox row: promote* (the held row), the glossary's *rollback*. Of the entry's three ways, (a) the scratch worktree; (b) the kept wheel and (c) the quicker revert were turned down, the history says why. Closes with TD-226. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-226.
**Blocked by:** TD-226
**Location:** design §4.7 (`ao promote`), §6 *Promote*, `src/sessionorc/promote.py` and the promote's `run` (TD-132, the build #666 refuses `--sha`)

**Why:** design §4.7 promised `ao promote --sha <commit>`, a promote of an older commit, which is the rollback. TD-132's build (#666, grinder-ao-1) refuses it: the promote's `run` installs the checkout's tree, and precondition (1) holds that tree at main's head, so an older commit has no way to reach `run`. The techlead, reading #666, asked for it on the board since it narrows what the design promised. Today a rollback is a revert merged to main and then a press, which is slow when the live copy is broken (TD-062's 31 minutes).

**Fix:** design how an older commit reaches the install: (a) `run` installs from a scratch worktree at the commit (`git worktree add --detach`) instead of the main checkout; (b) install the wheel the last promote wrote to `~/.agentorc/wheels/` (kept per commit, so a rollback needs no build); (c) keep the refusal and make the revert PR a one-press path. Also: what the live reading says after a rollback (*live is older than main*, not *behind*), whether the automatic promote then leaves it alone until a newer main, and what `ao promote --sha` refuses (a commit not on main, one older than the last schema change). Then the build TD. Done when a person can put the live copy back on the previous commit in one command and the page says it is a rollback.

**Related:** TD-132 (the promote), TD-062 (why the live copy is promoted, not edited), #666.

## TD-214: A wound-down team never starts again when its lanes gain work

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul: *it looks like the dc-grind and ao-grind teams both have work to do but are both idle — should they restart automatically?*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #721; the steer to Paul is `m-9bcd1abd8d1a`, bound 2026-09-29 10:14 MDT): design §6 *Keeping a team running* rule 8 (**Work for a team that wound down**) and rule 6 (the first write at the declaration, the `owner:` lane word), §4.9a, §5 `teams.<team>.on_work`, §4.7 `ao team on-work`, §4.5a *Inbox row: team start*, *work waiting* note and the Settings page's Teams row; the glossary. The default is `ask`; `start` is the person's setting. The owner words themselves are `org.yml`'s, on the board for Paul. Closes with TD-227. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-227.
**Blocked by:** TD-227
**Location:** design §6 (*Keeping a team running*, rule 6), §4.9a (wind-down), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (rule 6: `lane_seen`), the team's Start (`agentorc.teamrun`), TD-133 (schedules)

**Why:** ao-grind wound down at 2026-09-28T06:56Z. Every member had declared out of work, so the manager closed them and exited. Over the next day the designer's lane gained entries (TD-198, 199, 203, 206, 207, 208, 210, 212, 213, and TD-180 once answered), and nothing started the team again; Paul noticed at the Org page and the anchor pressed Start. Three gaps:
1. **Nothing restarts a wound-down team.** Start is a person's press or `ao team start`; the schedule that could press it (TD-133) is designed and not built, and it is a clock, not a response to work.
2. **Rule 6 (TD-195) cannot reach a closed member.** It tells a *live* finished member its lane gained work. The wind-down closes the members, so the rule has nobody to tell.
3. **Rule 6's first reading counts today's backlog as seen.** When TD-195 went live with the promote of `9a023752`, its first tick (15:00:19Z) wrote each finished member's `lane_seen` as every entry then matching its lane, as §6 says, *written on the first tick after the declaration*. The designer's ten entries were thus recorded as seen and would never have been told: nine filed after its declaration of 2026-09-27T14:27Z (TD-198 to TD-213), and TD-180, filed earlier but made pickable again by Paul's answer on 2026-09-28. The rule is right from here on; it was wrong for the backlog it went live on. Also: `free-pick` matches by `Pickable` and `Kind` but not `Owner`, so grinder-ao-1's `lane_seen` holds TD-159, an anchor evaluation.

dc-grind is the other case: its grinder is live, idle and truly out of work (four entries wait on Paul's decisions), which is working as designed.

**Fix:** design (1) a team-level rule: when a wound-down team's lanes (its members' lane words matched against the ledger reading, as rule 6 does) gain an entry filed or made pickable after the wind-down, the home starts the team again, the same sequence as Start. Bounded by the usage gate, the team's stop time, a per-day start budget, and a person's *hold* on the team, and saying so on the team card (*restarted: TD-213 filed*). Or it puts a *Start?* row in the Inbox instead, if an automatic start is too much; the round decides which, maybe per team. (2) `lane_seen`'s first write takes the declaration's time: entries filed, or made pickable, after `out_of_work.at` are new even if the first tick is later. (3) The lane match keys on `Owner` as well (the grinder's lane is `Owner: grinder`, the designer's `Owner: designer`). Then the build. Done when an entry filed into a wound-down team's lane starts the team (or asks the person to) within a tick or two, and a promote that turns rule 6 on does not swallow the backlog.

**Related:** TD-187 / TD-195 (rule 6), TD-133 (schedules), TD-053 (the wind-down), TD-213 (the wind-down drops the person's questions), TD-199 (a running member keeps its start brief).

## TD-217: Build rule 7, brief changed

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-199's design)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built, live check pending — **slice 1 built 2026-09-28 (grinder-ao-2, PR #714)**: `Role.compose` returns `prompt_from` `{base, slots, prefix}` beside the text; `ao team start`, `ao new --role/--brief` and the New session form hand it to `create`; `LAUNCH_KEYS` keeps it; a create routed to a node drops it (the home keeps the launch record). For slice 2: slots fill in order, the `{repo}` file's text stripped and `none` when empty, `{context}` is the primer's path as text, `prefix` (the Project block) goes in front; a `{file}` read across the link (a team on a machine node) is a path on that node, not the home. **Slice 2 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #745):** `sessionorc/brief.py` (`fill`: files as merged at origin's default, others from disk, each named by its git blob id; `record`), the replay's `_refill_prompt` (every replay with `prompt_from`; unreadable, none, or a node's member → `prompt: stored`), `brief: {at, sources}` on the record at each create; both of the techlead's #714 notes tested. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #747):** `brief.changed`, the detached `_brief_pass` (reports' cadence, this host's live records; the settle restarts on new blob ids; the mark goes when the files read as recorded again), `brief_changed` on the record, `ao status -v`'s `brief:` line, the card slot and the Focus `#fbc` chip; a replay of the stored prompt writes no `brief` (the techlead's note on #745). **Slice 4 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #748):** the clause on every `ao` reply (`mail.brief`, not to a declared member or a seat; a word said under the mark is never *early*), `_brief_restart` (idle, unclaimed, undeclared, pushed, on the home, under the ceiling; `why: brief`), the grinder and manager templates' sentences. **Left:** the live half of *Done when* — after the promote that carries it, a merged change to a team's supplement reaches its idle manager within a round (the board line, due 2026-10-02) — then archive this entry and TD-199. **Next:** built; what is left is the live check.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/repoconfig.py` (`Role.brief_text`: the slots and their sources), `src/agentorc/teams.py` (`_brief`), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao new --brief`, `ao status -v`), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`rpc_create`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_write_launch`, the replay, the keep-running passes), `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`brief`, `brief_changed`, `HOME_OWNED`), `src/sessionorc/gitinfo.py` (a file read at `origin/<default>`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (the chip), `src/agentorc/briefs/*.md` (what the reply clause means); design §6 *Keeping a team running* rule 7. Held path: the techlead reads the PR.

**Why:** TD-199's *Why*, and what its design found: a restart replays the stored prompt, so a changed template reached nobody short of a team's Start.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **`prompt_from`**: `brief_text` returns, beside the text, `{base, slots}` — the template's installed path, and per slot `{file}` or `{text}` (an empty slot is the text the template's `none` word is); `ao team start`, `ao new --role/--brief` and the New session form hand it to `create`; `_write_launch` keeps it. A role with no template and a supplement has the supplement as `base`. No behaviour changes yet.
2. **The replay**: a replay with `prompt_from` fills `base` from the slots — a file inside a git checkout read at `origin/<default>:<path>` as last fetched, any other file from disk — and hands that prompt to `create`; unreadable, or no `prompt_from`: the stored prompt, `prompt: stored` on the `restarts` entry. `brief: {at, sources: [{path, sha}]}` written on the record at every create, from the texts as read.
3. **The mark**: on the reports' cadence the home re-reads each source; a difference that has stood `BRIEF_SETTLE` (ten minutes) writes `brief_changed: {at, paths}`; a create clears it. `ao status -v` and `--json`; the chip on the card and the Focus header (§4.5a).
4. **The two tellings**: the reply clause through `mail` as `mail.context` rides (`mail.brief`), printed by `ao` beside the unread line; the tick's restart of a member that is hook-idle, not a seat, unattended, with no claim in progress, no declaration, git known clean and pushed, no wrap-up, stop time, gate or suspension, on the home — closed, replayed, `why: brief`, under the ceiling. The worker and manager templates gain the sentence that says what the clause means.
5. **Tests**: a replay after the supplement changed on `origin/main` hands the new text, and the working tree's unmerged edit is not read; an unreadable file replays the stored prompt; a change shorter than the settle marks nothing; the mark clears at the restart; a working member gets the clause and no restart; an idle manager with a clean tree is restarted once; a seat, a finished member, an interactive session, a gated one and a node's member are left alone; `sessionorc` imports nothing of `agentorc` (`tests/test_agent_split.py` stands).

**Done when** a template change that is promoted, or a supplement change that is merged, reaches a running manager within a round — the tick restarts it on the new brief — and until it does the card says *brief changed*; design §6 rule 7, §4.8, §4.4a and §4.5a lose their *not built*.

**Related:** TD-199 (the design), TD-114 (supplements), TD-103 (the tick's rules), TD-186 (the restart rules' races), TD-190 (rule 5, whose two tellings this follows), TD-191 (the round log that makes a manager's restart cheap).

## TD-219: Build Add entry, the form

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-180's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** the package's `briefs/entry.md` with its three slots and `repoconfig.entry_text`, and the techlead template's *An entry handed to you*, which takes `entry.md` through a new `{entry}` slot (design §4.8's placeholder list), PR #758. **Slice 2 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** the team's `entries:` key (`org._entries`, `TeamDef.entry_role`, each role checked when the definition is read), `ao team list --json`'s `entries`, PR #761. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** **Add entry…** on the Repo page and the team card's Technical debt line, the form (one `<dialog>` in the base page), **Open a session** (`/api/entry/plan`, `/api/entry/session`: `entry-<n>` interactive in a new worktree, the role `entries:` names, the team's badge and live manager, no prompt) and Focus's draft from `localStorage`; no help line was added for the button (its `title` says what it does); a live look is Paul's after a promote. The session way's tests (slice 5's first half) are in `tests/test_ui_entry.py`. **Slice 4 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** **Hand to the techlead** — `/api/entry/hand` over `entry_add`, the button's state and *When it is read* from `/api/entry/plan`'s `hand` (`ui/repo.py` `entry_teams`, `entry_hand`), disabled with its reason and while **What** is empty, a toast linking `/inbox/<id>`; its tests are in `tests/test_ui_entry.py`. **The Waiting-on-them row built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** `ui/inbox.py` `handed_rows` over the `inbox` read's `handed` list, *Waiting on them* whatever the holder's state and *Needs you* once `blocked`, the `handed` macro in `inbox_row.html`, `/inbox/<id>` finds it; and a seat with no checkout on its host reads *<seat>: the techlead seat has no checkout on its host*. Left: a live look after a promote (Paul's) **Next:** built; what is left is Paul's live look after a promote.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/briefs/` (`entry.md`, new; `techlead.md`), `src/agentorc/teams.py` and `src/agentorc/org.py` (the `entries:` key), `src/agentorc/ui/repo.py`, `src/agentorc/ui/templates/repo.html` and `team_summary.html` (the button), a new form template, `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.js` (the composer's fill: TD-170's Shift+press sets `compose.value`), `src/agentorc/ui/help.py` when a help line is wanted for the button (the wording is this entry's to write, with the doc-bound tests run); design §4.9 *Add an entry to the ledger*, §4.5a **Add entry…**, **Hand to the techlead**, **Open a session**; mockup `docs/mockups/AddEntry.dc.html`

**Why:** TD-180's *Why*: the person has no way to put an entry in the ledger from a page.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **`entry.md` and the preset**: the package's `entry.md` — what an entry needs (the ledger's template and its newest entries, the three-line header, `Type:`, the first free number over `origin/main`, the archive and the open PRs, read again before the merge, the Summary row, `**Added:**` naming the person and the drafter, branch → PR → fact-check → cadence check → squash) with `{repo}`, `{type}` and `{ledger}` slots; the `techlead` preset's brief gains *An entry handed to you*: draft on a branch, push and open a draft PR before asking, ask on the thread with two to four answers, end the turn to wait, report the outcome naming the entry and the PR.
2. **`entries:`** on a team definition: `{feature: <role>, debt: <role>}`, either key optional, `techlead` where unsaid; an unknown key or a role nothing resolves is an error naming it; `ao team list --json` carries it.
3. **The button, the form and the session way**: **Add entry…** on the Repo page's Technical debt heading and the team card's Repo facet; the form (the repo shown, **What**, **Type**); **Open a session** creates an interactive session as the New session form's Team pick does — a new worktree `entry-<n>`, the role from `entries:`, no opening prompt and no brief — and goes to its Focus with the composer holding `entry.md`'s lines and the words, focused and unsent, kept in the browser as that session's draft until sent. A repo no team services starts `plain` with no badge.
4. **Hand to the techlead**: the button calls `entry_add` (TD-218), draws the *when it is read* sentence under it, is disabled with its reason where there is no seat or no team, and its toast links the message's page.
5. **Tests**: the form is drawn from both places with the repo filled; **Open a session** with a Type of `feature` on a team whose `entries:` names a role starts that role, interactive, with no prompt sent; the composer's text reaches Focus and nothing is typed into the pane; the first button is disabled without a seat; nothing on the page writes a repo file.

**Done when** Paul presses **Add entry…** on the agentorc Repo page, writes two sentences, and gets either a merged entry reported in his Inbox or a session whose composer holds his words; design §4.9 and §4.5a lose their *not built* for this entry, and TD-180 archives with TD-218 and this one.

**Left for Paul:** `entries: {feature: designer}` under `teams.ao-grind` in `~/.agentorc/org.yml`, which gives his split — an interactive designer for a feature, a techlead for the rest. Without it both types start a techlead session. On the board.

**Related:** TD-180 (the design), TD-218 (the home's half), TD-173 (a person in the team: the start this one reuses), TD-170 (the composer's fill), TD-176 (the Repo page and the facet).

## TD-222: A board write-back is never pushed and the host agent never pulls

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, found in TD-208's round)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Blocked by:** TD-263, TD-264
**Status:** **Designed 2026-10-01** (the designer, PR #892; the steer to Paul on how a write-back reaches origin is `m-23539eb2dcc8`, bound 2026-10-02 05:31 MDT). Design: §6 *Pull* (the policy: every registered checkout, in the promote's pass, `--ff-only` when git allows — default branch, no git operation or promote run under way, the fast-forward accepted — and the anchor session is idle — every occupant of the root at rest, an external `claude` by its registry's `status`, one it cannot read taken as mid-turn; its `pulls` reading; the `repos.<repo>.pull` switch, on when absent), §4.4 *Board write-back* (the edit made on origin's head in a tree of the host agent's own and pushed; the checkout never written, the refusals for its state gone, *origin unreachable* and *the line is not on origin* in their place), §4.5 screen 6 (*the host agent does not pull* reversed; every row actionable; the read after a press a fetching one), §4.5a (the origin note's tail, the Repos card's **pull**, the Decide row), §5 (`pull`). **Paul's reply, 2026-10-01: *go with the default*.** The same night the anchor found the push itself refused by the repo's ruleset (*PR required*; `ALLOW_MAIN_PUSH=1` passes only the pre-push hook), so the road is now a branch of the host agent's own and a PR it opens and squash-merges at once (§4.4), steered to Paul as `m-9c19613f7220` (bound 2026-10-02 09:48 MDT) over a bypass on the ruleset for the pushing identity, which would let every session in the checkout push `main` under the override. **The steer's default is the third road, origin's head** — not the entry's (a), the checkout's own commit pushed, which leaves the checkout ahead on every press and needs a rebase the host agent must never make when origin moves first, nor (b), as it was. The two alternatives stay in the design-history. **Cadence §4.5's sentence** (*the tool commits in the main checkout … never pushes — the anchor's next push carries it*) is dev-cadence's: on the board for Paul, and TD-264 waits on it. The *Decided* and *Fix* below are the record of the round.
**Location:** design §4.4 *Board write-back* (*it never pushes*), `src/sessionorc/board.py` (its docstring: *the person's click is never turned into a dirty file under the anchor session's feet*), cadence §4.5 (the carve-out: the edit is committed in the main checkout and *never pushes — the anchor's next push carries it*), dev-cadence's reader (`_fetch_board`: *both sides changed — pull/push; showing local*)

**Why:** a Snooze, Done or Reply commits on the main checkout's default branch and stays there until the anchor pushes it. Until then the checkout has an edit origin lacks, and the next merged PR that touches the board makes the two differ on both sides; the reader then shows the local board, and what origin added is hidden until someone pulls and pushes by hand. The other way round, a row the Inbox reads from origin (TD-208) cannot be acted on until the checkout is pulled, because the host agent does not move the person's tree. TD-208's design says both on the page and cures neither.

**Fix:** decide, as one question or two: **how a write-back reaches origin** — (a) the host agent pushes its one commit, fast-forward only, which cadence §4.5 does not permit today (it names the anchor's push), so dev-cadence's rule changes first; (b) as today, the anchor's next push, with the page's warning as the reminder; (c) the edit is made on origin through the forge's API and the checkout catches up by a pull. And **whether the host agent may fast-forward the main checkout** (`--ff-only`, clean tracked files, on the default branch) so a row read from origin can be acted on, against *the tree is a person's* (design §6 *Promote*). The designer recommends (a) and no fast-forward: one commit of one line is the smallest thing that keeps every host's view the same, and a pull stays a person's or the anchor's act. Done when the choice is written in design §4.4 and its build entry exists.

**Decided, 2026-10-01 (Paul): the host agent fast-forwards the main checkout.** He asked after finding the anchor's `main` 19 commits behind origin with every board row read from origin disabled: *it seems like main on the anchor falls behind consistently — should we have our host agent pull it routinely if no session is running on the anchor?* The rule he chose: **when git allows and the anchor session is idle.** This reverses the designer's *no fast-forward* above and the design's *the host agent does not pull* (§4.5 screen 6 *Boards are read against origin*; §6 *Promote*: *the tree is a person's*), so the design changes first. For the round to settle:
- **When git allows:** the checkout is on its default branch and `git merge --ff-only origin/<default>` goes through. Git itself refuses a fast-forward that would touch a locally changed file, so a dirty file elsewhere (an unsaved memory note) does not stop it and nothing of the person's is overwritten. A checkout with a commit of its own is left alone and said on the page as it is today, which is every checkout holding an unpushed write-back commit, so this rule does little there until the first question is settled too.
- **The anchor session is idle:** no session in the checkout's root is mid-turn. The anchor is often a plain Claude Code session agentorc did not start, so the round says how the host agent knows and what it does when it cannot tell (the anchor's recommendation: no pull, and the origin note stays). No session at all counts as idle.
- **Where and how often:** a policy of the home's tick over the registry's checkouts, as the promote is; whether it is a setting (§5), and what the page says after a pull or a refused one.
- **The live copy is not moved by it:** a pull changes the checkout, and the promote stays its own press or policy (§6 *Promote*).

**Related:** TD-208, TD-221, TD-069 (the write-back), cadence §4.5 and §4.6 (the audit that classifies a `board-edit` commit).

## TD-223: Rule 6 matches nothing in a ledger without Pickable, Owner and Kind lines

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (found walking Paul through dc-grind: *I do not see anything in the inbox from dc-grind*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #723, stacked on #721; the steer to Paul is `m-1280433e8140`, bound 2026-09-29 10:24 MDT): design §4.4 *Repo facts* (**Pickable is derived, never written**: the rule, the archive, no header line needed, the page's kinds in order, the written line while it lasts), §6 rule 6 (the lane words over the derived reading), §4.8 *Choosing in a free-pick lane*, §4.5 screen 10's lists, §4.5a *Repo facet*; the glossary's *pickable*. Option (b), agentorc's own reader held equal to the repo's script by a test. Settles TD-198 and answers TD-211. Closes with TD-228. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-228.
**Blocked by:** TD-228
**Location:** design §6 rule 6 (*a lane word matches by the entry's header*), `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`lane_matches`, `kind_of`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (rule 6), the Repo facet's kind bar; dev-cadence's `ledger.py` (pickability derived from `Blocked by:`, its TD-070)

**Why:** rule 6 (TD-195) tells a finished member its lane gained work when an entry matching its lane appears: `free-pick` matches `Pickable: yes` not design-first, `design-first` matches `Kind: design-first` with `Pickable: yes`. dev-cadence's ledger carries none of those lines: its entries have Priority, Type, Status and `Blocked by:`, and its `ledger.py` *derives* pickability from `Blocked by:` (a written `Pickable:` line never overrides, its TD-070). So every dev-cadence entry reads `pickable: ''` in `repos.json`, and rule 6 matches nothing there. On 2026-09-28 dev-cadence's TD-070 (the ledger query script) was pickable by its own tool while grinder-dc-1 sat idle and out of work, its `lane_seen` an empty list. The anchor woke it by hand. The kind bar has the same blind spot: every dev-cadence entry is *other*.

**Fix:** design one reading of pickability for every consumer. Either (a) agentorc reads each repo's ledger through that repo's own `ledger.py` once dev-cadence's TD-070 lands (`--list --json` gives the derived `pickable`, `owner`, `kind`, `type` and the raw header fields), so the header lines are one repo's convention and not the rule's; or (b) agentorc's reader derives pickability from `Blocked by:` as dev-cadence's does when the header has no `Pickable:`. Also decide what a lane word means where there is no `Owner:` (dev-cadence's grinder is the repo's only builder: everything unblocked is its lane). Then rule 6, the kind bar and `ao repo` all read the same way. Done when an entry that becomes pickable in dev-cadence tells an idle, finished grinder-dc-1, and a test covers a ledger with no header lines.

**Paul's question, 2026-09-28:** *should we consider retiring "pickable" and using ledger.py to determine it based on "blocked by" instead?* The anchor's reading: yes, and it is option (a) taken to its end. One rule decides pickability for every consumer, dev-cadence's (derived from `Blocked by:`, its TD-070 `--list --json`), and the written line goes. What the design round has to add: on 2026-09-28 the ledger held 89 `Pickable: no` lines, 17 `yes` and 23 `Blocked by:`. Most of the *no* reasons are not blocks but states: *built, the live check pending* (16 or more), *designed, the build is TD-n* (13), *for evaluation* (5), *the anchor's*, *Paul's*. Each needs a home the derivation reads: a real dependency becomes `Blocked by: TD-n` or `decision (Paul)`; *designed, the build is TD-n* becomes `Blocked by: TD-n` (the design entry closes with its build); *live check* and *evaluation* are already `Kind:` values that no grinder picks; *the anchor's* and *Paul's* are `Owner:`. So the derived rule is *unblocked, Kind build (or design-first for the designer), Owner the reader's role*. That is also what rule 6's lane match, the kind bar (TD-198) and `ao repo` read. `tests/test_ledger.py`'s header test (TD-118) changes with it, and every open entry's `Pickable:` line migrates in one PR with its reason moved, not dropped.

**Related:** TD-195 (rule 6), TD-214 (the wound-down team, the backlog baseline, the missing Owner check), TD-198 (the kind bar's buckets), dev-cadence's TD-070 (the query script) and TD-064 (`Blocked by`).

## TD-225: A restart or close raises an identity alarm from the old run's last hook

**Priority:** Low
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul, on the anchor's report of the alarms: *yes file a TD for the alarms*)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #746): (a) — `_id_note_panes` records a record's pane as gone when the list shows no pane **or another one** under it, so a restart or supersede that reuses the name starts the grace; and the log — a hook that still alarms logs its peer's pid, start and sid, the record's listed pane and its gone one with its age (`hook for … matched no pane`). Design §4.8a says it. **Left:** the live check — after the next promote, a day of team restarts leaves `identity_alarms.json` unchanged; if alarms still land, the host agent's log line says which case, and (b) (match the old run by its pid and start while it lives) is built only if some are later than the grace. **Live check 2026-10-03 (the anchor's look agent): not cured.** `ao identity` kept gaining *outside claimed <itself> on hook* alarms after #746 — grinder-ao-2 ×27 to 2026-10-03T09:25Z, grinder-ao-1 ×51 to 08:30Z, designer-ao-1 to 05:20Z, grinder-sam-1 and -2 on 10-03, tdgrind-cm-1 at 10-04T01:53Z — each with a journal line *hook for … matched no pane: … listed pane N, gone pane None*: the old pane is not recorded as gone, so (a) misses these cases; read why before (b). **Next:** built; what is left is a live check.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/agent_identity.py` (`_id_channel`: a peer that matches no pane is *outside*), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_apply_event`: *ignored the end of a previous run*, TD-186), `~/.agentorc/identity_alarms.json`

**Why:** every alarm on record has the same shape: `channel: outside`, `rpc: hook`, a session's own id claimed. grinder-ao-1 14 times (2026-09-23T23:48:09Z to 2026-09-29T03:23:24Z), designer-ao-1 3 (latest 2026-09-29T03:22:37Z), grinder-ao-2, manager-ao-1, manager-dc-1 and techlead-ao-1 once each. Those that can be placed fell at a supersede: designer-ao-1's latest two seconds after the host agent restarted it (03:22:35Z, *superseded the closed session of the same name*), grinder-ao-1's at its own restart a minute later, and techlead-ao-1's when its seat was refilled (2026-09-28T06:56:02Z, *superseded the closed session of the same name*), in the same second as alarms for designer-ao-1 and grinder-ao-1. TD-115 already covers part of this. A hook that matches no live pane is judged against the pane its record held if that pane left the list less than `PANE_GONE_GRACE` (10 s) before (`_id_gone_channel`, `identity.classify_gone`). The alarms still fire, so something falls outside it. The likeliest: a **restart reuses the tmux session name**, so the old pane never leaves the list as *gone*. The name at once has the new pane, and the old run's last hook (its `SessionEnd`, which the tick then ignores as the end of a previous run, TD-186) matches neither the new pane nor a gone one. designer-ao-1's alarm two seconds after its restart fits that, not the grace running out. Others may be hooks later than 10 s. The false alarms train the person to dismiss the list that exists to catch a real impersonation.

**Fix:** first confirm which case each alarm is. Log, for an outside hook claiming a session, the peer's pid and its start, the session's current and previous pane pids, and the time since the supersede or close. Then (a) on a supersede or restart that reuses the name, record the old pane as gone at that moment, so the grace applies to it too; (b) if some are genuinely later than 10 s, match the old run by its pid and start pair for as long as that process lives, not by a clock. Keep the alarm for a claim from a process that was never that session's pane. Tests: a restart followed by the old run's `SessionEnd` raises no alarm; an unknown process claiming the id still does. Done when a day of team restarts leaves `identity_alarms.json` unchanged.

**Related:** TD-115 (the gone-pane grace), TD-186 (the previous run's end, ignored), TD-077 (identity), TD-201 (hooks outside the turn), design §4.8a.

## TD-226: Build the rollback — `ao promote --sha`, `--back`, the hold, `ao promote clear`

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-212's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2, PR #777):** `promote_rows`' held row under *Needs you* whatever `auto` says (*rolled back from … · auto on · held*), Dismiss on it, the three promote-row help paragraphs (§4.5a *The help text*, `ui/help.py`, the Help page's *Inbox*), CLAUDE.md's *Going back* with the by-hand rollback. **Left:** the live half of *Done when* (the board). **Slices 1–2 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #757):** `promote.resolve`, the worktree, the `AGENTORC_PROMOTE_*` variables on every run, `last.json`, `held.json` and `held` on the reading, the policy waiting under a hold, `--sha` / `--back`, `ao promote clear` (`clear_promote` returns `which`); slice 4's tests but the row's. **From the techlead's read of #757 (2026-09-29):** the hold is written by the agent that concludes the run, and for this repo that is the agent the rollback installed — so a rollback to a commit older than #757 (`8841800`) is concluded by code that knows no hold (no `held.json`, no `last.json`, the note reads *promoted*), and with `auto` on main is put back on the next pass. A design change is the designer's (one shape: the hold written with the intent, before the start); until then slice 3's CLAUDE.md sentence says a rollback past `8841800` needs `auto` off first. And under `auto` on a held repo draws no row until slice 3 lands: `ao promote status` is the only place the hold shows. **Next:** slices 1–4 are built; what is left is the live half of *Done when*, on the board.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/promote.py` (the commit's resolution, the worktree, `start`'s `cwd` and environment, `last.json`, `held.json`, `unmet`, `survey`, `_conclude`, `note_text`), `src/sessionorc/agent_promote.py` (`rpc_promote`'s `sha` and `back`, `rpc_clear_promote`), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`--sha`, `--back`, `clear`, `_promote_line`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (`promote_rows`, the held row), `src/agentorc/ui/help.py` (the promote row's entry), `CLAUDE.md` (the by-hand rollback), `tests/test_promote.py`; design §6 *A rollback*, §4.7, §5 `promote:`, §4.5a *Inbox row: promote*. Held path: the techlead reads the PR.

**Why:** TD-212's *Why*: a rollback today is a revert merged to main and then a press, which waits on CI while the live copy is broken.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The commit and the run** (`sessionorc`): `promote(repo, sha, back)` resolves the commit in the checkout after the fetch (hex of seven to forty only, then `rev-parse --verify <x>^{commit}`, `merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> origin/main`) and refuses as §6 lists, each refusal naming its reason; `back` reads `last.json` and is refused under a hold. The worktree at `~/.agentorc/promotes/<repo>/tree` (the one a previous run left is removed first, then `worktree prune`; it is kept after the run, since this repo's `_build.json` names it as `source`), `run` started there with the three `AGENTORC_PROMOTE_*` variables, which a plain promote is handed too. The intent gains `from`, `kind` (`promote` or `rollback`) and `tree`. `unmet` takes the kind: a rollback skips `tree` and `failed`. `_conclude` writes `last.json` on success (for every promote), and words the note by kind.
2. **The hold**: a rollback that concludes writes `held.json`; the reading carries `held`; `survey` starts nothing under `auto` while it stands, and `unmet` names it for the policy and not for a press; a plain press that concludes clears it, and `clear_promote` clears a failure, or the hold when no failure stands, and says which; a second rollback under a hold keeps `from`. `ao promote --sha`, `--back`, `ao promote clear`; `ao promote status` prints *rolled back from* and *auto on · held*.
3. **The row and the words**: `promote_rows` draws the held row under *Needs you* whatever `auto` says, with Promote and Dismiss; the promote row has no help entry today: one is added to §4.5a's help list and `ui/help.py` together (bound by `tests/test_help.py`), saying what Promote, Snooze and Dismiss do, the hold included; CLAUDE.md's *The live copy is promoted* gains the by-hand rollback for a live copy that does not start: the worktree, then the pair with the worktree's path.
4. **Tests**: a rollback with the checkout on a branch and dirty goes through and leaves the checkout's `HEAD`, index and files as they were; a commit not on main, an ambiguous prefix and the live commit are each refused by name; `--back` with no `last.json` is refused; a rollback with a failure standing starts, and clears the failure when it concludes; under `auto: true` and a hold nothing starts as main moves, and starts after `clear_promote`; a `run` that exits before changing anything reads as a failure with live unchanged; a branch name, a tag and `HEAD~1` are refused as not hex; `--back` under a hold is refused; Dismiss with a failure and a hold clears the failure first; a leftover tree does not stop the next rollback.

**Done when:** on a scratch repo with a `promote:` block, `ao promote --back` puts live on the commit before the last promote in one command while the checkout sits on a branch, `ao promote status` and the Inbox row read *rolled back from*, `auto` leaves it there until the person promotes or clears, and slice 4's tests pass.

**Related:** TD-212 (the design), TD-132 (the promote; its slice 4, this repo's block, writes a `run` that installs the tree it is started in), TD-062.

## TD-227: Build rule 8 — work for a team that wound down

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-214's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #784):** `lane_matches(lane, entry)` with owner words (`models.owner_word`, `lane_refs`, `OWNER_WORD`), `agent_common._lane` setting them aside, the nudge, the report line and the brief's `{lane}` slot (`repoconfig._no_owner`) leaving them out; `lane_seen`'s first write from `ledger.entries_before`. **Slice 2 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #788):** `sessionorc.work` (`wound_down`, which `teamrun` now imports, `team_wound_down`, `crew`, `gained`), the tick's `_work_marks` / `_work_mark` writing `work_waiting` on the `host` record after `WORK_SETTLE` (`agent_common`; `_work_first` in memory), `teams.<team>.on_work` (`settings.ON_WORK`), and `clear_work` (`agent_wake.py`, in `modes.HOME_EDITS`). Until slice 4, `start` writes the mark as `ask` does. **The techlead's notes on #788, settled 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #790):** a crew member's ledger that cannot be read keeps the mark, its `at` and the settle's memory (rule 8 says so); §6 *Balance* no longer calls the mark not built; rule 8's *first repo by name* reads *first registry root*, as `min(news)` takes it; `modes.py` gives `clear_work` its reason. **Slice 4 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #792):** `_work_start`, `_work_replays`, `_work_held` in `agent_tick.py` — under `on_work: start` the replay of the team's ended crew records (the lead first, a seat with `keep_mail`), `restarts` entries `why: work` with `ids`, `work_started` on the host record, the four bounds (`WORK_STARTS_DAY`, `WORK_DAY`, `WORK_EARLY` in `agent_common`) writing `held: {why}` on `work_waiting` (`usage`, `until`, `day`, `early`, and `nothing` with no record to replay), and `techlead-context.md` §2 reworded; `_replay` takes `mark=`. **Slice 5's tests are in with slices 1, 2 and 4 (checked 2026-09-30, grinder-ao-1):** `tests/test_lane_news.py` (the declaration read from history, its fallback, `owner:` words, told once), `tests/test_work_waiting.py` (the settle, a stopped team, `off`, an unreadable ledger, Dismiss) and `tests/test_work_start.py` (the replay counted once, each bound) and, added then, `ask` starting nothing in the settle test. **Slice 3's first half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** `ao team on-work <team> ask|start|off` (`cli.cmd_team_on_work`; the team's settings line says *when work appears: …* where the key is set), the Settings page's **when work appears** picker (`settings_page.team_cards`, `/api/settings/teams` taking `on_work`, written only when the pick moves) and its help paragraph in §4.5a's list and `ui/help.py` (key `on-work`, the Help page's Settings heading). **Slice 3's second half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** the home's `host` read carries `work: {<team>: mark}` and `attention_snooze` takes `work` (the two edits in `src/sessionorc/`, with `balance.crossed_words`); `ui/inbox.py` `work_rows` / `work_held` (the row, counted under *Needs you*, five ids linked to `/repo/<name>#<id>`, the held bound in words), the `work_row` macro, Start through the team card's route, Dismiss through `/api/person/clear_work`; `work_note` and `work_started` on the team's header (`ui/repo.py` `team_groups(work=)`, `group_head.html`); `ao team list`'s *work waiting: n entries* and `work_waiting` in its `--json`; the three help paragraphs (`work-start`, `work-snooze`, `work-dismiss`); tests in `tests/test_ui_work_row.py`. **The three the techlead's reads left for the row and the card, built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #839):** a usage hold carries the window's `resets` (`_profile_over`, the gate's crossed row) and the row says *resets in 3h 0m*; `held: {why: link}` carries every down host as `hosts`; each `restarts` entry of one start carries `start` and `of`, and the card's *started … for* note counts *n of m records started — <names> did not* from the records (`ui/inbox.py` `work_held`, `work_started`; `tests/test_work_start.py`, `tests/test_ui_work_row.py`). **Left:** a team whose every replay failed still shows no note — the note is a live team's, and the errors are on the records' `restarts`; the balance hold lost to another reason (the read of #799, below) if the row ever shows it matters. **The row's link built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** the Repo page unfolds the list its hash names and scrolls to the row (`app.js` `AO.repo` `showHash`, `AO.hashId`; `tests/test_ui_repo_hash.py`) — the review of #824. A live look after a promote is Paul's (the board, 2026-09-30); the balance rule's fifth bound is built (TD-239). **The techlead's read of #792, its home half settled 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #797):** `_work_replays` no longer counts a seat's `fill` entries toward `RESTART_CEILING`; a member behind a down link holds the start as `held: {why: link, host}` (rule 8 says so; slice 3's row draws it); and rule 6's sentence from the read of #784 is narrowed to the first write. **Left for slice 3, from the same read:** a start whose replays partly fail is still one start and removes the mark, so a team can run without its lead with only the `error` on its `restarts` entry to say so — the card's *started … for* note should say *n of m*. **For slice 3, from the techlead's read of #797 (merged 2026-09-30):** `held: {why: link, host}` names the first down host in replay order, so a team behind two down links shows one, then the other; if the row should name all of them, `held` carries `hosts` and rule 8's sentence changes in the same diff. **Next:** built; what is left is Paul's live look after a promote, and two notes in the Status for whoever meets them.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`lane_matches`), `src/sessionorc/agent_common.py` (`_lane`, the three `WORK_*` constants) and `src/agentorc/org.py` (`_lane`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_lane_news`, `_nudge_line`, `_keep_running`, `_replay`), `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`entries_before`, the ledger at a commit, built), `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (`teams.<team>.on_work`), `src/sessionorc/work.py` and the `host` record (`work_waiting`, built), `src/sessionorc/agent_wake.py` (`clear_work`, built), `src/sessionorc/modes.py`, `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao team on-work`, `ao team list`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (the Inbox row, the card's note, the Settings page's picker, `help.py`); design §6 rules 6 and 8, §5, §4.7, §4.5a. Held path: the techlead reads the PRs.

**Why:** TD-214's *Why*: a wound-down team sat a day with ten entries in its designer's lane, and rule 6's first tick took a day's backlog as seen.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **Rule 6's two repairs** (`sessionorc`): `lane_matches` takes the whole lane, so `owner:<word>` narrows the other words (an entry with no `Owner:` matches; a lane with no owner word matches as today). Owner words are set aside before `_lane`'s checks in both packages, so `[free-pick, owner:grinder]` is accepted, and every reader of a lane as references leaves them out: `_nudge_line`, the card's lane count, the brief's lane slot, `ao progress`. `lane_seen`'s first write reads the ledger at the last commit of `origin/<default>` before `out_of_work.at` (`git rev-list -1 --before`, which reads committer dates, right for squash merges; then the file at that commit, at the record's `ledger` path, through the reader `ledger.entries` already is), and falls back to the current reading when no commit or no file is found, the log saying which was used.
2. **`work_waiting`** (`sessionorc`): `_lane_news` already writes `lane_seen` for a member that is gone and stops before the telling; the new part is the team's pass. It reads a team as wound down from the records — unattended sessions only, a seat known by the record's `seat` field and not by the definition's names — written once in `sessionorc` and imported by `teamrun.wound_down`, whose answer for the card must not change (a test holds the two equal on a team with a seat and a person's session). It writes `work_waiting` on `host` once `WORK_SETTLE` has passed since the home first read the newest id. `teams.<team>.on_work` in `settings.py` with its three values, refused otherwise; `set_settings` takes it. A `clear_work` RPC, a person's own, in `modes.HOME_EDITS`, is Dismiss's half.
3. **The row and the words**: the Inbox row from `work_waiting`, its Start the team card's, Snooze by time in the attention store under `work:<team>`, Dismiss through `clear_work`; the card's *work waiting* note; `ao team list`'s words; `ao team on-work`; the Settings page's picker. The help entries for the row and the picker are this slice's wording, in §4.5a's help list and `ui/help.py` together.
4. **The start**: under `on_work: start` the replay of the team's launch records, `why: work`, the four bounds read first and each leaving the row with its reason; `work_started` counts a start once however many records it replayed; the card's *started … for* line. `docs/briefs/techlead-context.md` §2 says the host agent *starts nothing new by itself*: the PR that builds this slice rewords it, and the techlead reads that PR.
5. **Tests**: a member that declared at T with an entry merged at T+1 h and a first tick at T+1 d is told of it; a history that cannot be read falls back; `[free-pick, owner:grinder]` leaves out an entry owned by the anchor and takes one with no `Owner:`; a wound-down team whose lane gains an entry gets `work_waiting` after the settle and not before; a stopped team (one member killed) gets none; `ask` starts nothing; `start` replays every launch record once and is held back by each of the four bounds in turn, the row saying which; Dismiss adds the ids to `lane_seen` and a later entry asks again; `off` writes nothing.

**Done when:** on a scratch home, an entry merged into a wound-down team's lane puts a *team start* row in the Inbox within `WORK_SETTLE` and two ticks, the same under `on_work: start` starts the team and the card says why, and a promote that turns the rule on tells a finished member of what was filed since it declared.

**Related:** TD-214 (the design), TD-195 (rule 6), TD-133 (the schedule, whose replay slice 4 shares: whichever is built first writes it), TD-223 (what pickable means where the header has no lines).

## TD-228: Build the derived pickable — the reader, the page's kinds, the lane words, the ledger's migration

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-223's design, with TD-198 and TD-211)
**Owner:** anchor
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #793):** `sessionorc.ledger` derives `pickable` (`entries(text, archive, resolve, fallback)`, `parse_blocked`, `archive_path`, `Registry` over `Host.repos()`, `entries_before` reading the archive at the same commit), `kind_of`'s new order, `lane_matches` with `build` for no Kind; `tests/test_ledger_derived.py` holds it equal to `scripts/ledger.py --list --json`. No lane gained an entry on this ledger (a bare `free-pick` lost TD-159). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** a Repo page row says *blocked by …* after its owner (`repo_part.html`, from `blocked_by`), `ao repo` lists in cadence's order, debt before a feature within a priority (`cli._pick_key`), and `ui/org.py`'s comment is corrected; the kind bar, its legend, *n pickable entries wait* and the four lists already read `for_page`, which slice 1 re-derived. No held path. **Slice 3 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #898):** no open entry, the template or the `Fields:` line carries `**Pickable:**` (`tests/test_ledger.py` `test_no_entry_writes_a_pickable_line`; `HEADER` is Owner and Kind), every *no*'s clause moved to its Status as **Next:**, three to a `Blocked by:` pointer instead (TD-108, TD-142, TD-201; TD-189 is blocked by TD-190); ten entries built with a live look left are `Owner: paul`, `Kind: live-check` and three the anchor's (TD-151, whose hover is unbuilt, stays the grinder's, blocked by the designer's word); a Status or a `Blocked by:` set apart below 25 header blocks is back in the block (cadence §2.12); TD-127, TD-158, TD-246 (designed, their builds archived) and TD-211 are archived by it. The two lanes match what was written *yes* before (`[free-pick, owner:grinder]`: TD-228, and TD-255 until #899 built its last slice and blocked it on Paul's look; `[design-first, owner:designer]`: TD-222, TD-262). **What the derived rule changed and the lanes did not:** the page's *pickable* and `ao repo`'s list now hold the anchor's and dev-cadence's unblocked `Kind: build` entries too (TD-004, TD-036, TD-050, TD-080, TD-093, TD-098, TD-114, TD-118, TD-125), since `kind_of` reads no owner; the grinder brief says an entry whose Owner is not the lane's is not the reader's, and `ao repo` prints the owner on each line (`cli.cmd_repo`, built 2026-10-01 by grinder-ao-2: `-` where the header has none; `tests/test_repo_facts.py`), since a run that read ten *pickable* lines found one its own. The briefs: `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md`, `entry.md`, `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md`; design §4.5 *Add entry* no longer says `Pickable: yes`. **Slice 4 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #901):** `sessionorc.ledger.entries` takes no `fallback` and reads no written line, in the header block or below it (`tests/test_ledger_derived.py` `test_a_written_pickable_line_is_not_read`); design §4.4 *Repo facts*, §4.5 screen 10 and §4.8 *Choosing in a free-pick lane* lose *a written `Pickable: no` still blocks*. **Every slice is built. Left:** the live half of *Done when* — an entry unblocked in dev-cadence's ledger telling an idle, finished grinder-dc-1 with no edit to the entry — after the promote that carries #901; TD-198 and TD-223 archive with this entry then. **Slice 3 waited on nothing (the anchor, 2026-10-01):** `org.yml`'s ao-grind lanes carry `[free-pick, owner:grinder]` and `[design-first, owner:designer]` (the board line closed with #826), and this entry's own written *no* had outlived that wait, which kept the migration out of every lane. **Paul, 2026-10-01, shown that the page counts 8 pickable where `scripts/ledger.py --pickable` derived 84 at that moment (76 on this PR's branch, later the same day):** *lets use the derived pickable - the grinders can tell us if they run into issues* — so slice 3 is the next thing a grinder takes here, slice 4 after it, and an entry the derived rule hands a grinder that it cannot build (a live look, a person's press) is said on the entry, by its `Kind:` or `Owner:`, as slice 3's migration writes them. **From the techlead's read of #793:** fixed before the merge: a pass re-reading one repo resolved `<repo>#TD-NNN` among the re-read repos only, so `_read_repos` now takes the registry's whole list for the `Registry`. The note: `src/agentorc/ui/org.py`'s comment that a pickable design-first entry sits under *pickable* is now false (slice 2's). `entries_before`'s docstring now says a `<repo>#TD-NNN` item resolves at the other repo's tip today.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/ledger.py` (`FIELD`, `entries`, `kind_of`, `KINDS`, `lane_matches`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (rule 6 reads the same entries), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao repo`: the `by_kind` line and the lists), `src/agentorc/ui/org.py`, `ui/repo.py` and `ui/templates/team_summary.html` (the kind bar, its legend and *n pickable entries wait*, the Repo page's lists; `org.py`'s comment on *pickable design-first* goes), `tests/test_ledger.py` (`HEADER`, a new equality test) and the tests that write `pickable: "yes"` by hand (`test_lane_news.py`, `test_repo_facts.py`, `test_ui_*`), `docs/technical_debt.md` (the preamble, every open entry), `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md`, `docs/briefs/designer-ao-1.md` and any other brief that names the line; design §4.4 *Repo facts*, §6 rule 6. Held paths: the techlead reads slices 1 and 3.

**Why:** TD-223's *Why*: the home reads a written line that dev-cadence's ledger never carries and that cadence §2.4 says is derived, so two tools give two answers and rule 6 is blind in dev-cadence.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The reader** (`sessionorc`): `entries` reads `**Blocked by:**` and `**Type:**`, takes the archive's ids from `<ledger>_archive.md`, and gives each entry `type`, `blocked_by` and `pickable` by §4.4's rule — an unknown id, an item it cannot read and an item after the decision's comma each keeping the block, the fields read from the header block as the script reads them, an empty line blocking nothing; a `<repo>#TD-NNN` item resolved in the registry's checkout of that repo (`Host.repos()`; the directory's name, or `owner/name` by its origin; `docs/technical_debt.md` and its archive at `origin/<default>`, else the working tree; archived there lifts it; open, unlisted, a name held twice or unfound keeps the block, and `blocked_by` lists the item as written), with a test on two scratch repos and one scratch registry that both readers are pointed at; the archive is the ledger's name with `_archive` before its suffix; a written `Pickable: no` still reads as blocked (the fallback, a parameter of `entries` that the equality test turns off). `kind_of` tests *for you*, *design-first*, *pickable*, *other*. `lane_matches` reads the derived field, with `build` for no Kind. A test holds the set of ids `entries` calls pickable equal to the script's on this repo's ledger, with the fallback off (`python3 scripts/ledger.py --list --json` prints every entry with its derived `pickable`, `type` and `blocked_by`, so the test compares the record and not only the id; `--pickable --json` prints `{pickable, blocked, flags}`), and a second covers a ledger with no header lines: every unblocked entry matches `free-pick`.
2. **The page and `ao repo`**: the lists and the bar from the new kinds; a blocked row says *blocked by TD-n*; `ao repo` lists a lane's pickable entries in cadence's order (Priority, then debt before feature).
3. **The migration and the briefs**, one PR, **not before the lanes carry their owner words** (TD-227 slice 1 built, and `org.yml` edited: the board line of 2026-09-28): every open entry's `**Pickable:**` line goes, its reason moved and never dropped. A dependency becomes `**Blocked by:** TD-n`; *designed, the build is TD-n* becomes `**Blocked by:** TD-n`; a wait on Paul becomes `decision (Paul)` with its pointer; *live check pending*, *for evaluation*, *the anchor's*, *Paul's* are the entry's `Kind:` or `Owner:` where the header already says so, and are **made so where it does not**: on 2026-09-28 about thirty entries were `Kind: build`, unblocked and written *no* — *built; the live look is left* (TD-146, TD-148, TD-152 and others) becomes `Kind: live-check`; *needs an attended run* or *one press in a browser* (TD-005, TD-006, TD-029) becomes `Owner: paul` or `anchor`; *a synced file* (TD-050, TD-061) `Owner: dev-cadence`; a wait on another repo or on a reading (TD-142, TD-201) a `Blocked by:` that names it. The clause moves to the Status where it says more. **The PR's body lists, per lane, the ids matched before and after**: `[free-pick, owner:grinder]` and `[design-first, owner:designer]` match after the migration what was written `yes` before it, every difference named and meant, so rule 6 has no burst to tell. The preamble's paragraph and `tests/test_ledger.py`'s `HEADER` lose the line. The briefs pick from `ao repo`, or from `scripts/ledger.py --pickable` filtered by Owner and Kind, and no brief names the line.
4. **The fallback goes**: `FIELD` stops reading `Pickable`; a ledger that still writes it is read as if it did not.

**Done when:** an entry that becomes unblocked in dev-cadence's ledger tells an idle, finished grinder-dc-1 with no edit to the entry; this ledger has no `**Pickable:**` line and the kind bar counts no design-first entry as pickable; the equality test and the header-less test pass.

**Related:** TD-223, TD-198, TD-211 (each closes with this), TD-227 (rule 8 and the `owner:` lane word, which slice 1 builds on when it is there), TD-118 (the header lines), dev-cadence's TD-070 and TD-064.

## TD-229: Build the org as an aggregate of the repos' own definitions

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-210's design, with TD-209)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **the switch, 2026-10-03 (the anchor, on Paul's word):** `.agentorc.yml`'s ao-grind brought level with `org.yml`'s first (its `entries:` and `owner:` lanes, PR #964), then `ao-grind` and the held lists taken out of `org.yml`; the live check is the team's next start from the repo's file. **Slice 1 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** `org.with_repos`, the one aggregate `ao team` and the pages read (the registry's checkouts, from any directory; `ao team`'s `-d` reads nothing now); a name two repos define refused in both (`Org.refused`, *refused:* in `ao team list`, `ao team start`'s reason, a note on the page); the org file's win recorded (`Org.shadowed`, *shadowed by org.yml*); `projects:`, `host:` and a nested member refused on a repo's team. **Slice 4 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** **Members…** drawn disabled on a repo's team with *defined in `<repo>`'s .agentorc.yml — changed by PR* and **Open file**; `edit_members` refuses a `.agentorc.yml`. **Slice 5 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #800):** `sessionorc.defs` (`init`, `commit`, `settings_message`); `ao service install` makes the home a work tree at the home alone (`service._home_history`); the commit after `set_settings`, the `commit_defs` RPC (`modes.HOME_EDITS`) that `teamrun.add_member` / `remove_member` call after `edit_members` wrote, and the hand-edit commit on the reports' cadence (`_defs_task`). **Slice 3's host-agent half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #801):** the home's `host_repos {host}` RPC (`agent_remote.py`) and the node's `repos` link method (`agent_link.py`); `place:` in the org file and the landing rule in `teams.plan` are left, grinder-ao-2's package. — a node on an older build answers `unknown link method 'repos'`, which the landing rule reads as *unknown*, never *no repos* (the review of #801). **The techlead's three notes on #800, settled 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1):** the commit after `set_settings` is detached from the reply (`_bg`, as the tick's is); `brief.read` reads a file under the home's own work tree from disk, whatever remote is added there; and §4.9 says the daily backup carries the three files, not their history. **From the techlead's read of #801 (merged 2026-09-30), for the clients' half:** (1) asked at a node, `host_repos` answers that node's own registry and refuses any other host (*is a node of <home>: … ask the home*), as `host_dir` does — the landing rule run from a node reads that refusal as *unknown*, as it reads an older node's *unknown link method 'repos'*, never as *no repos*; (2) `_from_home`'s docstring in `agent_link.py` lists ping, `act` and `stat` only — `repos` and `files` join it when the file is next touched. **Slice 2 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #802):** this repo's `.agentorc.yml` carries `teams: ao-grind` as the org file had it that day and `roles:` `grinder` and `designer` with their `review:` whole (the designer's brief beside it); the org file's `ao-grind` shadows it, so no team start changes (the New session form now lists `designer` for this repo), and `tests/test_org.py` holds the two equal. The switch is the board line of 2026-09-28 (*take `ao-grind` and the two `held:` lists out of `org.yml`*); after it, the board's owner words in the lanes and `entries: {feature: designer}` are edits to this file by PR, not to `org.yml`. Once the org file no longer defines `ao-grind`, a second registered checkout of agentorc would refuse the name in both (`with_repos`); the registry holds main checkouts only, so worktrees never do (the review of #802). **Slice 3's clients' half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** `place:` in the org file (`Org.place`; refused on a team the org file defines), the landing rule `org.landing` and `org._land`, run by `with_repos` for `ao team` and the pages alike (`teamrun.repos_via` over `host_repos`; the pages keep a host's registry five seconds, `ui/common.repos_of`): a placed team takes that host and the repo's path there is the one registry entry of the checkout's name; a registry that cannot be read, or holds the name not once, leaves the team listed and its start refused in those words (`Org.unlanded`, `teams.plan`). **Open question for the designer, found in the build:** the rule's third clause (*else the one linked node whose registry does*) is in `org.landing` and tested, and no client reaches it — every definition is read from a checkout in this host's registry, so that registry always holds the repo; how the home reads the `.agentorc.yml` of a repo held only on a node is not designed. The `repos` and `files` lines of `_from_home`'s docstring (the read of #801) are in (grinder-ao-1, PR #839). **Slice 6 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2):** `ao org` and `ao org check` (`agentorc.orgcheck` `view`, `check`, `remainder`; `cli.cmd_org`; `tests/test_cli_org.py`), §4.7 saying what the build chose. **Every slice is built. Left:** Paul's switch (the board line of 2026-09-28: `ao-grind` and the two `held:` lists out of `org.yml`), the designer's open question above, and `ao org check` on kmaster after a promote (the board, 2026-09-30) — the entry archives when *Done when* holds. **Next:** every slice is built; what is left is Paul's switch and the live check after a promote (the board), and the designer's open question.
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — his `org.yml` switch and the live check after a promote, both on the board, and the designer's open question; every slice a grinder can build is built
**Location:** `src/agentorc/org.py` (`load`, `merge_repo_teams`, `_team`, `edit_members`), `src/agentorc/teams.py` (`plan`, `project_of`, `reach_block`, `_launch`), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao team list`, `ao org`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (the Org page's team cards, the Members dialog, the New session form's Team pick, the Settings page's file cards), `src/sessionorc/hosts.py` (`repos`), `src/sessionorc/settings.py` and the `set_settings` RPC (the commit), a link method for a node's registry, this repo's `.agentorc.yml`, `tests/`; design §4.9 *The org is an aggregate*, *What is left at the home has a history*, §4.7 `ao org`. Held path: the techlead reads the slices that touch `src/sessionorc/**`.

**Why:** TD-210's *Why*, and Paul's answer to it: a team's definition belongs with the repo it works, versioned and reviewed there, and the home should hold only what no repo can.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The aggregate**: `ao team` reads `teams:` from every checkout in the host's registry, as the pages already do (`teamrun.org_with_repo_teams`), and one function serves both; a repo's team takes the repo as its project (name the directory's, path the registry's; an org project of that name is used as it stands) and a `projects:` key, a `host:` and a nested `{team: …}` member on it are each refused; two repos naming one team are both refused, each naming the other (today `merge_repo_teams` tests the org file alone, so the later repo wins silently); the org file wins a name and the repo's reads *shadowed*. `ao team list`, the Org page and the New session form read the one aggregate. Nothing in `org.yml` has to change for this slice: what it defines still wins.
2. **This repo's definitions**: `.agentorc.yml` (created here, or by TD-132 slice 4 if that lands first) holds `teams: ao-grind` as the org file has it today (the manager, the techlead seat, the two grinders and the designer, their lanes and briefs; no `host:`, and no `profile:` the overlay already gives) and `roles:` `grinder` and `designer` with `review: {reader: techlead, held: [src/sessionorc/**, docs/briefs/**]}`, whole: `resolve_role` replaces a layer's `review:` and `normalize_review` refuses one with no reader. While the org file still defines `ao-grind` it shadows this one, so the PR changes nothing that runs; Paul's edit of the org file (the board line) is the switch.
3. **`place:` and a node's registry**: `place: {<team>: <host>}` in the org file; the landing rule of §4.9; a link method that returns a node's registered checkouts, asked when the home's registry lacks the repo.
4. **Members…**: disabled on a repo-defined team with its reason and **Open file**; `edit_members` refuses a source that is not the org file.
5. **The history**: at the home, `~/.agentorc` initialised as a git work tree by `ao service install` when it is not one (an ignore file that tracks `org.yml`, `profiles.yml` and `settings.yml` alone; never on a node). The host agent is the one committer: after `set_settings`, on `commit_defs {message}` (a person's own, in `modes.HOME_EDITS`; the UI calls it after `edit_members` wrote), and on the reports' cadence for a hand edit; a file that does not parse is not committed; no remote is added and nothing is pushed; a commit that fails is logged and never fails the write.
6. **`ao org` and `ao org check`**, and tests: two registered checkouts each defining a team give both; the same name twice refuses both; the org file shadows; a repo's team with `projects:` is refused; a team lands on the node that holds its repo and is refused with two; a repo's team with `host:` is refused; `set_settings` leaves one commit and a failed commit leaves the setting written; a half-written `org.yml` is not committed and the next whole one is; `hosts.yml` is never tracked; `ao org check` exits 1 on a registered checkout that is missing and on a settings team nothing defines, and warns on a checkout off its default branch.

**Done when:** with `ao-grind` removed from the org file the team starts from this repo's `.agentorc.yml` with the same names, lanes, briefs and held paths; grinder-dc-1's record holds dev-cadence's own held paths or none; `git -C ~/.agentorc log -p org.yml` shows a Members… press; and `ao org check` passes on kmaster.

**Related:** TD-210 (the design), TD-209 (the held paths), TD-172 (Members…), TD-146 (`settings.yml`), TD-132 slice 4 (this repo's `.agentorc.yml`, which whichever lands first creates), TD-222 (whether a write-back is ever pushed).

## TD-230: The usage chip shows a stale reading with no age, and the gate acts on it as if fresh

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul: *our percentage used for the claude sub seems to be stale — our page reads 88%/95% but actual is 93% used*; then: *add how long ago the reading was made in the display*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Blocked by:** TD-233
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #734; the steer to Paul is `m-3cb2968e75c3`, bound 2026-09-29 11:24 MDT): design §4.5a **usage** chip (*The age*), §6 *Usage gate* (*A reading the gate can no longer trust*), §5 `usage_gate.max_age`, §4.7 `ao gate`, the Settings page's Usage row. The Fix's three: (1) the age on the chip past five minutes, *unknown since* past three hours; (2) the gate projects from the rate it last saw and pauses on the projection, resuming only on a reading; (3) the Settings card says the age. Closes with TD-233. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-233.
**Location:** design §4.5a **usage** chip, §4.2 (*usage*: keeps the last good reading), §6 *Usage gate*; `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_usage`), `~/.agentorc/usage.json` (`fetched`), the gate (`ao gate`, `settings.lines`)

**Why:** on 2026-09-28 the chip read *week 88% · stale* while the account was at 93% (Paul, from the account's own page). The held reading's `fetched` was 22:21Z, and every poll since 22:26Z had been refused (TD-231), so by 04:27Z it was six hours old. The chip says *stale* but not how stale, so a person can't tell five minutes from six hours. The gate reads the same reading: with a 5/day week reserve and one day left its line was 95%, so it saw 88% and paused nobody. A stale reading can't show the gate a crossing, and the real account was two points from the line with four agents working and 19 hours to the reset. Paul accepted the risk for the night.

**Fix:** design (1) **the age on the chip**: *week 88% · 6 h ago* (and in `ao gate`'s line), with a threshold past which it reads plainly *unknown since 22:21* (the hover already gives when the reading was taken and why the poll failed, §4.5a; the new part is the age on the chip itself and in `ao gate`); (2) **the gate on a stale reading**: past a set age (e.g. an hour, a setting), the gate no longer treats the held reading as the truth. Either it projects it forward by the rate the account was rising at while readings were fresh (`usage.json` holds only the latest reading per profile, so a short history of readings would need keeping), or it pauses unattended work once the held reading plus a margin reaches the line, or it asks the person with an Inbox row, *usage unknown for 3 h; keep going?*. The round picks, maybe per window. (3) The Settings page's Usage card says the same age. Then the build. Done when a six-hour-old reading says so on the chip, and the gate does not run an account past its line on a reading it can't trust.
