**Related:** TD-231 (why the readings stopped), TD-122 (one poll per account; its live check failed on 2026-09-28), TD-087 (the reason on a refused poll), TD-100 (the gate), TD-189 (context bounds; the same *measure, then act* shape).

## TD-231: The usage endpoint answers 429 for hours though agentorc polls once per account

**Priority:** High
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (Paul: *investigate the 429s — we may need to research anthropic's best practices for retrieving usage programmatically*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Blocked by:** TD-233
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-28 (the designer, PR #734), on the research below and its recommendation: design §4.4 *Usage, reported first and asked for last* and §4.3 `usage_report`. The Fix's step (2), measuring whose calls spend the endpoint's allowance, is not done and is no longer needed to choose: the design stops depending on the endpoint. Closes with TD-233. **Research done 2026-09-28** (the anchor's Sonnet pass, read-only, the endpoint not called; Claude Code 2.1.284). Findings: (1) **no documented way to read a Pro/Max subscription's limits.** The Usage & Cost Admin API needs an admin key, is *unavailable for individual accounts* and reports tokens and cost for API orgs (platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/usage-cost-api); `/api/oauth/usage` is undocumented. (2) **The statusline gets them, documented**: its stdin JSON carries `rate_limits.five_hour` and `rate_limits.seven_day`, each `{used_percentage, resets_at}`, for Pro/Max only, after the session's first API response, any window possibly absent (code.claude.com/docs/en/statusline). OpenTelemetry has no rate-limit metric; hooks were not found to carry it. (3) **The 429s are widely reported** for Max accounts even at five-minute polling, lasting hours, with `Retry-After` 0 or absent and no Anthropic reply (anthropics/claude-code #30930, #31637 closed *not planned*, #31021). So agentorc's doubling back-off can't learn the window. (4) Model responses carry `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-5h-utilization`, `-7d-utilization` and their resets, named only in the client binary (undocumented). (5) **The installed client calls the same endpoint itself** (`fetchUtilization`, with `?at_wall=1&skip_spend=1` variants; the trigger was not found), so the sessions on the account plausibly share its allowance with agentorc. **The recommendation, for the design round:** stop polling on a timer. The primary source is an agentorc-managed statusline command in each session it launches, set in the adapter's `--settings` layer and chained so a person's own statusline still runs. It writes the `rate_limits` it receives, with a timestamp, to a per-profile file under the home, and the host agent takes the freshest per account and shows its age (TD-230). That is zero extra traffic, fresh whenever any session is active, which is when the gate matters. The endpoint stays only as a fallback on demand: at most once per 15 minutes per account, a fixed hour's cool-off after a 429 and nothing during it. Caveats: an idle account gives a stale reading (TD-230's rule covers it), and the statusline fields need a client version that has them (v2.1.80 and later, per #31637), a window is dropped once its `resets_at` passes, and nothing is there before a session's first API response. The per-token-not-per-account reading of the limit in #30930 and #31637 is community speculation. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-233.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py` (`USAGE_URL = https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage`, the `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20` header, `usage_for`, `UsageRefused`, `_retry_after`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_usage`: one poll per account, `USAGE_EVERY` 300 s, the doubling back-off to `USAGE_BACKOFF_MAX` 3600 s), design §4.2 (*usage*)

**Why:** TD-122 (built 2026-09-23, #517) cut the polls to one per account, because four profiles on one login had been refused. The refusals are back. The agent's log on 2026-09-28 reads *rate_limited (was ok)* at 16:26 MDT and *rate_limited (was no reading yet)* at 21:26 after a restart, with the last good reading at 22:21Z. The endpoint then refused every poll for six hours or more. agentorc asks at most every five minutes, less once backed off. So either the endpoint's allowance is far tighter than that, or something else on the account spends it: every Claude Code client may read the same endpoint for its own `/usage` and status, and on this account a dozen sessions run at once. The endpoint is not a documented API: agentorc reads it as Claude Code does, with the user's OAuth token and a beta header.

**Fix:** (1) **research** (Sonnet, read-only, with sources): whether Anthropic documents a supported way to read a Pro/Max subscription's usage programmatically (and if not, what the supported surfaces are: the Claude Code `/usage` command, the statusline's input JSON if it carries rate-limit fields, response headers such as `anthropic-ratelimit-*` on the model calls a session already makes); the endpoint's limits if stated anywhere; what the Claude Code client polls and how often; whether a `Retry-After` is sent (the agent's log records only the reason's transitions, so this needs the headers logged). (2) Measure here: count this account's calls to the endpoint across the sessions (is it us, or them?) and log the response headers of a refusal. (3) Then choose, in the design round the research makes needed: read usage from a supported surface (e.g. the headers or statusline data the sessions already receive, reported through the hook), poll less and share the reading, or stop polling while any session can report it. Done when the chip holds a reading under an hour old through a busy evening, and the source is one Anthropic supports or tolerates.

**Related:** TD-230 (the age on the chip, the gate on a stale reading), TD-122 (one poll per account), TD-087 (the reason), TD-073 (the reading), design §4.2, §4.2a.

## TD-233: Build usage reported first, its age, and the gate's projection

**Priority:** High
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-28 (the designer, from TD-231's and TD-230's designs)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Built, live check pending — **slice 1 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-2):** the age on the chip (`usage_chip` / `AO.usageChip`, `USAGE_AGED`, `USAGE_FRESH`, `USAGE_UNKNOWN` in `ui/common.py` and `app.js`; the chip drawn again each minute from `data-usage`), `ao gate`'s *· read 6h ago (asked)* (`_gate_read`, from the `usage` RPC beside `gate`), and the Settings card's age line (`settings_page.reading_age`); *stale* gone. No held path. **Slice 3 built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #768):** `_refresh_usage` asks an account only when its reading is older than `USAGE_FRESH` (900 s, `_usage_young`) and at most once per `USAGE_FRESH`; a 429 cools it off `USAGE_COOL` (an hour), a longer `Retry-After` kept, and its end (`cool_until` on the reading) held across a restart; the doubling, `USAGE_EVERY` and `USAGE_BACKOFF_MAX` went. **A window past its reset pauses nothing, built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #776, ahead of slice 4, at the anchor's ask):** `settings.lines` marks the row `unknown: "reset"` and `crossed` skips it. **Slice 2's host-agent half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #770):** the `usage_report` RPC (`agent_wake.py`, the caller the session), `sessionorc.usage` (`merge`: reports by the highest inside one reset, the endpoint's answer outright, resets within `RESET_SLACK` one window; `asked_only_due`, the hourly ask for an endpoint-only window, `USAGE_ONLY_EVERY`), each window's `at`, `source` and `history` (one point per ten minutes, three hours). **Slice 2's adapter half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #780):** `agentorc-hook --statusline` (`hook.statusline`: `report_due`, a report on a change or once a minute, `fresh` from `cost.total_api_duration_ms`, the last sent in `statusline/<session>.json` under the home, one second's timeout, nothing queued; then the displaced line with the same stdin), the adapter's `usage_report`, `displaced_status_line` and the layer's `statusLine` (`STATUSLINE_REFRESH`, the displaced `padding` in a `+pad<n>` layer); §4.4 *The poll, as built* says *while nothing reports* (the techlead's read of #770). **A node's report to the home built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #783):** the link method `usage_report {id, account, windows, fresh}` (`_forward_usage_report`, `_take_usage_report`), the node's account key remembered per node and profile (`_usage_remote_keys`), the home keeping and showing it (`_usage_remote_live`) and never asking for it. **The home's send built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #786):** `usage_reading {account, reading}` home → node (`_push_usage_readings`, `_usage_told`; the node's `_take_usage_reading` and `usage.adopt`, window by window), with the techlead's two notes on #783: a node's report under a profile metered at the home is taken, and a node's account is seeded from its profile's held reading after a home restart (the passes before its first report keep it). Slice 2 is done. **From the techlead's read of #786, for when the entry next moves:** (1) a node's endpoint answer never reaches the home (only reports are forwarded), so for an account only the node asks for, a number the endpoint lowered at the node is overwritten by the home's higher, report-merged number at the next fresh report — it errs toward pausing early; say so in §4.4 or forward the node's answer; (2) `adopt` compares `at` alone inside one window, so a number the home raised from a report that was not fresh is sent and not taken — rare, and it errs the other way; (3) `_push_usage_readings` sits on the reply path of a home session's `usage_report` RPC, so a wedged link holds each reply up to `REPORT_WRITE` per node against the status line's one-second timeout — detach it, as the node's forward is. **Slice 4's host-agent half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #781):** `usage.max_age` (`settings.parse_max_age`, `max_age`, `usage`; `set_settings usage=`), the one reader `usage.project` (`rate`, `RATE_SPAN`, `RATE_MIN`) behind `_gate_windows` for the gate, `_profile_gated` and `gate`, the mark's `projected`, a pause held while its window is unknown for want of a rate, and the two notes (`_projection_notes`). **Slice 4's client half built 2026-09-29 (grinder-ao-1, PR #789):** `ao gate --max-age <age|off|default>` (`_max_age_said`), the projected window's print (`now 88% · … · projected 96%`, *· no rate to project by*), the Settings page's **trust a reading for** (one field above the accounts: `usage.max_age` is one key; `POST /api/settings/max_age`) and a reserve's line carrying the projection. **The chip's *projected* built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #791):** `usage_chip` and `AO.usageChip` read the gate's row (`_projected` / `projectedOf`) — *week 88% · 6h · projected 96% / 95%*, the worst window and the colour from the projection, a projected reading never *unknown since*. Slice 5's tests are in with slices 2–4. **Left:** the live half of *Done when* (the board line, due 2026-10-03), then archive this entry with TD-230 and TD-231 **Next:** built; what is left is the live check.
**Location:** `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/__init__.py` (`hooks_settings`: the `statusLine` key; `usage_report`; `usage_for`, `UsageRefused`), the hook command (`src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/hook.py`, `agentorc-hook --statusline`, built), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_usage`, `_profile_gated`, the gate's pass, `USAGE_FRESH` and `USAGE_COOL`, which `agent.py` imports), `src/sessionorc/agent_wake.py` (a `usage_report` RPC beside `rpc_hook`; `set_settings`, `_reserves_change`), `usage_store` and `usage.json`, `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (a fifth key, `usage.max_age`), `src/sessionorc/agent_link.py` and `agent_remote.py` (the report, node to home, and the reading, home to node, built), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao gate`), `src/agentorc/ui/` (the chip, the Settings page's Usage card), `tests/`; design §4.4 *Usage*, §4.3, §4.5a **usage** chip, §6 *Usage gate*. Held path: the techlead reads the slices in `src/sessionorc/**`.

**Why:** TD-230's and TD-231's: a six-hour-old reading ran the account to within two points of its line unseen, because the one source was an endpoint that refuses for hours.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The age, first, since it needs no new source**: every reading's `at` reaches the chip and `ao gate`; the chip prints the age past five minutes, dims past `USAGE_FRESH`, reads *unknown since … (was n%)* past `USAGE_UNKNOWN`; the word *stale* goes from the chip (`ui/help.py` does not name it); the Settings page's Usage card shows age and source.
2. **The report**: `hooks_settings` sets `statusLine` to `agentorc-hook --statusline` with a refresh of sixty seconds; the command reads stdin, takes `rate_limits` through the adapter's `usage_report` (the percentage a float, the reset epoch seconds; `fresh` from the payload's running totals of API work, which the build names after reading a real payload), sends it as the `usage_report` RPC when a number or a reset changed or sixty seconds passed (a small file per session under the home holds the last sent), with a timeout of a second and no queue, then runs the status line it displaced (local, project, then profile settings; its padding kept) with the same stdin and prints its output, nothing when there is none; it never fails the status line: any error is swallowed and the chained command still runs. The host agent merges per account and window (the highest inside one `resets`, a later `resets` outright, `at` moved only by a fresh report or an answer) and keeps the newest reading and one per ten minutes for three hours; a node sends reports on to the home by a link method of its own, dropped when the link is down. A payload with no `rate_limits` (an old client, a metered profile, before the first response) reports nothing.
3. **The endpoint as the fallback**: `_refresh_usage` asks only for an account with a live session and no reading younger than `USAGE_FRESH`, at most once per `USAGE_FRESH`, and not for `USAGE_COOL` after `rate_limited`; the doubling back-off goes; it is asked by the host holding the profile's credentials; a window only the endpoint gives is asked for once an hour while a reserve names it or it stood within ten points of its cap; a window past its `resets` reads unknown and is never a cap for `limited`.
4. **The projection**: `usage.max_age` (`set_settings`, `ao gate --max-age`, the Settings field; `off` allowed); **one reader** gives a reading or a projection to the gate's pass and to `_profile_gated` alike; past `max_age`, for a profile with unattended sessions live and a window whose `resets` is ahead, it reads held + rate × age, at most 100, the rate between the oldest and newest kept readings inside two hours, never below zero and none under a span of twenty minutes; the gate pauses on a projection that reaches the line (a team's reserve priority counted), the mark and one `system` note saying so; an unknown window or no rate pauses nothing and notes *usage unknown*, once per account per day, never under `off`; a pause on a projection ends on a reading under the line, on the window's reset, or on `max_age` or a reserve moved.
5. **Tests**: a reading of 88% taken six hours ago with a rate of one point an hour and a line of 95% does not pause, and with two points an hour does; no history, or one spanning ten minutes, pauses nothing and files one note; a fresh reading under the line resumes and a projection never does; a pause on a projection lifts when the window's reset passes; a profile paused on a projection is not restarted by the keep-running pass; a report that is not fresh moves no age; two sessions reporting 61.5 and 60.0 inside one reset read 61.5; a project's own status line is the one chained when the profile names another; the status-line command with a person's own command prints that command's output and reports once for ten redraws inside a minute; a payload without `rate_limits` reports nothing and breaks nothing; after a 429 the endpoint is not asked for an hour; a report from a node reaches the home's chip; a window past its reset reads unknown.

**Done when:** through a busy evening the chip's reading is under fifteen minutes old with the endpoint asked at most a handful of times, a reading that does go old says its age on the chip and in `ao gate`, and the gate pauses the team on a projection rather than run the account past its line.

**Related:** TD-230, TD-231 (the designs and the research), TD-122 (one poll per account), TD-087 (the reason on a refusal), TD-100 (the gate), TD-201 (the unattended layer's settings).

## TD-239: Build the balance rule — a team over its line takes no new claim

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-29 (the designer, from TD-177's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #772):** `parse_balance` and `balance` in `TEAM_KEYS`; `sessionorc/balance.py` (`crossed`, `span`); the tick's `_balance_marks` writing `balance: {since, repo, crossed}` on the home's `host` record, which is `host.json` (`HostStore`, `paths.host_file`); `rpc_repos` and the `repos` event carrying `balance: {<team>: mark}` on each checkout's reading (`_repo_view`). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #796):** `rpc_progress` refuses a new declared claim (and `_ending` a `none`) by an unattended non-seat member of a marked team, `--force` included, in `balance.refusal`'s words; the mark read by team from `_host_rec` (`Agent._balance_of`, `_balance_refusal`); `balance_refused: {at, ref}` on the record; `_idle_nudge` and `_lane_news` pass it by while the mark stands; §6 now says a team with no registry root crosses no `prs`/`oldest` line (the techlead's read of #772). **Slice 3 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #798):** the crossing's and the clearing's `system` notes to the manager (`_balance_leads`: the controller the unattended non-seat members share) and the person (`balance.crossing`, `balance.clearing`, `_balance_tell`), `balance_told: {state, at, since}` on the team's `host` entry, the ten-minute flap (`balance.FLAP`), and the ring of each refused member (`_balance_ring`, `balance.CLEAR`), which removes `balance_refused`; a wound-down team is told nothing. With it, the first two of the techlead's notes on #796 below: §6 and `_balance_of` now say a node's member is checked at the home (its clearing note lands in the home's copy of its record, tested), and `balance_refused` is written only when the caller is the record; **open gap, from the techlead's read of #798:** nothing rings a node's idle member on the clearing — the note is read only at its next forwarded `inbox` or `wait` (§4.4a, TD-057), though the refusal told it to end its turn; the slice that writes the grinder's brief (5) or draws the card (4) covers it, and the manager, told of the clearing, can be the one to look; the third (a branch cut for a new entry is no way round the line) waits for slice 6's brief. **Rule 8's fifth bound built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #799):** `_work_held` ends with `_work_balance`, which reads the team's lines itself against every registry root the team's records name (and `review` against the ended seats' queue) and holds the start as `held: {why: balance, repo, crossed}` — a reading that cannot be told writes no new hold and lifts none, and an unchanged hold is kept with the crossing's numbers (the techlead's read of #799); so the note from TD-227 slice 4 is settled. **From the techlead's read of #799 (merged 2026-09-30), for a later slice:** a standing `balance` hold is lost whenever another reason takes `held` or strips it (a usage hold, `on_work` set to `ask` and back), so a failing PR reading at that moment starts the team — keep the balance hold beside the other if TD-227 slice 3's row shows it matters; and `tests/test_work_start.py`'s module docstring still said *four bounds* and the fifth-bound test's *against `work_waiting`'s repo* (both reworded 2026-09-30, grinder-ao-1). **For slice 4, from the techlead's read of #798:** (1) `_balance_leads` reads only the bottom layer of controllers, so a team of manager → g1 → w tells g1, and with a second grinder beside g1 tells only the person — a sentence in §6 if such teams are meant to exist; (2) a person removing `teams.<team>.balance` while the mark stands sends *under its line again*, which is not what happened — word it when the setting's page is built. **Slice 4's terminal half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2, PR #847):** `ao team balance` (`cli.cmd_team_balance`; the numbers as they read now from `teamrun.balance_now`), *over its line since …* in `ao team list` and at the end of `ao repo` (`teamrun.balance_marks`, `balance_note`), and the lines on `ao settings`' team row; a mark with an empty `repo` is in no `repos` reading, so the terminal does not say it — the `host` read would have to carry `balance` as it carries `work` (`src/sessionorc/agent_wake.py`, grinder-ao-1's package) if that case matters. **Slice 4's page half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2, PR #850):** the team card's **over its line** note (`ui/repo.py` `team_groups` → `balance_note`, `group_head.html`, live teams only, from the marks of the `repos` reading), the Settings page's **balance** switch and three fields with `ao team balance`'s three lines under them (`settings_page.balance_card`, `teamrun.balance_rows` moved from `cli`; `/api/settings/teams` takes `balance` whole, `null` for off, written only when a Save moved it), and the **balance** help paragraph in §4.5a's list and `ui/help.py` — which words the second note of #798's read: turning the rule off while a mark stands is told as the line clearing; tests in `tests/test_ui_settings.py` and `tests/test_ui_team_summary.py`; a live look after a promote is Paul's (the board). **Slice 5 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-2, PR #853):** `briefs/grinder.md`, `hunter.md` and `manager.md` say the rule (not `auditor.md`, which the Fix names: the auditor is a seat, and `_balance_of` never refuses a seat) (`tests/test_cli.py`), the grinder's with the branch-is-no-way-round sentence and the manager's with the one send to an idle node member after the clearing (the open gap from #798's read, until TD-057 rings it). **Every slice is built. Left:** Paul's live look after a promote (the board, due 2026-10-02), and three notes kept above for whoever meets them: a balance hold lost to another `held` reason, `_balance_leads` on a nested team, and a mark with an empty `repo` said nowhere but the refusal; **slice 6's tests are in with slices 1–3 (checked 2026-09-30, grinder-ao-1)**, `tests/test_balance.py`, each case of the Fix's list among them. **From the techlead's read of #796 (merged 2026-09-30):** `progress` is in `modes.REPORTS`, so a node forwards it and the home refuses a node's member too — §6's last sentence (*a claim made at a node's agent is not checked*) and `_balance_of`'s docstring are wrong; say it is checked at the home, and test the clearing's ring for a node's member (TD-103 if it needs the pane). Also from that read: anyone's refused write on a member's record (a person's `--id`) sets `balance_refused`, so write it only when the caller is the record if the ring should follow the member's own attempt; slice 6's grinder brief should say a branch cut for a new entry is no way round the line (a derived claim renews). **Next:** built; what is left is Paul's live look after a promote, and three notes in the Status for whoever meets them.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/settings.py` (`TEAM_KEYS`, `parse_team`, `teams`), `src/sessionorc/agent_wake.py` (`rpc_set_settings`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_refresh_repos`, the idle nudge, `_lane_news`), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`rpc_progress`, the `_ending` refusals), `src/sessionorc/agent_mail.py` (`_system_note`), `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`prs_waiting`, `review`), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao team`, `cmd_repo`), `src/agentorc/ui/settings_page.py` (`team_cards`) and `ui/templates/settings.html`, `ui/templates/group_head.html`, `src/agentorc/ui/help.py`, `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md`, `hunter.md`, `auditor.md` and `manager.md`

**Why:** TD-177's *Why*: the repo's numbers make a growing pull-request count visible and nothing acts on it, so the person finds out from the card once it is large.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The setting and the mark** (`sessionorc`): `balance` joins `TEAM_KEYS`; `parse_team` takes `{prs: <int ≥ 1>, oldest: <n>[mhd], review: <bool>}`, each optional, an empty mapping refused, an unknown key refused by name. After each repo reading and on each tick the home reads the lines of every team that has the key — the open pull requests of `prs.open`, drafts included as the card counts them, the oldest's `created`, and the seat's `prs_waiting.oldest` against the shortest `review.bound` of the team's live members (two hours where none) — and writes or removes `balance: {since, repo, crossed: [{line, value, limit}]}` on its `host` record under the team's name. A reading carrying `error` changes nothing. The team's repo is the registry root its live members' records name (`repo`); a team with no live member loses its mark. The `host` record is rule 8's (TD-227): whichever of the two builds lands first makes it. The mark is served with the `repos` reading (`rpc_repos`, `src/sessionorc/agent_wake.py`), which is where the page and `cmd_repo` read it.
2. **The refusal** (`sessionorc`): `rpc_progress` refuses a declared `claimed` by a record that is unattended and no seat and whose team carries the mark, `force` or not, in the design's words with the mark's numbers; a renewal of a reference the record holds, a pull-request reference, `done`, `dropped`, `restart` and every interactive record pass. `none` is refused to such a record in the same words. The record keeps `balance_refused: {at, ref}` so the clearing can ring it; the idle nudge and rule 6's telling skip a record that carries it while the mark stands.
3. **The notes**: on a crossing and on its clearing, one `system` note to the controller the team's members share and one to the person, FYI; on the clearing, one to each record carrying `balance_refused`, which is then removed. A mark that flaps inside ten minutes sends one pair, not several.
4. **The page and the terminal**: the team card's **over its line** note from the mark; the Settings page's **balance** switch and fields with the numbers as they read now; `ao team balance`; the line in `ao team list` and `ao repo`. The help entries for the new controls are added to §4.5a's help list and `ui/help.py` together (bound by `tests/test_help.py`); their wording is this slice's.
5. **The briefs**: the worker presets say what a refusal *over its line* means — take nothing new, finish, rebase or answer what is open of yours, end the turn, and never declare `none` on it; the manager preset says a team over its line is neither crashed nor finished, and that its part is one log line.
6. **Tests**: nine open pull requests against `prs: 8` refuse a new claim and eight do not; a draft is counted; a seat's claim passes; a claim on `#712` passes; a renewal passes; `--force` is refused; an interactive member's claim passes; `none` is refused while the mark stands and taken once it goes; a failed reading leaves a standing mark standing and writes none; `review: true` with an `ask` carrying `pr` three hours old at the seat and a bound of `2h` crosses; a team with no key is never marked; the clearing rings a refused member once.

**Done when:** on a scratch home with `ao team balance <team> --prs 1` and two open pull requests in the scratch repo, a member's `ao progress claim` is refused with the numbers, the card reads *over its line*, the manager's inbox holds one note, and closing one pull request clears the mark within a repo reading and rings the member.

**Related:** TD-177 (the design), TD-176 (the numbers), TD-227 (rule 8, which gains the fifth bound when both are built; whichever lands second adds it), TD-093 (the reader and its bound), TD-100 (the usage gate, the precedent for a line a person sets).

## TD-245: A restart at an entry's end, past the context bound, is marked early and left to the person; and 200k is under one entry's cost here

**Priority:** High
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (Paul, on grinder-ao-2's 20 idle hours: *would it make the most sense to have the grinder restart on a TD boundary after 200K (or larger if that is determined) instead of during a TD? That handles both the large case (restarts after single TD) and small case (restarts after n TDs)*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-30 (the designer, PR #822; the steer to Paul is `m-1747ebd23a10`, bound 2026-10-01 07:25 MDT): design §4.9a *Inside the ceiling* (early from the record; repeated work; what counts toward the ceiling), §4.8 *The bound has two layers*, §6 rules 1 and 5, §4.5a **Inbox row: restart**. The Fix's five points as written, with two decisions the round made: a wanted restart that carried new work does not count toward the ceiling, and the package default goes to 300k on TD-189's break-even while this repo's own bound waits on the measurement in TD-249 slice 4. Closes with TD-249. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-249.
**Blocked by:** TD-249
**Location:** design §4.9a (*Inside the ceiling*: `RESTART_EARLY`, `restart_wanted.early`), §6 rule 2 and rule 5; `src/sessionorc/agent_common.py` (`RESTART_EARLY`), `agent_tick.py` (`_wanted_restart`), where `ao progress restart` writes `early`; `src/agentorc/repoconfig.py` (`WORKER_CONTEXT = {"bound": "200k"}`); this repo's own part: `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-2.md` (the reading rule) and `.agentorc.yml` `roles.grinder.context` (this repo's bound) — the package template `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md` is not where either goes

**Why:** the boundary restart Paul describes is what TD-189/TD-190 built: past the bound a member is told *finish the entry in hand, push, then `ao progress restart`*, and rule 2 restarts it. Two things defeat it. (1) **`early` reads a run that finished its entry fast as a run that never began.** A `restart` declared inside thirty minutes of the record's start carries `early: true`, and neither the tick nor a controller acts on one. grinder-ao-2's record on 2026-09-30: restarts at 00:58Z, 01:29Z, 02:14Z and 03:15Z, each a clean restart after merged work, then `restart_wanted` at 03:40Z — 25 minutes in, context 209k, TD-229 slices 1 and 4 merged (#778, #779), nothing unpushed — marked `early`. It sat idle until Paul had it restarted by hand at about 23:50Z, holding the team's remaining package, with TD-244 (High) merged and unclaimed for 11 hours, the manager waiting on Paul each round, and no Start on the card (a member wanting a restart is not finished). (2) **The bound is under what one entry costs here.** `docs/design.md` is 755 KB and `docs/technical_debt.md` 692 KB, each most of 200k tokens if read whole; grinder-ao-2's fresh run on TD-244, a docs-only entry, read 148k with its PR open. TD-189's own research put a fresh run's start-up at a median 342k weighted and the cost break-even *around 300k*; 200k was Paul's starting number. So a run crosses the bound inside its first entry, restarts after one entry every time, and when that entry is quick the restart is `early`.

**Fix — a design round, then the build:**
1. **`early` is decided from fields, not the clock alone.** A `restart` inside `RESTART_EARLY` is not early when the run did **new** work: the record holds a `progress` entry reported `done` since its start whose reference (the entry, or its PR) is not one an earlier run in the record's `restarts` window already reported `done`, or — where the same entry is being worked in slices — whose PR is a different one, merged. What stays early is the case the rule was written for — a run that is over before it began: no `done`, context under the bound. The context reading alone does not lift it: a run that read itself past the bound and finished nothing is the loop. The `why` is still never read.
2. **The loop guard is the ceiling and repeated work** (Paul, 2026-09-30: *should we also check that new work is being done, not repeated work?*). The ceiling (three restarts in two hours, TD-186's window) already goes to the person. Beside it: a run that restarts having reported nothing new — the same reference `done` again with no new merged PR, or a claim on the entry its last run claimed and did not finish, twice running — is the person's at once, with the Inbox row saying which entry repeats. The round says where the last runs' references are kept (the `restarts` entries are the natural place: `{at, why, done: [refs]}`), since a supersede carries that list and the `progress` entries are the new record's. It also says whether a run of one-entry restarts with new work each time counts toward the ceiling at all.
3. **The bound, in two layers.** The package default (`WORKER_CONTEXT`) is every repo's and moves only on evidence that is every repo's: TD-189's break-even (around 300k). What is particular to agentorc — a 755 KB design and a 692 KB ledger — goes in this repo's `.agentorc.yml` as `roles.grinder.context: {bound: …}`, not in the default. Measure first what a fresh grinder run reads before its first claim in this repo (brief, `ao --skill`, the primer, how much of design.md and the ledger) from the run logs under `~/.agentorc/runs/`, since a bound under start-up plus one entry restarts every entry.
4. **The reading at start-up is this repo's rule, so it goes in this repo's briefs** — `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-2.md`, the supplements filled into the template's *This repo's rules* (design §4.8, TD-114) — never in the package template, which every repo's grinders get: *never read design.md or the ledger whole — `scripts/ledger.py --pickable`, then the entry and the sections it names* (TD-118 holds the token-cost side). `docs/briefs/**` is a held path, so that PR waits for the techlead's read.
5. **Tests:** a restart 20 minutes in with a new `done` since the start is acted on by rule 2; one with no `done` is `early` and is not, context past the bound or not; one whose only `done` repeats the last run's reference is the person's and the row names the entry; the ceiling still stops a fourth in two hours.

**Done when:** a grinder that finishes an entry and declares `restart` past its bound is restarted by the tick within a tick however short the run was; a run that declares with nothing done, or with only what its last run already reported, is the person's; the package template and default say nothing particular to this repo; and a fresh run in this repo finishes at least one entry before its bound.

**Related:** TD-189 and TD-190 (the bound, its research and its build), TD-188 (the design), TD-186 (the ceiling's window), TD-103 (rule 2), TD-083 (`restart_wanted`), TD-118 (start-up reading), TD-246 (the person's restart), TD-240 and TD-241 (the card with no Start).

## TD-247: The manager's remaining jobs are mostly mechanical: move them to the tick, and make its judgement a seat on call

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (Paul: *if the manager is required for a team (wind downs, etc) we should probably default every team to having one. Ideally we would not need to and all of its required jobs could be scripted or given to the agent host, but let's investigate*; the anchor's read with two Sonnet readers, of the brief, design §6 and the code, nothing run)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-30 (the designer, PR #845; the steer to Paul on when the default flips is `m-7b65cae16909`, bound 2026-10-01 10:06 MDT): design §6 *What is left is judgement, and a seat holds it*, rules 10–12, rule 3 *A manager on call is a seat of this rule*, rule 4's owed clause and rule 6's dropped lease; §4.8 *session, not code*; §4.9 `manager.on_call`; §4.9b the `team` trigger; §4.5a the rows **cadence check failed**, **merged without its read**, **idle · open work** and the seat slot's words; the glossary. The Fix as written, with the round's decisions: the four jobs are three rules and a clause of rule 4, none typed by a session; the judgement is the `manager` role as a seat with a trigger of its own (the techlead holds no grant and stays the ladder); no permission allow-list, since an unattended launch skips permissions already; `control` stays on the seat's record across the close; on the default the designer steered *a standing manager until dc-grind has run on call once* and Paul chose the alternative (2026-10-01, answer 2 of `m-7b65cae16909`): on call is the default from the build, every team at its next Start; dc-grind's first run is TD-259's last slice as a first look, not a gate. Closes with TD-258 and TD-259. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-258 (the policies) and TD-259 (the seat).
**Blocked by:** TD-258, TD-259
**Location:** design §6 (*Keeping a team running*, *What stays the manager's*), §4.8 (*The manager is a session, not code*, *Waking a manager*, `manager: {role: person}`), §4.9a, §4.9b (the seat rule); `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` (*A round*, *Out of work*, *Escalate*), `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`, `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py`, `src/agentorc/org.py` (the default manager), `scripts/check_cadence.py`, `scripts/cadence_changes.py`

**Why:** what the read found. (1) **A manager is already every team's default and is not required**: a definition with no `manager:` gets one named `<team>-lead` (`org.py`), `manager: {role: person}` starts none, and supervision keys on no role or controller, so rules 1–7 and the balance rule act on a person-led team's members as on any. No team on kmaster is defined that way (four teams, four managers), so the shape is unproven. (2) **The one thing a person-led team cannot do is end**: nothing closes members that have declared and sit idle, so the team never reads wound down and rule 8 never starts it again; rule 9 does that and is TD-241, raised to High with this entry (Paul, 2026-09-30) and partly built. With a manager the same step hangs on its memory (TD-240), which is what left ao-grind with no Start that day. (3) **Of what the brief still gives the manager, four jobs read fields and scripts, not screens**: the cadence check on a member's PR (`check_cadence.py --pr N --json` gives a verdict per row), the held-path check (a PR merged on `src/sessionorc/**` or `docs/briefs/**` with no read from the seat), the relay of `docs/cadence-changes.md` entries once per member (`cadence_changes.py --json`, today `manager-ao-1.relayed.json`), and the chase of an owed outcome (rule 4 already names an entry the person handed). (4) **What needs a reader is occasional**: an `ask` addressed to the manager, a permission or a question outside an allow-list, a `stalled?` or *idle · open work* member read from its tail, the second reading of the ledger before a wind-down, and the prose of an escalation. For that a manager wakes every 25 to 60 minutes and mostly finds nothing: manager-ao-1 stood at 686k of context on 2026-09-30, its rounds reading *nothing needs a person*.

**Fix — a design round, in this order:**
1. **The four mechanical jobs as tick policies**, each a fixed line and an Inbox row on the second failure, none typed by a session: the cadence check when a member reports `done` with a `pr` or its PR merges; the held-path check on a merge; the relay per member per entry; the outcome chase as rule 4's own. The round says which run only on the home, what a node's member gets, and what the `review` row's *self-attested* means when no session reads the PR.
2. **The judgement that is left, as a seat on call** (§4.9b's shape, as the techlead is): filled by a trigger the tick computes — a member `stalled?` or *idle · open work* past its nudge, a pending permission or question on an unattended member, an `ask` addressed to the team's manager — and closed when it has answered. The round says whether that is the `manager` role with a trigger or the techlead seat with a wider brief, what holds `control` while no manager is live (a seat's grant, as now, or nothing), and whether a permission allow-list in the definition takes the common prompts off it.
3. **The default.** Whether a definition with no `manager:` should then mean the seat rather than a standing session, and what `manager: {role: person}` means beside it; and rule 3's fill ceiling, which groups seats by a shared controller and so gives each seat of a person-led team its own.
4. **A first look, not a gate** (as asked: *proof before the default moves*; Paul, 2026-10-01, chose to flip the default at the build): dc-grind's first run on call, whose needs are the most mechanical, watched, and what the seat could not do written in TD-259.

**Done when:** the design says, for each line of the manager brief's round, which tick policy or which seat trigger has it; TD-103's *done when* (a night with the brief silent) holds because the brief no longer lists those jobs; and a build entry carries the slices.

**Related:** TD-103 (the first move of policy to the tick: built, live check pending), TD-240 and TD-241 (finished as the home's reading), TD-214 and TD-227 (rule 8), TD-239 (balance), TD-245 and TD-246 (the early restart, the person's restart), TD-098 (seats with triggers), TD-118 (one model for every role, the round's cost).

## TD-249: Build early from the record, the repeated-work guard, and the bound's two layers

**Priority:** High
**Type:** debt
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (the designer, from TD-245's design)
**Owner:** grinder
**Kind:** build
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #846):** `_replay` writes `done: [{ref, pr}]` and `left: [ref]` on every `restarts` entry, a failed replay's too (`agent_tick._reported`: `done` since the record's `created`; `left` its declared claims not closed, a derived claim left out); nothing reads them yet. Its test is `tests/test_crash_restart.py` (slice 7's last two cases). **Slice 2 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #848):** the `restart` declaration reads the record — `agent_common._restart_reading`: early only inside `RESTART_EARLY` with nothing new `done`; `repeat: {ref}` with `early` when the run's `done` is all in earlier `restarts[].done` inside `RESTART_WINDOW`, or a claim it leaves is in the `left` of the last two entries — and the reply carries `decided`, which `ao progress restart` prints; `brief_changed` lifts both. Its test is `tests/test_restart_reading.py`. The row's and the card's words still read `early` alone (slice 6). **Slice 3 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #851):** one count, `agent_common._counted`, for `_window_full`, rules 1, 2 and 7's own counts and rule 8's `_work_replays` (`fills=False`): the entries inside `RESTART_WINDOW`, leaving out a `wanted` entry with no `error` whose `done` holds a pair no entry in the window before its own held (`_new_done`, the test the declaration uses). `_scheduled_start` still counts its own `start` entries. Its test is `tests/test_restart_ceiling_count.py`. Slice 5 built (PR #849). Slice 4's package half built (PR #852; the note is on its item), its measuring half open. **Slice 6 built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #857):** an early mark carries `decided`, the reading's words, and the Inbox row (`inbox.restart_mark`), the card's slot and hover, the Focus chip and `ao status -v` print them, a repeat naming its entry; the repeat test reads the run's **declared** `done` only, so the earlier run's merge the tick derives onto a replayed record is not a repeat (the techlead's read of #848); the grinder and hunter templates say early is nothing new done. No help entry was added. **What is left:** slice 4's measuring half (the anchor's, or a session told it may read `~/.agentorc/runs/`), alone; slice 7's last case is built (PR #859, merged; its line is on the item). **Next:** built; what is left is slice 4's measuring half, the anchor's, and the live check after a promote.
**Blocked by:** decision (Paul) — the live check after a promote, on the board, and slice 4's measuring half, the anchor's; every slice a grinder can build is built
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the `restart` declaration: where `early` is written from `created` and `RESTART_EARLY`), `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_replay`: the `restarts` entry; `_wanted_restart`; `_window_full` and the inline ceiling counts), `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`restart_wanted`, `restarts`, `ProgressEntry`), `src/agentorc/ui/inbox.py` (`restart_mark`), `src/agentorc/ui/cards.py` (the *restart wanted · early* texts and the view's `early`), `ui/templates/focus.html` and `ui/static/app.js` (the chip), `src/agentorc/repoconfig.py` (`WORKER_CONTEXT`), `src/agentorc/briefs/grinder.md` line 16 (the early sentence), this repo's `.agentorc.yml` (`roles.grinder.context`) and `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md`, `grinder-ao-2.md` (held path: that PR waits for the techlead's read), `tests/`

**Why:** TD-245's *Why*: a run that finished its entry fast was marked `early` and sat idle twenty hours, and a 200k bound under one entry's cost in this repo restarts a grinder after every entry.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The record remembers the run it replaced** (`sessionorc`): `_replay` writes `done: [{ref, pr}]` (the old record's `progress` entries with `status: done` since its `created`) and `left: [ref]` (its declared claims not `done` or `dropped`) on the `restarts` entry it appends; a failed replay carries them too. The `restarts` comment in `models.py` says the shape.
2. **Early and repeat from the fields** (`sessionorc`): the `restart` declaration computes `new` = this run's `done` entries whose `{ref, pr}` pair (ref alone when `pr` is null) is in no `restarts[].done` inside `RESTART_WINDOW`; inside `RESTART_EARLY` it writes `early` only when `new` is empty; it writes `repeat: {ref}` with `early: true` when this run's `done` is non-empty and none of it is new, or when a claim it leaves is in the `left` of both of the last two `restarts` entries; `_wanted_restart`, the card and the row read `early` as today and the row's words come from `repeat`. The reply names what decided it. `brief_changed` still lifts early as today.
3. **The ceiling** (`sessionorc`): one counting function for `_window_full`, the inline counts and rule 8's `_work_replays`, leaving out `wanted` entries whose `done` holds something new; crash, fill, early and repeat entries count.
4. **The bound**: `WORKER_CONTEXT` to `300k`, and **300k the default for every role that sets no `context:`** (Paul, 2026-09-30) — `repoconfig` fills `context_bound` for a role with no `context:` key, a seat's record excepted, `context: none` still clearing it; `ao roles` prints *(default)* on a filled one; `ao roles` and the §4.8 and rule 5 sentences that say 200k change in the same PR (design-bound). The manager preset's brief gains the round's ending at the bound: `ao progress restart --why "context bound"` in place of `ao wait` once the round's sends are made and nothing owed is open. **The package half built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #852):** `WORKER_CONTEXT` and `DEFAULT_CONTEXT` are 300k; `resolve_role` fills a role no layer gave a `context:` and marks it `context_default`; a team start writes no default on a seat, and `ao new --role`, the New session form and Add entry's *Open a session* write one only on an unattended session (`Role.bound_for`: a person's own session takes none; a session with no role has no role); `ao roles` prints *(default)*; the manager template's round ends with `restart` at the bound. Tests: `tests/test_context_bound.py`, `tests/test_cli_teams.py`. **The measuring half is open** — it reads run logs under `~/.agentorc/runs/`, which a grinder's brief keeps it out of; it is the anchor's or a session told it may. Then **measure** in this repo — from the run logs under `~/.agentorc/runs/` and `ao transcript`, a fresh grinder run's reading before its first claim (brief, `ao --skill`, the primer, design.md, the ledger), three runs — and set `roles.grinder.context: {bound}` in `.agentorc.yml` to start-up plus one entry's median with room, with the numbers in the PR body and the design's two-layers paragraph gaining the measured figure.
5. **The reading rule in this repo's briefs**: `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md` and `grinder-ao-2.md` gain *never read design.md or the ledger whole — `scripts/ledger.py --pickable`, then the entry and the sections it names*; `docs/briefs/**` is a held path, so this PR waits for the techlead's read (`ao pr held`). **Built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #849):** the paragraph is under *First reads* in `grinder-ao-1.md`, and `grinder-ao-2.md` names it among what it follows.
6. **The words**: `inbox.py`'s `restart_mark` and `cards.py`'s early texts say what decided it; the grinder template's line 16 says an early restart is one with nothing new done, not one inside thirty minutes; the help entry for the row, if one is added, goes into §4.5a's help list and `ui/help.py` together.
7. **Tests**: a restart 20 minutes in with a new `done` since `created` is replayed by the tick within a tick; one with no `done` is early and is not, context over the bound or not; a `manager` record and an org-defined role with no `context:` carry `context_bound` 300k and a seat none; one whose only `done` repeats the last run's reference carries `repeat` and the row names the entry; a claim left by the last two runs and left again is a repeat; a crash replay's `restarts` entry carries `done` and `left` too; four clean restarts with new work in two hours do not reach the ceiling and three crashes do; a replay's `restarts` entry carries the old run's `done` and `left`. **Built 2026-09-30 (grinder-ao-1, PR #859):** the first two cases, end to end through the declaration and the tick, in `tests/test_wanted_and_nudge.py`; the others were pinned by slices 1–6's own tests (`tests/test_restart_reading.py`, `test_crash_restart.py`, `test_restart_ceiling_count.py`, `test_context_bound.py`; the row naming the repeated entry in `tests/test_ui_inbox.py`, PR #857).

**Done when:** a grinder that finishes an entry and declares `restart` past its bound is restarted by the tick within a tick however short the run was; a run that declares with nothing done, or with only what its last run already reported, is the person's with the row naming it; the worker presets carry 300k and this repo's `.agentorc.yml` its measured bound; the package template and default say nothing particular to this repo; a fresh run in this repo finishes at least one entry before its bound.

**Related:** TD-245 (the design), TD-189, TD-190 (the bound and its research), TD-186 (the ceiling's window), TD-083 (`restart_wanted`), TD-118 (start-up reading), TD-246 (the person's restart, designed next), TD-240 / TD-241 (the card with no Start).

## TD-254: A board row draws no answers at all — the answer buttons, *Go with it* as a steer has, and a live look's two answers

**Priority:** High
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (Paul, on the Inbox after TD-244's groom: *I see many agentorc items in the inbox that do not have recommended answers*, then: *let's add "go with it" similar to the steering entries. We should probably build some guidance on this to keep it consistent*)
**Owner:** designer
**Kind:** design-first
**Status:** Designed 2026-09-30 (the designer, PR #836; the steer to Paul is `m-68df9eaccb90`, bound 2026-10-01 09:12 MDT): design §4.4 *Board write-back* (**Decide**), §4.5a **Due strip / Inbox board row: answers, Go with it** and **Works / Not right…**, the Inbox **keys** row. The Fix's five points as written; the round offered the default's button first carrying its text; Paul chose a bare *Go with it* in the steer row's place with the default marked among the answers, and *Not right…* decides with the typed text in one write. Closes with TD-255. **Next:** designed; the build is TD-255.
**Blocked by:** TD-255
**Location:** design §4.5a (**Inbox row: `steer`** for the shape; the board rows' controls: Snooze, Done, Reply, Open board — no Decide row exists), §4.5 screen 6, §4.10; `src/agentorc/ui/templates/inbox_row.html` (the `board()` macro, which never calls `answers(e)`), `src/agentorc/ui/inbox.py` (`BOARD_ACTS = ("snooze", "done")`), the board RPC (no `decide` action), `ui/help.py` and §4.5a's help list (bound by `tests/test_help.py`); `scripts/board_edit.py` (SYNCED: `decide`); `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-*.md` and the package briefs, where they say how a live look is written

**Why:** after the groom the board holds 60 open items: 6 `decide`, each with `Answers:` and five with a `(default)`, 49 `watch` that are nearly all *merged, live look pending*, and 5 `act`. On the Inbox a steer shows the sender's one line and **Go with it**, and an `ask` its suggested answers as buttons. **A board row draws none of it**: its controls are Open board, Reply, Snooze and Done, the template never draws an item's `Answers:`, and neither the page nor the board RPC has a `decide` action, so the answers and the recommendation the groom wrote are invisible on the Inbox and the only way to record a `Decided:` is `board_edit.py decide` by hand. That is why Paul saw *many items that do not have recommended answers* the day after a groom whose point was to add them. A live look has the same four controls, so *I looked and it works* is Done with nothing recorded and *it does not* is prose in a Reply; 49 rows read as a wall with nothing to press. The guidance for what a session writes is the cadence's and is dev-cadence TD-074 (the fixed pair `Answers: Works | Not right: <what>.` on a live look, when a default is allowed); this entry is the Inbox's half.

**Fix — a design round, then a build entry:**
1. **The answer buttons on a board row** — the Decide control cadence §4.5 already allows a tool and this page never built: an item's `Answers:` drawn as the row's buttons, as an `ask`'s suggested answers are (display from the board's own fields, never a control built from prose), each press `board_edit.py decide --answer "<its text>"` through a `decide` action on the board RPC beside `snooze` and `done`, with the refusals `board_edit.py` already gives (the line moved, a board read from origin). The row then reads *decided: <text>* and stays until its session closes it.
2. ***Go with it* on a row that carries a `(default)`**: one press, in the steer's words and place, that records the default — the same `decide`, the person's action as every Decide is, never something the board or a session falls to. Say how it sits beside the answer buttons (the default's button first and marked, or *Go with it* in its place), and that a row with no default has no such button.
3. **A live look's pair as two buttons** once boards carry it: *Works* decides at once; *Not right…* opens the Reply composer and decides with the text. Say what follows each: *Works* is the raising team's order to close the line and archive the entry (rule 4's owed-outcome shape, or a board note to the manager), *Not right* a TD handed to the techlead as Add entry does (TD-218).
4. **This repo's existing live looks** get the pair in one pass by a grinder, as TD-244 did the kinds, once TD-074 has settled the words; and the briefs' *merged, live look pending* line is written with the pair from then on.
5. **Keys and help**: a key for *Go with it* on the ringed row beside `x` and `r`; the help sentences for both controls.

**Done when:** the design's §4.5a rows say all of it and a build entry carries the slices; on a scratch home a board item's answers are buttons on its Inbox row, one with a default is decided by one press, a live look by one of two, and the board line carries `Decided:` after each.

**Related:** dev-cadence TD-074 (the guidance and the pair), TD-244 (the groom), TD-142 (Reply on a board row), TD-218 and TD-219 (an entry handed to the techlead), TD-124 (the pages' keys); the steer's *Go with it*: §4.5a **Inbox row: `steer`**, §4.10.

## TD-255: Build a board row's answers — the decide write-back, the buttons, Go with it, a live look's pair

**Priority:** High
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (the designer, from TD-254's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2, PR #861):** `decide` in `board.ACTIONS`; `edit_line` writes `Decided: <text> (<date>)` at the line's end and refuses a line already decided or an answer the reader would not read back (`DECIDED_RE`, the reader's own); `message` is `agentorc: decide <head>: <answer> (session <name>)`; `rpc_board_edit` takes `answer` and `answers` and refuses any answer that is not one of them word for word, or `Not right:` with words where the pair is among them (`_board_answer`). A reply is written ahead of the line's `Answers:` and `Decided:` fields (`_fields_start`): before this a reply on a line with `Answers:` was read by the reader as part of the last answer. Tests in `tests/test_board.py`, two of them reading the line back with `scripts/nudge_user_attention.py` itself. **Slice 2 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2, PR #863, merged; live look pending, on the board):** the board row carries the reader's `answers`, `default`, `decided`, `kind` and a `body` without the field tails (`board_rows`, `board_body`); `inbox_row.html`'s `board()` draws the answer buttons, the default's mark, **Go with it** in the foot after Reply and *decided: <text> · <date>* in their place, disabled on a row read from origin; `decide` joins `BOARD_ACTS` and the route hands `answer` and `answers` to `board_edit`; `g` and `1`–`4` in `AO.KEYS` (`AO.keyAnswers`, the digits yielding only on a row with answer buttons — an `ask`'s too); help entries `board-answers` and `board-go-with-it` in §4.5a's list and `ui/help.py`. Tests in `tests/test_ui_board.py` (one end to end through dev-cadence's reader and the real write-back) and `tests/test_ui_keys.py`. Slices 3–4 open and **waiting on dev-cadence TD-074** (checked 2026-10-01: neither `docs/cadence.md` nor the synced scripts carry the pair's words, `Answers: Works | Not right: <what>.`, so there is nothing to draw by its words and nothing to regroom to) — **dev-cadence TD-074 is resolved (seen by the anchor 2026-10-01: its PRs #186, #187 and #193 merged, the last at 13:30Z, and dev-cadence's `files/docs/cadence.md` §3.5 carries the pair); what slices 3–4 wait on now is the sync PR that brings it here**, **synced 2026-10-01 by the anchor from dev-cadence `b90272e`: `docs/cadence.md` §3.5 and `scripts/nudge_user_attention.py` now carry the pair's words, so slices 3–4 wait on nothing**. **Slice 3 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2, PR #896; live look pending, on the board):** `inbox.live_look` — a `watch` whose two answers are *Works* and the form *Not right: <what>* — rides the row as `pair` with the item's `head` and the checkout's `name`; `inbox_row.html` draws **Works** and **Not right…** by their words, a default on *Works* as *Go with it: Works* (which `g` presses, with none in the foot); `app.js`'s `board_notright` opens the composer with *Not right:* begun (`AO.compose`'s `text`), sends one `decide`, then `/api/entry/hand` with type `debt` — the designed `bug` is no word of `entry_add`'s; §4.5a now says `debt` — and *<head> — Not right: <what>*, a refusal toasted; `_board_answer` takes an offered answer word for word before the form (`board.NOT_RIGHT_FORM`; the review of #863); `briefs/manager.md` step 3 has the round read `decided` and tell the holder of a *Works*. Tests in `tests/test_ui_board.py` and `tests/test_board.py`. **Open from slice 3:** a team whose manager is on call has no round, so nothing tells the holder of a decided *Works* there — the raiser reads its own line at its next start (cadence §3.5); and the pair has no help entry (each button's `title` says what it does). **Slice 4 done 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2):** the board pass (PR #897) and the briefs' live-look line (PR #899); slice 5's tests are written with each slice. **Every slice is built. Left:** Paul's live look after a promote (the board). **Next:** Paul's live look after a promote, on the board; every slice a grinder can build is built.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/board.py` (`ACTIONS`, `write_back`, `_write_back`: the `decide` action calling `scripts/board_edit.py decide --answer`), `src/sessionorc/agent_inbox.py` (`rpc_board_edit`), `src/agentorc/ui/inbox.py` (`BOARD_ACTS`, the board row view: `answers`, `default`, `decided`, `kind` from the reader), `src/agentorc/ui/templates/inbox_row.html` (the `board()` macro; the `answers(e)` macro as the model), `src/agentorc/ui/static/app.js` (the keys `g`, `1`–`4`; the Not right… composer), `src/agentorc/ui/help.py` and §4.5a's help list (bound by `tests/test_help.py`), `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the API route), `src/agentorc/briefs/*.md` and `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-*.md` (the live-look line; `docs/briefs/**` is a held path), `tests/`

**Why:** TD-254's *Why*: the groom's `Answers:` and defaults are invisible on the Inbox and nothing on the page records a decision; 49 live looks read as a wall with nothing to press.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **The write-back** (`sessionorc`): `decide` joins `ACTIONS` in `board.py` as a branch of `edit_line` and `message` making the edit `board_edit.py decide` makes — `Decided: <text> (<date>)` at the line's end, the message `agentorc: decide <head>: <answer> (session <name>)` — under `ready(root)`, exact text, refused on a line already carrying `Decided:`; `rpc_board_edit` takes `answer` and `answers` (the reader's list, handed by the page as `refs` are) and refuses an answer that is neither one of them word for word nor `Not right: …` when the pair is among them. The reply edit learns to write ahead of a `Decided:` tail. Tests: a decide writes the field and the message; a moved line, a dirty board, a decided line and an answer not on the list are refused; a reply after a decide leaves `decided` readable; a decide after a reply follows its tail.
2. **The buttons**: the board row draws the item's `answers` as buttons in order, in the order written, the `default`'s marked, and a bare **Go with it** in the foot after Reply with the steer's confirm naming the default; a press calls `board_edit {action: decide, answer}` and the row re-reads *decided: <text> · <date>* (from the reader's `decided`) in place of the buttons; rows read from origin draw them disabled; the help entries for **answers** and **Go with it** go into §4.5a's help list and `ui/help.py` together (their wording is this slice's), and `g` / `1`–`4` on the ringed row, the digits yielding to the row only when it has answer buttons; the body stops printing the `Answers:` and `Decided:` tails.
3. **A live look's pair** — once dev-cadence TD-074 has landed the words (`Blocked by` is not written here because slices 1–2 do not wait): a `watch` item whose answers are the pair draws **Works** and **Not right…**; *Works* decides at once; *Not right…* opens the Reply composer with *Not right:* begun and decides with the text, one write; a decided *Not right* hands an entry to the repo's techlead by a second call from the page, `entry_add` with type `bug` and the repo's teams, after the decide committed, its first line the item's head and the text — a refusal is toasted and the decision stands; a decided *Works* is left for the raising team's round — the manager preset's round reads `decided` on its repo's board rows and tells the member that holds the item's `Context:` ref to close the line and archive the entry (`ao msg --kind note`), once per item.
4. **This repo's live looks and the briefs**: one pass over `docs/user_attention.md` giving every *merged, live look pending* item the pair (a grinder's PR; the words TD-074 settled), and the package briefs' and this repo's supplements' live-look line written with the pair (`docs/briefs/**` is held: that PR waits for the techlead's read). **The board pass done 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2, PR #897):** the ten open `watch` lines end with the pair, no default; TD-255's own look gets it in #896. **The briefs' line written 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2):** `docs/briefs/grinder-ao-1.md`, which its sibling's brief follows, says a live look is a `watch` line ending with the pair, no default without evidence, and what each answer orders; the package's templates carry no live-look line (cadence §3.5 is the rule for every repo), so none was added there. Slice 4 is done.
5. **Tests**: a row with three answers and a default draws three answer buttons in the order written, the default marked, and Go with it in the foot; a press writes `Decided:` and the row re-reads; a `watch` with the pair draws two buttons and *Not right…* writes the typed text; a decided row from *Board, coming up* moves to *Needs you* and is counted; a decided item's body prints no `Answers:` tail; `1` on a ringed board row presses the first answer as written and `g` Go with it.

**Done when:** on a scratch home, a board item's answers are buttons on its Inbox row, one with a default is decided by one press, a live look by one of two, and the board line carries `Decided:` after each; the help entries are in §4.5a's list; this repo's live looks carry the pair.

**Related:** TD-254 (the design), dev-cadence TD-074 (the guidance and the pair), TD-244 (the groom), TD-142 (Reply), TD-218 / TD-219 (an entry handed to the techlead), TD-124 (the keys), TD-140 (Put on the board, the first add), TD-036 (`board_edit.py`).

## TD-258: Build the manager's mechanical jobs as tick policies — the cadence check, merged without its read, conventions relayed, the owed nudge, the dropped lease

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (the designer, from TD-247's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** Partly done — **slice 1 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #868):** `sessionorc/cadence.py` (the head read, the script's run, `parse`, `record`, `line`, `clause`, `read_by`, `said`), `checks` on the record (home-owned), the tick's `_cadence_pass` (detached, one PR a run, the longest unread first) and `_cadence_line`, `mail.cadence` on every `ao` reply, `ao status -v`'s `checks:` line; the entry gained `merged` and `read_by` (design §6 rule 10 says why), and the line is typed only into an unattended member's composer. Tests in `tests/test_cadence_policy.py`. **Slice 3 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #872):** `sessionorc/conventions.py` (the script's run, `parse`, `sort`, `note`), `conventions_seen` on the record (home-owned), the tick's `_conventions_pass` (detached, one run a root; written at the first reading after the create, as design §6 rule 12 now says). Tests in `tests/test_conventions_policy.py`. **Slice 2 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #874):** `sessionorc/held.py` (the glob match, `held_paths` and the files read moved from `agentorc/review.py`, which imports them; `pr_read` with `mergedAt`; `read_by`, `crossing`, `line`, `clause`, `note`), `held_missed` on the record (home-owned), the tick's `_held_pass` (detached, five PRs a pass; every `done` with a PR, declared or derived, once merged; a crossing fifteen minutes after the merge) and `_held_line`, the clause said once on the member's next `ao` reply (in the `cadence` field); design §6 rule 11 says what the build settled. Tests in `tests/test_held_missed.py`. **For slice 5, from the review of #874:** what is settled is in memory, so a crossing whose entries Dismiss cleared is written again by a home restarted inside six hours of the merge — Dismiss should mark the entries dismissed, or the settled set be kept on the record. The techlead's two notes on #874, for slice 5: the serve-side clause marks `told` for a seat too (rule 10's is `seat is None`), so the row reads `told` with that in mind; and `_held_tried` and `_held_settled` are keyed by address and never pruned for a record that closed — harmless at this scale, kept until the home restarts. **Slice 4 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #877):** `_nudge_line` names an owed outcome apart (`_owed_part`, `OWED_NAMED`), after the open reference or alone, a seat's from its outbox with no `seat_due` needed; `_lane_news` tells a dropped lease through `_lane_drops` — another record of the repo, later than the declaration and than `lane_seen.dropped[id]`, not while a live record holds the id again — in the lane note, counted with the new ids; `clear_work` keeps `dropped`. Tests in `tests/test_wanted_and_nudge.py` and `tests/test_lane_news.py`. **Slice 5's host half built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #881):** `clear_mark {id, kind: cadence|held, pr}` (`agent_wake.py`, a person's own, in `modes.HOME_EDITS`) — `cadence` takes `row` off the PR's entry and drops its snooze, `held` marks the standing entries `dismissed` and keeps them, which settles the note from the review of #874 (design §6 rule 11 and §4.5a say so); `attention_snooze` takes `cadence:<pr>`; `cadence.rows`, `held.standing`, `held.row` (`held.ROW`) are what the rows read. Tests in `tests/test_cadence_policy.py` and `tests/test_held_missed.py`. **Slice 5's page half built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, in grinder-ao-2's package while it is out of work, PR #882):** `ui/inbox.py` `cadence_marks` and `held_mark` (one row a failing PR, its kind `cadence:<pr>` the snooze's key; one row for two crossings not dismissed), each `#n` a link (`review.pr_url`), *recorded* or *read by <name>* beside *review*; the `state` macro's two branches in `inbox_row.html`; `/api/person/clear_mark` and its press and toast in `app.js`; the help paragraphs `cadence-failed` and `held-missed` in §4.5a's list and `ui/help.py`. Tests in `tests/test_ui_mark_rows.py`. A live look after a promote is Paul's (the board). **Slice 6 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #883):** `briefs/manager.md` step 2 leaves the owed reminder to the tick (a node's member excepted), step 3 reads `checks` and `held_missed` and says the tick tells conventions, the relay step is gone (the round has five steps); `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` lost the cadence-check, merged-held-PR and relay paragraphs and `relayed.json`; design §4.8's role table says the same. Test in `tests/test_cli.py`. **Every slice is built. Left:** the live look of slice 5's rows (the board), and *Done when* on a scratch home. **Left from the review of #868:** an open PR with a settled read costs one `gh pr view` each cadence while a `done` names it, and a PR closed unmerged does so for the record's life — mark a closed PR settled as a merged one is if the calls show up. **Next:** every slice is built; what is left is the live look of slice 5's rows after a promote.
**Location:** `src/sessionorc/agent_tick.py` (`_keep_running`'s per-record pass; a detached read beside `_count_seats` and `_brief_pass`; `_idle_nudge` / `_nudge_line`; `_lane_news`), `src/sessionorc/agent_mail.py` (`_system_note`), `src/sessionorc/agent_common.py` (constants), `src/sessionorc/models.py` (the home-owned fields `checks`, `held_missed`, `conventions_seen`; `Session.owed()`), `src/sessionorc/reports.py` (`derive`, `merged_prs`), `src/agentorc/review.py` → `src/sessionorc/` (`held_paths`, `pr_files`: the glob match moves down; `ao pr held` imports it from there), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (the `ao` reply clause beside `mail.context` and `mail.brief`; `ao status -v`'s check line), `src/agentorc/ui/inbox.py` and `templates/inbox_row.html` (the two rows), `src/agentorc/ui/help.py` and §4.5a's help list (bound by `tests/test_help.py`), `tests/`

**Why:** TD-247's *Why* (3): four of the manager's jobs read fields and scripts, not screens, and a standing manager woke every hour to run them. Design §6 *What is left is judgement, and a seat holds it*, rules 10, 11, 12, rule 4's owed clause and rule 6's dropped lease say what each does; this entry builds them. `src/sessionorc/**` is a held path: every slice's PR waits for the techlead's read.

**Fix, in slices a PR each:**
1. **Rule 10, the cadence check.** For a supervised non-seat record, each `progress` `done` with a `pr` not in `checks` at the PR's head: run `python3 scripts/check_cadence.py --pr <n> --json` in the record's registry root at the home, detached on the reports' cadence, one PR per run; `checks: [{pr, at, sha, verdict, failed, told, row}]`; no script, no readable root or exit 2 → no entry; `unknown` kept and re-read. On the first `fail`: the fixed line through `_policy_send` into an idle composer on the home's host, else the reply clause (`mail.cadence` beside `mail.context`), `told` set; on a second fail at a new head or after a new `done`, or a fail on a merged PR: `row` set. Beside the `review` row's verdict: *read by <seat>* when a mailbox the home holds has an `ask` with `pr` and a reply from that seat on its thread, else *recorded*. `ao status -v` prints the last check per PR. Tests: a pass writes the entry only; a fail types the line once and a second fail marks the row; a merged fail marks the row at once; a pass clears `row`; no script means no reading; a node's member gets the clause and no line.
2. **Rule 11, merged without its read.** Move `held_paths` and `pr_files` into `sessionorc` (`ao pr held` keeps its behaviour, importing from there). For a supervised record carrying `review`, each derived `done` whose PR is merged: files against `held`; a held PR with no reply from the reader on an `ask` carrying that `pr` (the seat's mailbox for `reader: techlead`, the person inbox for `reader: person`) appends `held_missed: [{pr, at, paths}]`, sends the fixed line (idle: typed; working: the clause) and one `system` note to the person; a second entry raises the row. A failed files read is no reading. Tests: a held PR the seat replied on is no crossing; one it did not is a line, a note and an entry; the second is the row; a PR touching no held path is nothing; a record without `review` is nothing.
3. **Rule 12, conventions relayed.** For each registry root with `scripts/cadence_changes.py`: run it `--json` on the reports' cadence; on each supervised, non-seat, unfinished record of that root, `conventions_seen: {at, headings}` written on the first tick after the create as the headings landed at or before `created` (no `landed`: not counted); a later heading not in it and landed after `created` → one `system` note (three headings at most, *and n more*), the headings added. Tests: a member created after an entry landed is told nothing of it; one created before is told once; a seat and a finished member are told nothing; a restart is written afresh.
4. **Rule 4's owed clause and rule 6's dropped lease.** `_idle_nudge` counts an owed outcome (`Session.owed()`) as open work and names it apart in the line, for a seat and a member alike, once per stretch, never to a finished member. `_lane_news` tells an id in `lane_seen` whose lease any record of the repo released after `out_of_work.at` (*TD-108, dropped by grinder-ao-2*), keeping the drop's instant beside the id so it is told once. Tests for both.
5. **The two Inbox rows**: **cadence check failed** (`row` on a `checks` entry; Open, Snooze, Dismiss clearing `row`) and **merged without its read** (two `held_missed` entries; Open, Dismiss clearing them), under *Needs you*, counted, each `#n` a link, fixed words as §4.5a gives them; their help paragraphs into `ui/help.py` and §4.5a's help list together (their wording is this slice's); `attention_snooze` keyed on the record and the PR for the first. Tests: each row drawn from its mark, counted, and gone when the mark is cleared.
6. **The manager's words.** `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` (the package template; not a held path) drops round steps 3 and 4 and the chase of step 2, reads `checks` and `held_missed` on the records instead, and says the tick tells conventions; this repo's supplement `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` (held) drops the cadence-check, held-PR and relay paragraphs and the `relayed.json`. Rule 9's note and §4.9a are untouched.

**Done when:** on a scratch home with a registry root carrying dev-cadence's scripts, a member's `done` with a failing PR gets one fixed line and, on the second fail, the Inbox row; a held PR merged with no reply from the seat writes `held_missed`, one line and one FYI; a member started before a new `docs/cadence-changes.md` entry landed gets one `system` note naming it and a member started after gets none; an idle member owing an outcome is nudged about it once; a dropped lease is told once; the manager template names none of the four jobs.

**Related:** TD-247 (the design), TD-103 (the first move of policy to the tick), TD-093 (the reader, `review`), TD-079 (outcomes), TD-187 / TD-195 (rule 6), TD-118 (the round's cost), TD-050 (the cadence check's `review` row reads the first line), TD-259 (the seat).

## TD-259: Build the manager on call — `on_call`, the `team` trigger, `idle_open`, the sweep's seat exception, the card and the row, the brief, the first look on dc-grind

**Priority:** Medium
**Type:** feature
**Added:** 2026-09-30 (the designer, from TD-247's design)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Status:** In progress — slice 1 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2): the key, the launch's `seat: {trigger: team}`, `ao team list`. **The default is `true`** since the flip (below). **Slice 5's package half built 2026-10-01**: `src/agentorc/briefs/manager_on_call.md`, composed for a manager on call (`repoconfig.ON_CALL_BRIEFS`); its held half — `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` for a seat, the primer's decided row — goes with the flip. **Slice 4 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-2, PR #875), ahead of slice 2:** the *idle · open work* Inbox row (`inbox.idle_open_mark`, Open and Snooze, the snooze kind `idle_open` on `rpc_attention_snooze`), the card's slot reading `idle_open` — **with its `nudged_at` derivation kept as a fallback, which slice 2 or whoever follows it removes from `cards.py` once the tick writes the field** — the manager on call among `teamrun`'s seats (`org.MANAGER_WHEN`, *on call — comes when a member needs a reading*), and the help paragraph *idle · open work*. Tests in `tests/test_ui_inbox.py`, `tests/test_ui_org.py`, `tests/test_cli_teams.py`, `tests/test_attention.py`. No live look yet: no team is on call until the flip, so the look is slice 6's. **Slice 2 built 2026-10-01 (grinder-ao-1, PR #878), without the flip:** `_idle_open` writes `idle_open: {at, ref}` (a member's alone; `ref` none for an owed outcome alone); `_team_causes` and `_seat_due` set `seat_due: {at, by, member}` for `trigger: team` — `ask`, the question's id, in `member`'s place for `asks`; `seat_filled` is written on the record that took the seat (`_fill`) and loses an entry when its cause goes; an idle seat with a member's reading due is closed and filled for it (`_seat_done`; design §6 rule 3 says so); `Session.asks_ids`.
